## 1. Purpose and Usage

This document describes the design intent, architecture, and business logic of Manzil: a collaborative, agent-powered apartment-hunting dashboard for a small group of users. It is written for two audiences with equal weight:

**For the human (Yusuf):** the reference for every architectural decision, its rationale, and the trade-offs accepted. When implementation reality contradicts this document, update the document — that is what "living" means.

**For coding agents:** this document is authoritative context. When implementing any feature, read the relevant section *and* the [Glossary](#3-glossary-and-domain-model) first; domain terms (Hunt, Property, Listing, Criterion, Checkpoint) have precise meanings and the code must use them consistently. Do not invent alternative names for concepts defined here. Do not implement anything listed in [Deferred Features](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog) unless explicitly asked. When a design detail is ambiguous or missing, stop and ask rather than guessing — then the answer gets recorded here.

**Reading paths** (read §3 first in every case): implementing the schema → §8, §9; the scoring engine → §3, §9.3, §9.4; a pipeline stage → §10.1, that stage's subsection, §11.3; the frontend → §4.2, §13, §8.2 (the tables you subscribe to); anything cost-sensitive → §15 before writing a prompt.

**Update protocol:** material design changes append an entry to the [Decision Log](#20-decision-log) (date, decision, rationale, sections touched) and update the affected sections in place. Never let the log and the body disagree; the body wins for "what is," the log wins for "why it changed." Implementation mechanics — interfaces, tunables, work plans, runbooks — live in the sibling `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, which churns freely without this ceremony; the authority order is DESIGN (intent) > IMPLEMENTATION (mechanics) > code (exact interfaces).

`docs/scoped-criteria-and-amenities.md` preserves the approved decision workbook and rationale for the scoped-Criteria program. It is supplementary: this document owns design intent and `IMPLEMENTATION.md` owns the selected mechanics and execution status when either differs from the workbook.

---

## 2. Product Overview

### 2.1 Problem

Rental listing sites are hostile to comparison: fees hide behind "contact us," identical units appear at different prices across sites, and the true all-in monthly cost is never the headline number. The current workflow — a hand-maintained spreadsheet, one row per listing — is tedious to bootstrap, tedious to maintain, and silently goes stale.

### 2.2 Solution

Paste a listing URL. An agent pipeline validates it, deduplicates it against known properties, finds sibling sources, extracts structured details, verifies them against the page evidence, reconciles conflicts across sources, assesses photos, enriches with location and utility-cost data, and scores the result against a rubric the hunt owner fully controls. Everything lands in a live, shared table with provenance on every value.

### 2.3 Goals

1. One-click ingestion: URL in, fully populated and scored row out, within minutes.
2. Deterministic, transparent, user-controlled scoring — the LLM produces facts; only the rubric produces points.
3. Small-group collaboration: shared hunts, roles, comments, ratings, realtime sync.
4. Data honesty: provenance and confidence on every value; estimates visually distinct from facts; overrides preserve originals; the tool never makes an apartment look cheaper than a worst realistic month.

### 2.4 Non-Goals

- Not a search engine — it evaluates URLs users bring; no automated listing discovery or alerts (backlog, [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)).
- No native mobile app; responsive web only.
- No multi-tenant SaaS hardening or billing. Personal tool for a small circle.
- **Not publicly *usable*, but publicly *viewable*** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.53). The app is not a service anyone can sign up for and put their own hunt in — that remains out of scope, and public self-registration stays disabled (FR13). What is in scope is a **Demo Mode**: one shared, database-enforced read-only Demo Account showing one Demo Hunt, so the tool can be shown to someone without giving them an account. A Demo Account writes nothing, fetches nothing, and spends nothing; the distinction that keeps this a non-goal rather than a reversal is that no visitor can ever bring their *own* data. The scraping posture this non-goal used to carry is now stated directly in [§17](#17-risks-and-concerns-register) R2 rather than inferred from privacy.

### 2.5 Learning Objectives

Manzil is deliberately dual-purpose: a real tool with real users on a real deadline (the apartment hunt), and a vehicle for learning multi-agent architectures by building them against a genuine problem rather than a toy. For a learning project, overkill is not a bug — it is the assignment; the "don't build multi-agent unless justified" rule is shipping advice, deliberately suspended *off* the critical path. The reconciliation is one governing principle, applied everywhere it matters:

> **The critical path stays boring; the ambition lives where failure is survivable.** A listing that scores late is fine; a listing that scores *wrong silently* is not.

Concretely: the shipping pipeline is the workflow-mode baseline ([§10.2](#102-implementation-patterns)); the multi-agent architectures live in an isolated, flag-gated agents mode ([§10.11](#1011-agents-mode-learning-track)); and every learning claim is settled empirically by the dual-mode eval harness, not by faith in either direction. The deliverable of the learning track is *earned judgment* — knowing what multi-agent buys and costs on a real task, with data.

## 3. Glossary and Domain Model

Precise terms. Code, schema, API routes, and UI copy must use these consistently.

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Hunt** | A named apartment search (e.g., "Apartment Search 2026") owning a rubric, members, and a set of listings. The top-level collaboration container. |
| **Invitation Link** | A reusable, Owner-managed URL that grants the Member role in one Hunt. It may expire or have a successful-join limit; its join history is retained when the link is soft-deleted. Distinct from a single-recipient email invite. |
| **Property** | A canonical, globally shared apartment complex/building — deduplicated by address + name. Extracted facts attach to properties, not hunts. |
| **Listing** (`hunt_listing`) | The association of a Property with a Hunt. Scores, overrides, comments, ratings, pins, and the fees checklist live here. Comments may be Listing-wide or scoped to one Unit Group; ratings are always scoped to one Unit Group. Permanently deleting a Listing removes that Hunt-local association and its opinion/history, including Visits of that Property in that Hunt and Hunt-scoped custom-Criterion Extractions, while preserving the globally shared Property and its Sources, Floor Plans, images, and Catalog Extractions. |
| **Source** (`property_source`) | One known listing page (URL) for a Property on a specific site. Official complex sites are flagged `is_official`. |
| **Floor Plan** | One Source-specific marketed unit layout at a Property: plan name, beds, baths, sqft range, rent range, availability, and other facts explicitly tied to that layout. A Floor Plan is not an individual physical unit. |
| **Unit Group** | The derived presentation grouping of a Property's Floor Plans by `(beds, baths)`. It is not stored and does not imply that its Floor Plans share amenities, Unit type, price, or availability. Overview renders one row per Unit Group: group-level ranges/counts summarize its Floor Plans, while plan-specific values come from the group's **Display Floor Plan**. |
| **Display Floor Plan** | The one Floor Plan representing a Unit Group for plan-specific values: the Hunt's valid manual per-Unit-Group pin when one exists; otherwise, on filtered Overview/Map surfaces, the current Filter-selected Floor Plan when one is required; otherwise the highest-scoring Floor Plan with a known base rent (falling back to the group's highest-scoring Floor Plan only when none has one). Compare has no filter-selected state. The UI identifies which rule selected the plan and never promotes its facts into Unit Group-wide truth. |
| **Filter-selected Floor Plan** | Ephemeral Overview/Map view state for an unpinned Unit Group: the highest-scoring Floor Plan that satisfies every active Floor-Plan predicate on that same plan. It is visibly labeled, never written to `hunt_listings.pins`, and is recomputed or cleared as filters change. |
| **Interest Status** | The current manual disposition of one Listing's Unit Group within a Hunt. Nullable (`undecided`) or one of: `interested`, `not_interested`, `applied`, `application_rejected`, `application_withdrawn`, `offer_received`, `offer_accepted`, `offer_declined`, `offer_rescinded`, `unavailable`. Hunt opinion, never a global Property fact. |
| **Visit** | One tour of a Property by one or more Hunt members, spanning every unit walked on that occasion. Hunt opinion, never a global Property fact. Its state is **derived from timestamps** — planned, in progress, completed, or cancelled ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)) — and it is never an Extraction source: nothing a Visit records enters the fact store or the scoring engine. |
| **Visit Unit** | One door walked through on a Visit, identified by a human-typed **label** (`4B`, "the corner one") plus either a Floor Plan pointer or typed `(beds, baths)`. It is a label on a tour, not inventory: the app still cannot enumerate a Property's units and never learns them from a Source, so this does **not** lift §18's individual-unit-inventory deferral ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)). |
| **Visit Entry** | One answer on a Visit, of exactly one kind — **Fact** (objective, shared, one value), **Check** (a physical test somebody performed: untested / fine / problem), **Question** (for the agent; cannot be marked asked without a typed answer), or **Impression** (subjective, keyed per member). Sharing is a property of the *item*, not of the checklist. Append-only; newest row per identity wins. |
| **Visit Item Scope** | Whether a checklist item is answered once per Visit (`property`) or once per Visit Unit (`unit`) — the checklist's counterpart to **Fact Scope**. Ambiguous items default to `property`, because a property answer repeated per unit is harmless while a unit answer shown property-wide is a false statement about units nobody looked at. |
| **Visit Defect** | A problem recorded on a Visit against a Visit Unit or the building, with severity 1–5. A Check marked *problem* promotes into one automatically, carrying its unit and originating item. |
| **Fee Proposal** | A figure confirmed on a Visit, offered to its Listing for acceptance. Accepting writes through the ordinary human path (`fee_checklist` upsert or an `overrides` insert); rejecting records the decision and leaves the Visit's own figure untouched. A Visit never mutates Listing cost data on its own ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)). |
| **Criterion** | One scoreable dimension (e.g., in-unit laundry). Predefined criteria live in the `criteria_catalog`; hunts may add custom criteria. Every Catalog Criterion declares a **Fact Scope**, acquisition-escalation policy, and conflict policy independently from its Rubric behavior. |
| **Fact Scope** | What a Criterion describes: `property`, `floor_plan`, `mixed`, or `composed`. One stored Extraction target is concrete (`property` or `floor_plan`); `mixed` means the Criterion may produce either kind, while `composed` is deterministically derived rather than page-extracted. |
| **Unit Applicability** | How far a Source's unit-feature claim reaches: `specific_floor_plans`, `all_units`, `select_units`, or `unit_scope_unspecified`. An unqualified unit amenity list is unspecified, never universal. Applicability is evidence scope, not confidence or the feature's value. |
| **Effective Presence** | The value SCORE may use for one Floor Plan on a presence-like unit Criterion: `confirmed`, `advertised_unconfirmed`, `none`, or `unknown`. Select/unspecified evidence becomes `advertised_unconfirmed` without an exact claim and cannot satisfy a confirmed-feature non-negotiable. |
| **Rubric** | A hunt's enabled criteria with their options, point deltas, unknown-deltas, and gate flags. Versioned per hunt. |
| **Gate** | A dealbreaker (option-level: if matched, score is SET) or non-negotiable (criterion-level: if no acceptable option matched, score is SET). An option is *acceptable* when its `delta ≥ 0` and it is not itself a dealbreaker option; an unknown value never satisfies a non-negotiable — missing data cannot pass a gate. |
| **Extraction** | One append-only candidate or reconciled fact: `(Property, nullable Hunt/custom scope, Criterion, origin, concrete target)` → value + Unit Applicability + confidence + evidence + model + timestamp. Source candidates and reconciled resolutions occupy the same fact store but have distinct record kinds; current lookups are centralized views/repository seams, never ad-hoc “latest Criterion” queries. |
| **Override** | An append-only per-Listing human value targeting either one Floor Plan or all units; it displays instead of the reconciled Extraction without destroying provenance. A null row reverts that same target to extracted truth. |
| **Job** | One pipeline run (ingest / refresh / rescore) for a listing. The `jobs` table is simultaneously the queue, the state store, and the task-history record. |
| **Stage** | One step of the pipeline state machine (PLAN, VALIDATE, …). Stages are idempotent and resumable. |
| **Checkpoint** | A pipeline pause (`WAITING_USER`) presenting a structured question to the submitter, a Curator, or the Owner. Typed by `kind`: `confirm_value`, `resolve_dedupe`, `resolve_dispute`. Auto-resumes with a declared default after 24h. There is no separate "needs review" state — human review *is* a checkpoint. |
| **Adapter Registry** | Per-domain record of which fetch tier a site requires and its settings. |
| **Source Policy** | A per-submission constraint on cross-checking (defaulted from hunt settings): whether DISCOVER runs at all and how high the fetch ladder may climb for *discovered sibling sources*. One of `trust_link`, `tier_1`, `tiers_1_2`, `tiers_1_2_3` (default), `tier_1_plus_official`. Never constrains the submitted URL itself ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)). |
| **Slate** | The set of sources extracted for a run: the submitted URL (a permanent member — always fetched and extracted, never substituted out) plus one discovered sibling per permitted census fetch tier, each from a distinct Syndication Family. The official site is not a slate member by default ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)). |
| **Syndication Family** | A census grouping of aggregator domains that share an upstream listing feed (e.g., Zillow/Trulia/HotPads). RECONCILE counts at most one vote per family, and a Slate never carries two same-family sources. |
| **Escalation** | RECONCILE's bounded spend ladder for unresolved decision-relevant fields: official-site extraction as arbiter, then one sibling round drawn from DISCOVER's candidate pool ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)). |
| **Single-sourced** | A Listing whose Property has exactly one extracted source. `single_source_reason` distinguishes an explicit `trust_link` choice, a completed search with no siblings (`discover_exhausted`), and a bounded discovery loop that could not produce a usable result (`discover_failed`). The quiet badge is noteworthy, not an error, while preserving the difference between evidence of absence and unavailable discovery. |
| **Problematic** | Derived Listing state: at least one field resolved by conservative fallback (stored `disputed`, [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)). Rendered as a badge; clears when the dispute resolves. |
| **Baseline** | A cached regional median/peak utility cost used when a utility is not included in rent. Keyed by `(geo_level, state, region_name)` — city+state, county+state, or state-only ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)). |
| **Site Admin** | An **account-level** operator, orthogonal to the three Hunt roles: a Site Admin has no membership anywhere and gains no Hunt role by being one. Held in `site_admins`, whose first row is the **system owner** and is immutable — no path revokes it, which is what makes lockout impossible. Everything a Site Admin does is attributed to their real account and written to the Admin Audit Log ([§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions)). |
| **Ghost View** | A Site Admin reading a Hunt they are *not* a member of: the Owner's screens and controls, but absent from the member list and from Presence, and writes labelled `Admin [Name]`. Conditional by definition — an admin who **is** a member of that Hunt is an ordinary member there, with no ghost treatment at all. |
| **Demo Account** | The single **virtual principal** every visitor to the public demo runs as: a UUID held in `demo_accounts` with **no `auth.users` row behind it** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.55). It is a **Curator of the Demo Hunt and nothing else**, and that role is *synthesised* by `private.member_role()` rather than stored, because a subject with no account cannot hold a `hunt_members` row. It is read-only at the database layer: a statement-level trigger on every `public` table refuses any write carrying its subject, and restrictive `SELECT` policies confine its reads to the Demo Hunt. Having no account is the point rather than an accident — there is no password to change, no email to rebind, no MFA to enrol and no session to invalidate, so the shared identity cannot be taken over or used to sign other visitors out. A consequence a contributor must expect: the demo visitor is **absent from the member list and from Presence**, which is correct — they are not a person in the Hunt. |
| **Demo Hunt** | The one Hunt named by `site_settings.demo_hunt_id`, selected and published by a Site Admin from the Hunts that Site Admin personally owns. It is an ordinary Hunt in every respect: its Owner builds the Rubric, submits live URLs, curates the public state, and pays for normal ingestion. Active Listings are the live Overview a Demo Account reads; archived Listings are the input slate for Replay Captures. Selection and derived assets survive disabling Demo Mode. A Site Admin grant is necessary but not sufficient to publish somebody else's Hunt: ownership is the deliberate-consent boundary. |
| **Demo Release** | One atomically promoted, immutable set of the Demo Hunt's **derived public assets**: sanitized Replay Captures plus light/dark Google Static Maps basemaps and their exact-path manifest. It does not copy the live Hunt. A release is built durably by the worker, versioned in private tables/Storage, and becomes visible only after the Hunt, ownership, generation, and input fingerprint are re-checked. Failed or stale builds leave the previous release intact. Publishing is explicit: ordinary Hunt edits mark a release stale in the Admin UI but never trigger billable map capture or change visitor replay content on their own. |
| **Replay Capture** | A recorded ingest — the `job_events` timeline plus the finished read payload — derived at Demo publication time from the latest completed real ingest of one archived Demo-Hunt Listing and replayed **in the browser** when a Demo Account submits a URL. A Demo Release carries one ordered capture per eligible archived Listing, with a visible count of the runs remaining and both submit affordances disabled once the slate is spent until the visitor reloads. The submitted URL is never fetched, and the substitution is disclosed before the user confirms. The protected capture is fetched lazily from the current release; it is not a world-readable frontend build artifact. A Replay Capture is a recording, never a simulation: every value in it was really extracted from a real page. Its **clock** is staged ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.57) — playback is compressed to roughly half a minute while recorded durations remain in the payload — and checkpoint rendering remains deliberately cut ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.58). |
| **Tombstone** | A record that something was deleted, carrying no foreign key to its subject — an FK would cascade the tombstone away with the very row it commemorates. `via_cascade` distinguishes a thing deleted directly from one that died with its parent. |

Ownership chain: `Hunt → hunt_listings → Property → (sources, floor_plans, extractions, images)`. Global facts flow left-to-right; per-hunt opinion (scores, overrides, comments) never leaves the Listing.

---

## 4. Requirements

### 4.1 Functional

- **FR1 — Hunts:** create/rename/archive; a Property may appear in multiple Hunts; extracted data is shared globally, everything opinionated is per-hunt.
- **FR2 — Rubric:** guided builder, skippable but required before first submission. Owner-edited, member-viewable (read-only rubric page). Toggle catalog criteria, edit deltas, per-criterion unknown-delta, custom criteria, dealbreakers, non-negotiables. Edits bump `rubric_version` and trigger a free re-score of all listings.
- **FR3 — Ingestion:** submit URL → pipeline per [§10](#10-agent-pipeline). Each submission carries a **Source Policy** ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)) — defaulting to full cross-check — controlling whether sibling sources are discovered and how high the fetch ladder may climb for them. Progress visible live; jobs cancellable by their submitter or the Owner.
- **FR4 — Utilities intelligence:** determine included utilities; estimate excluded ones from winter-weighted regional baselines; compose all-in monthly cost with actual/estimated/unknown tagging ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)).
- **FR5 — Table and detail:** sortable/filterable table, one row per Unit Group, detail panel (right `Drawer` on desktop and mobile) with full breakdown, evidence, images, floor plans, sources, comments, ratings.
- **FR6 — Collaboration:** single-recipient email invites plus reusable Invitation Links with Owner-managed expiration, usage limits, soft deletion, and join attribution; per-user colors, realtime sync, comments, ratings.
- **FR7 — Overrides:** any extracted value overrideable per role matrix below; original value + provenance preserved and viewable; override badge in UI.
- **FR8 — Refresh:** per-listing and hunt-wide refresh re-running only stale data classes ([§14](#14-caching-and-refresh-strategy)); field-scoped partial refresh.
- **FR9 — Compare:** 2–4 listings side-by-side at criterion granularity.
- **FR10 — Task history:** the Tasks view shows Active and History tabs. Every past job is retained and inspectable: plan manifest, per-stage timeline with outcomes, checkpoint Q&A (including auto-resolutions), errors, and actual LLM cost per run.
- **FR11 — Dual-mode pipeline + eval harness:** the pipeline runs as `--mode=workflow` (baseline, ships) or `--mode=agents` ([§10.11](#1011-agents-mode-learning-track)); both share the RunState/persistence contract and the deterministic truth layer. An eval harness runs both modes against the bench set and reports accuracy (verification pass-rate, gate-criterion correctness), tokens, and latency.
- **FR12 — Property investigation (agents mode, off critical path):** an on-demand "Investigate this property" action producing a structured brief — reviews and management reputation, scam signals and cross-listing price checks, area context — via a spawnable worker crew. Slow, costly, or occasionally dumb is acceptable here by design; nothing downstream depends on it.
- **FR13 — Account identity:** only a Site Admin may create an account, through the audited admin path; public self-registration is disabled. Password sign-in and recovery-email enrollment are supported. Magic link and six-digit OTP may authenticate an **existing** account only and must never create one. Every authenticated user completes default-display-name onboarding before entering a Hunt.

### 4.2 Roles and Permissions

Three roles per hunt. The middle role is named **Curator** — chosen over "Editor" precisely because of the ambiguity Yusuf flagged: *every* member can submit and edit their own contributions; what the middle role adds is stewardship over the *data quality* of the whole hunt. Curator says exactly that. (Rejected: Editor — implies members can't edit anything; Moderator — implies people-management, which stays with the Owner.)

Enforced via Supabase RLS at the database layer, mirrored by API checks. Never frontend-only.

| Action | Owner | Curator | Member | Site Admin (non-member) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Edit rubric | yes | — | — | yes |
| Edit hunt settings ([§8.2](#82-tables)) | yes | — | — | yes |
| View rubric | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Add listing / submit URL | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Manage (cancel/retry/reorder) **own** jobs | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Manage **anyone's** jobs and Listing data | yes | — | — | yes |
| Resolve checkpoints | any listing | any listing | own listings | yes |
| Overrides + utility/fee entries | any listing | any listing | own listings | yes |
| Interest Status + visited | any Unit Group | any Unit Group | read-only | yes |
| Create a Visit; add/edit its Visit Units | yes | yes | yes | **read-only** |
| Answer Visit Entries, add custom items, log Defects | yes | yes | yes | **read-only** |
| Start / end / **reopen** a Visit | yes | yes | yes | **read-only** |
| Cancel or delete a Visit | any Visit | own Visits | own Visits | **read-only** |
| Accept / reject a Fee Proposal | any listing | any listing | own listings | yes |
| View task history | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| Comments, ratings, own color | yes | yes | yes | — |
| Archive / restore Listings | yes | — | — | yes |
| Permanently delete an archived Listing | yes | — | — | yes |
| Email invites and Invitation Links, remove members, change roles, archive Hunt | yes | — | — | yes |
| Provision a new account | — | — | — | yes |
| Select, publish, enable, or disable the Demo Hunt | — | — | — | yes, but selection/publication require owning that Hunt |

**The Demo Account is not a fourth role** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.53). It reads the **Curator** column, because that is genuinely its Hunt role — and is then separately denied the write half of every row by the database, in the Demo Hunt and nowhere else. Two consequences a contributor must not confuse. Its *permissions* are Curator's, so permission-derived UI (P2-12) needs no demo branch and must not grow one: what a Demo Account may see is exactly what a Curator may see. Its *capabilities* are empty, and that is enforced in Postgres, never by the frontend — which means the honest UI treatment is a control that works and then plainly says nothing was saved, not a disabled control pretending the role lacks the permission. Writing demo into the role enum was rejected for exactly this reason: it would have made every existing `member_role(...) is not null` write policy silently authoritative over a security boundary.

**The Site Admin column is the ghost view only** — a Site Admin who is a genuine member of a Hunt reads the Owner/Curator/Member column for their actual role and nothing here applies. Three rules shape the column. **Visits are read-only**: an Entry feeds the per-member roll-up in [§9.7](#97-the-visit-checklist), and a ghost answering a tour they did not attend makes those numbers lie. **Nothing personal is written**: no comments, ratings, colour or Presence, because a ghost is not a participant. **Everything else is the Owner's set, attributed and audited** — support means fixing a bad Override and re-sending a broken invite, and an admin action that is not attributable is worse than one that is not possible.


### 4.3 Non-Functional

- **NFR1 — Cost:** <$0.15 LLM+API per new listing, <$0.01 per refresh, $0 per re-score ([§15](#15-cost-model-and-optimization)).
- **NFR2 — Latency:** ingestion async; target <3 min single-source, <5 min baseline-slate multi-source submit-to-score (escalated runs exempt, but the round is visible in the manifest); UI via realtime events, no polling.
- **NFR3 — Resumability:** every stage idempotent; failed stages retry with backoff; completed stages never re-run on resume; deploys/restarts must not lose jobs (queue is durable in Postgres).
- **NFR4 — Provenance:** every stored fact carries source, timestamp, model, confidence, and (post-reconciliation) the resolution rule that selected it.
- **NFR5 — Simplicity:** boring infrastructure *on the critical path*; the Postgres instance is the database, the queue, the realtime bus, and the auth store. New moving parts require a decision-log entry justifying them (the learning track's two — LangGraph, Langfuse — are justified in [§10.11](#1011-agents-mode-learning-track) and gated by [§19](#19-implementation-phases)).
- **NFR6 — Observability:** every LLM call in **both modes** is traced (Langfuse) with stage, mode, model, tokens, and cost, wired before the first agent call and non-negotiable thereafter. Multi-agent debugging without traces is the canonical failure mode — and the debugging itself is curriculum.

---

## 5. System Architecture

```
┌──────────────┐      ┌───────────────────┐      ┌─────────────────────┐
│  Frontend     │─────▶│  API (FastAPI)     │─────▶│  Supabase            │
│  React+Mantine│◀─────│  Render service    │      │  Postgres · Auth ·   │
│  CF Pages     │      └──────┬────────────┘      │  Storage · Realtime  │
│      ▲        │             │ INSERT job         └──────────┬──────────┘
│      └────────┼── realtime subscriptions ◀─────────────────┘
└──────────────┘             ▼
                      ┌───────────────────┐
                      │  Worker (Render)   │  polls jobs (FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)
                      │  pipeline runner   │──▶ LLM APIs (extract/verify/vision)
                      │  + Playwright      │──▶ fetch tiers (§10.6)
                      └───────────────────┘──▶ Google Maps Platform
```

**Data flow, narrated once:** the frontend authenticates against Supabase Auth and calls the API with the JWT. Mutations (submit URL, edit rubric, override) go through the API, which validates, writes, and — for pipeline work — inserts a `jobs` row. The worker claims jobs from Postgres, executes the stage machine, and writes results (extractions, scores, job_events) back to Postgres. The frontend hears every change through Realtime subscriptions on the tables it renders — the worker never talks to the frontend directly and the API never blocks on pipeline work.

**Role of FastAPI background tasks:** used for *lightweight, post-response* work only — firing a re-score after a rubric save and cleanup chores. Product email is not a `BackgroundTasks` fire-and-forget: database triggers and the worker write a durable private notification outbox, and one dispatcher in the API lifespan claims deliveries, sends them through Resend HTTPS with a stable idempotency key, and records provider webhooks ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.75). Pipeline runs are explicitly **not** background tasks: they are minutes long, memory-heavy (Playwright), must survive deploys, and need retry semantics — all properties `BackgroundTasks` lacks and the durable Postgres queue provides. The API's job in ingestion is one `INSERT`; the worker does the rest.

### 5.1 API Surface

REST, resource-oriented, versioned under `/v1`. The contract is intentionally boring so it need not be specified route-by-route — a coding agent should derive it from these conventions plus the [schema](#8-database-design), and any endpoint that can't be derived this way earns a line here first:

| Resource | Routes | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Hunts | CRUD `/hunts`, `/hunts/{id}` | archive = PATCH status; `settings` edits (Owner-only) validated against the [§8.2](#82-tables) settings contract, with the per-key edit effects specified there (rescore / location refresh / nothing) |
| Members / invites | `/hunts/{id}/members`, `/hunts/{id}/invites`, `POST /invites/{id}/resend`, `POST /invites/{token}/accept` | role changes and invite management Owner-only; resend reuses an active token and queues a new product-mail delivery |
| Rubric | `GET/PUT /hunts/{id}/rubric` | PUT validates against `value_schema`, bumps version, enqueues rescore |
| Listings | `POST /hunts/{id}/listings` (body: URL + optional `source_policy`, defaulted from hunt settings) → creates Listing + ingest Job; `GET`; `PATCH /listings/{id}/status`; `PATCH /listings/{id}/source-policy`; `GET /listings/{id}/deletion-impact`; `DELETE /listings/{id}` | Status PATCH is reversible Owner-only archive/restore. Permanent DELETE is Owner-only, archived-only, exact-Property-name confirmed, blocked while a Job is active, and returns the pre-delete impact receipt. Source Policy per [§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem); policy edits are Owner-or-submitter only and atomically enqueue DISCOVER refresh when they widen discovery. Ghost View mirrors impact/DELETE under `/admin/ghost/` with audit. |
| Overrides / utilities / fees / comments / ratings / Unit Group state | nested under `/listings/{id}/…`; `PATCH /listings/{id}/unit-groups/{key}/state` | permissions per [§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions) |
| Jobs | `GET /hunts/{id}/jobs?state=…`, `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel|retry`, `POST /jobs/{id}/checkpoint` (answer) | reads also served by Realtime |
| Refresh | `POST /listings/{id}/refresh`, `POST /hunts/{id}/refresh` (optional `fields`) | inserts refresh jobs |
| Profile | `GET/PUT /profile` | self-only default display name + optional default color; onboarding uses PUT |
| Notifications | `GET/PUT /notification-preferences`, `GET/PUT /hunts/{id}/notification-preferences`, `GET /hunts/{id}/attention`; provider callback `POST /webhooks/resend` | exact per-event email defaults plus nullable per-Hunt overrides; attention is member-readable and role-aware; webhook is public only in transport and accepts nothing without a valid Svix signature |

Reads that the table renders continuously (listings, scores) go straight from the frontend to Supabase (RLS-guarded selects + Realtime); the API exists for validated mutations and anything requiring the service role.

**Scheduling.** Three duties recur on timers: TTL-driven refresh scans ([§14](#14-caching-and-refresh-strategy)), the 24 h checkpoint-timeout sweep ([§10.10](#1010-user-checkpoints)), and the 120 d utility-baseline job ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)). All three are owned by a **scheduler tick inside the worker's main loop** (every ~5 min: run three SQL queries, insert due jobs / flip timed-out checkpoints to their defaults). No cron service, no scheduler infrastructure — the duties are just rows becoming due, which Postgres answers directly (NFR5). If the worker is down, ticks are missed and caught up on restart; nothing here is time-critical at minute granularity.

**Worker deployment posture:** the worker is a separate logical package and the
pipeline always runs through the durable Postgres Job queue, but the default
deployment is an asyncio worker loop inside the API process via FastAPI lifespan.
That loop already runs the Tier-2 Playwright fetcher and is appropriate for this
two-person Hunt: one process and one paid service until observed load justifies
isolation. A separate worker process/service is an optional operational
migration, not a Phase 3 prerequisite. Make that migration if browser Jobs
repeatedly cause API memory pressure, restarts/502s, materially degraded API
latency, or if Job concurrency/always-on scheduling becomes a real requirement.
NFR3 makes either deployment safe: interrupted Jobs resume from the durable
Stage boundary. Migration and rollback mechanics live in
[IMPLEMENTATION §8](IMPLEMENTATION.md#8-runbooks).

---

## 6. Monorepo Layout

```
manzil/
├── DESIGN.md                    # this document — intent, contracts, decisions
├── IMPLEMENTATION.md            # current mechanics — interfaces, tunables, work plans, runbooks
├── CLAUDE.md                    # agent instructions (AGENTS.md symlinks to it)
├── pyproject.toml               # uv workspace root: members = shared, api, worker
├── uv.lock                      # single lockfile, root only
├── .python-version              # 3.12
│
├── shared/                      # domain-blind, LLM-free (CLAUDE.md hard rules)
│   ├── pyproject.toml           # manzil-shared
│   ├── src/manzil_shared/
│   │   ├── models.py            # Pydantic domain models — §3 terms, verbatim
│   │   ├── catalog.py           # criteria catalog seed (generates supabase/seed.sql)
│   │   └── scoring/
│   │       ├── engine.py        # pure score() → breakdown (§9.3 contract)
│   │       └── composition.py   # cost composer behind an interface (§18 buy-domain seam)
│   └── tests/golden/            # exact-breakdown assertions
│
├── worker/
│   ├── pyproject.toml           # depends on manzil-shared { workspace = true }
│   ├── src/manzil_worker/
│   │   ├── cli.py               # Phase 0 entry: `manzil ingest <url>`
│   │   ├── runner.py            # run_job — persist-before-advance (§10.2)
│   │   ├── state.py             # RunState
│   │   ├── stages/              # one module per §10.3 stage
│   │   ├── fetching/            # tiers, outcome classifier, cleaner, adapter registry (§10.7)
│   │   ├── agents/              # agents mode ONLY (§10.11): LangGraph graph, orchestrator,
│   │   │                        #   critic, triage router, investigator crew
│   │   ├── llm/                 # client.py = the seam (§11.1); config.py = model pins
│   │   └── schemas.py           # dynamic extraction model from catalog (§10.2 P1)
│   ├── tests/fixtures/
│   │   ├── corpus/              # 50+ saved real listing pages (local eval asset — gitignored, §20 v2.8)
│   │   ├── bench/               # stratified ~20: labels/ local (gitignored) + manifest.md tracked
│   │   └── recorded/            # captured LLM responses — smoke fixture tracked for CI replay; the rest local
│   └── evals/                   # dual-mode harness + written reports (FR11, §19 Learning Track)
│
├── api/                         # near-empty until Phase 1 (health route only)
│   └── src/manzil_api/
├── frontend/                    # Vite scaffold; untouched until Phase 1
├── supabase/
│   ├── migrations/              # 0001: global tables only (Phase 0 scope)
│   └── seed.sql                 # generated FROM shared/catalog.py — never edited by hand
├── infra/                       # environment template + deployment inputs
└── docs/adr/                    # rationale too detailed for §20
```

**Testing strategy:** the scoring engine gets exhaustive golden tests in `shared/` (rubric + values in, exact breakdown out — this is the component where a silent regression does the most damage); pipeline stages get unit tests against recorded fixtures (saved cleaned text + canned LLM responses; no live LLM calls in CI); tier-detection heuristics get committed synthetic signal pages plus a local (gitignored, [§20](#20-decision-log) v2.8) corpus of real pages collected during Phase 0 — the corpus sweep skips cleanly where the corpus is absent; RLS policies get integration tests that attempt every forbidden action per role (Phase 2 exit criterion); LLM prompt changes are validated by re-running the Phase 0 bench set, not by eyeballing.

Conventions: the **scoring engine and Pydantic domain models live in `shared/`** and are imported by both services — a scoring result must be identical whether computed during a pipeline run or a rubric-edit re-score. Python tooling: `uv` **workspace** rooted at `/`: a single shared `uv.lock` and one venv; `shared/`, `api/`, `worker/` are workspace members, with `api` and `worker` depending on `shared` via `[tool.uv.sources] shared = { workspace = true }`. Run `uv sync` at root; target a member with `uv run --package <name>`. `ruff` for lint/format, `pytest`. Frontend: `pnpm`, `eslint`, `vitest`.

---

## 7. Technology Stack

| Layer | Choice | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Frontend framework | React 18 + Vite + TypeScript | SPA is sufficient (no SEO need); Vite for iteration speed |
| UI library | **Mantine** (+ `@mantine/hooks`, `mantine-react-table` or Mantine + TanStack Table) | Yusuf's choice. Fits unusually well: Mantine ships the exact primitives this app needs — DataTable-grade tables, `Drawer` (detail panel), `Modal`, `Stepper` (rubric wizard), `Timeline` (job history), `Badge`/`Indicator` (override, auto-resolved, multi-score, stale markers), `Spotlight`, dark mode — without Tailwind's design-from-scratch tax |
| Client data | TanStack Query for server state + Supabase JS for auth/realtime | Query cache invalidated by realtime events — one consistency mechanism |
| API | FastAPI + Pydantic v2; `BackgroundTasks` for light post-response work only (see [§5](#5-system-architecture)) | Same stack as RoleCast — reuse deployment, client, and testing patterns |
| Worker | Python 3.12 asyncio loop; claims from Postgres queue | No Celery/Redis: durable queue in Postgres via `SKIP LOCKED`; jobs debuggable with plain SQL (NFR5) |
| Orchestration (workflow mode) | Hand-rolled state machine: Pydantic `RunState` + ordered stage functions | The shipping baseline: linear pipeline, two fan-outs, no framework tax |
| Orchestration (agents mode) | LangGraph + Postgres checkpointer | Learning track only ([§10.11](#1011-agents-mode-learning-track)); its interrupt/resume model matches §10.10 checkpoints exactly — the design converged on it independently |
| Observability | Langfuse (cloud free tier or self-hosted) | Traces every LLM call with stage/mode/cost in both modes; wired day one (NFR6) |
| LLM access | Thin provider-agnostic client in `worker/pipeline/llm/`; per-stage model config in one settings file | Keeps the multi-provider door open without committing to it ([§11](#11-llm-strategy)) |
| Fetching | Tiered ladder: httpx → Playwright → unblocker/actor ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)) | Escalate per domain, not per fetch |
| HTML cleaning | trafilatura primary, readability-lxml fallback, script-stripped visible text as last resort, price-retention guard between layers ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-14), custom fee-table preserver, embedded structured-data miner (JSON-LD + framework state blobs, [§20](#20-decision-log) v2.6) | 5–10× token reduction before any LLM sees the page; the data sites ship as JSON survives it; an extraction that lost the page's rendered prices is rejected, not persisted |
| Maps/location | Google Maps Platform: Geocoding, Places, Routes | One vendor; monthly free credit covers this scale |
| DB/Auth/Storage/Realtime | Supabase | Postgres as the single stateful system (NFR5); RLS as the security boundary |
| Hosting | Cloudflare Pages (frontend) + Render (api, worker) + Supabase | Identical operational model to RoleCast |

---

## 8. Database Design

Postgres via Supabase migrations (`supabase/migrations/`). All timestamps UTC `timestamptz`. Soft deletes only where noted (comments and Invitation Links); everything else hard-deletes under RLS.

### 8.1 Enums

`hunt_role`: owner | curator | member · `job_type`: ingest | refresh | rescore | investigate · `job_state`: queued | running | waiting_user | done | failed | cancelled · `confidence`: high | medium | low | not_found · `fetch_outcome`: success | shell | blocked | not_listing | error · `value_state`: extracted | manual | estimated | unknown

Each enum type is created by the first migration whose tables need it (0001 creates `confidence` and `fetch_outcome`; the rest ship with the per-hunt/pipeline tables in 0002). Catalog and scoped-fact vocabulary columns (`category`, `domain`, `requires_tool`, `refresh_class`, `fact_scope`, `target_scope`, `applicability`, `record_kind`, `escalation_policy`, `conflict_policy`) are deliberately **text + check constraints**, not enum types: their vocabularies live in `shared/models.py`, and adding a catalog category must never require a Postgres enum migration. Current vocabularies are: `fact_scope = property | floor_plan | mixed | composed`; concrete Extraction `target_scope = property | floor_plan`; `applicability = specific_floor_plans | all_units | select_units | unit_scope_unspecified`; `record_kind = candidate | resolved`; `escalation_policy = decision_relevant | available_sources_only`; `conflict_policy = standard_ladder | verified_positive_preferred`.

### 8.2 Tables

Global (shared across hunts):

- **properties** — `id, name, canonical_address, city nullable, state nullable, county nullable, place_id, lat, lng, official_url, first_seen_at`. `name`/`canonical_address` start as URL-derived placeholders at submit; EXTRACT's `property_identity` block refreshes them (plus `official_url`) at ingest persist, non-null wins (§20 2026-07-10). DEDUPE's geocode owns `place_id`/`lat`/`lng` and normalized locality (`city`, `state` as a two-letter USPS code, `county` as Google's `administrative_area_level_2` long name). City precedence: Google `locality` → strict original-address postal city → null; `administrative_area_level_3` is never treated as a city. State precedence: US `administrative_area_level_1.short_name` → strict original-address parse. Worker-side address parsing is allowed; frontend parsing remains forbidden. Existing rows may remain locality-null until geocode refresh or the `backfill-locality` admin command. Dedup identity: geocode proximity (<100 m) AND name similarity above threshold; gray zone → checkpoint ([§10.10](#1010-user-checkpoints)). A `split_property` admin operation exists from day one — unmerging after extractions accumulate is miserable to retrofit.
- **property_sources** — `property_id, url, site_domain, is_official, last_fetched_at, last_success_at, cleaned_text, cleaned_text_path, cleaned_text_hash, image_urls jsonb, screenshot_path nullable`. Cleaned text is persisted as a plain `text` column (§20 2026-07-10 — KB-scale after cleaning; the gzipped-in-Storage artifact and its `cleaned_text_path` are superseded unless size ever argues otherwise) + image URL list; raw HTML is transient within a run. Trade-off accepted: cleaner bugs or future markup-dependent fields require a refetch. `screenshot_path` remains reserved and unpopulated; checkpoint screenshots are deferred in §18.
- **floor_plans** — `property_id, source_id, source_native_id nullable, detail_url nullable, plan_name, beds, baths, unit_types jsonb, sqft_min, sqft_max, rent_min, rent_max, deposit, availability_date, available_units, pricing_observed_at, lease_terms jsonb nullable, accessibility_features jsonb nullable, plan_fees jsonb nullable, concessions jsonb nullable, first_seen_at, last_seen_at, is_current, field_provenance jsonb, raw jsonb`. A Source-native ID is the preferred stable identity when present; otherwise the fallback natural identity is `(source_id, plan_name, beds, baths)`. Partial unique indexes enforce both paths. Refresh upserts a plan, updates `last_seen_at`, and marks a missing plan inactive only after a successful complete Source refresh; it never mints a replacement merely because price or availability changed. Lease terms, accessibility, fees, and concessions populate only when Source evidence explicitly ties them to that Floor Plan; otherwise they remain null rather than inheriting Property claims. `scores`, pins, scoped Extractions, and diagram associations reference the stable `floor_plan_id`.
- **extractions** — the single append-only Property/Floor Plan fact store: `property_id, hunt_id nullable, criterion_key, record_kind (candidate | resolved), origin_key, source_id nullable, target_scope (property | floor_plan), floor_plan_id nullable, applicability nullable, claim_variant nullable, claim_group_id, value jsonb, confidence, evidence_quote, model, resolution_rule nullable, disputed bool, job_id nullable, extracted_at`. `origin_key` is the non-null Source-claim lineage (`property_source:<id>` for page facts; stable producer keys such as `google_maps:grocery` for non-page facts); `source_id` is populated only for an actual Source. Constraints require `floor_plan_id` exactly for a `floor_plan` target and require the Floor Plan to belong to the same Property. Property facts have null applicability; exact unit claims use `specific_floor_plans`; generalized unit claims target the Property with `all_units`, `select_units`, or `unit_scope_unspecified`. One EXTRACT claim may target several Source-local Floor Plans; persistence expands it to one candidate row per plan with a shared `claim_group_id`. `claim_variant` is non-null for typed multi-claim Criteria (`in_unit_laundry`, `parking`, `cooling`, and the internal `heating_type`) and for distinct `flooring_materials` sets, keeping each supported value/set independently current.

  **Current-value contract:** `current_extraction_candidates` selects the newest candidate per `(property, nullable hunt/custom scope, criterion, origin_key, concrete target, nullable claim variant)` using `(extracted_at, id)` as the deterministic version order. `current_extractions` selects the newest resolved row per `(property, nullable hunt/custom scope, criterion, concrete target, nullable claim variant)`. Applicability remains claim data rather than a lineage dimension; for one variant, a newer `select_units` observation supersedes an older `all_units` observation from the same origin/target, while a different typed value remains independently current. These security-invoker views (or one repository seam with exactly the same SQL) are the only supported current-value reads. Successful complete refreshes append not-found/unknown candidates per disappeared variant; partial or failed work appends nothing, so silence never becomes `none` and history is never updated or deleted. `hunt_id` is NULL for Catalog facts and set for custom Criteria. The pipeline still extracts the full text Catalog regardless of the active Rubric.

- **extraction_resolution_candidates** — append-only provenance join: `resolution_extraction_id, candidate_extraction_id, selected bool`, both referencing `extractions`. The resolution row carries the winning value/confidence/evidence, `resolution_rule`, and `disputed`; the join preserves every candidate considered without embedding an opaque candidate blob. A single-Source run writes one candidate plus one resolved row (`resolution_rule = single_source`).
- **property_images** — `property_id, source_id, source_url, source_page_order, storage_path, content_hash, perceptual_hash, width, height, byte_size, kind (listing_photo | floor_plan_diagram | other), normalization_profile, discovery_context jsonb, vision_assessment jsonb`. Content-addressed normalized WebP. IMAGE_FETCH stores up to `MAX_STORED_IMAGES = 30`, source-balanced when several Sources contribute. `vision_assessment.classification` is versioned by classifier model/prompt and records room/visibility/usability signals; derivatives used for classification are generated in memory and never stored.
- **floor_plan_images** — append-only many-to-many image-association ledger for ordinary photos and diagrams: `id, floor_plan_id, property_image_id, source_id, action (link | unlink), association_method, confidence, evidence_context, display_order, actor_user_id nullable, producer_job_id nullable, observed_at`. The current association is the newest `(observed_at, id)` event per Floor Plan/image pair when its action is `link`; `current_floor_plan_images` centralizes that rule. Image and Floor Plan must belong to the same Property; automatic links are Source-local and require unambiguous native ID, containing-card, or nearby-label evidence. Visual similarity and cross-Source matching never establish identity. A successful complete refresh may append an `unlink`; partial work cannot.
- **extraction_images** — structured visual provenance joining one candidate/resolved Extraction to each contributing `property_images` row, with same-Property validation and matching RLS. Storage paths never exist only in prose.
- **property_contacts** — append-only property-level contact observations (P3-21): `id, property_id, kind (phone | contact_url), value, provenance (official_site | google_places | listing), source_id nullable, observed_at`. Deliberately **not** a Catalog Criterion: Catalog rows carry rubric options and deltas, and a phone number has no rubric semantics — contact values never enter the scoring path. Deliberately **no column for a person's name or role** (§16): property-level business contact only. Two producers — EXTRACT's `property_contact` block (ranked `official_site` when the fetched Source is the Property's official site, else `listing`) and ENRICH's Place Details call (`google_places`). Re-ingesting the same page refreshes `observed_at` rather than appending, so append-only does not mean unbounded.

  **Resolution is strict provenance precedence, not the [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution) ladder.** `property_contacts_current` (security-invoker) selects one winner per `(property, kind)` ranked `official_site` → `google_places` → `listing`, ties broken by freshest `observed_at`. The ladder is built for numeric agreement — tolerance collapse, supermajority, family-deduped votes — and none of it transfers here: several aggregators carrying identical digits usually means one call-tracking proxy line echoed through a feed, not corroboration. Contact facts are never scored, never Gate-bearing, and never raise a `resolve_dispute` checkpoint. When no rung yields a value the system stops rather than escalating: the drawer renders no Contact row and the reader falls back to the Sources card and `official_url`.
- **criteria_catalog** (seeded from `shared/`) — `key, label, category, domain (rent | buy | both), fact_scope, value_schema jsonb, claim_value_schema jsonb nullable, default_options jsonb, extraction_hint, requires_tool (null|maps|vision|web_search), refresh_class, escalation_policy, conflict_policy`. A hunt sees only its domain's slice. `value_schema` is the single source of truth for backend Rubric-option validation, deterministic scoring, and frontend widgets. `claim_value_schema` is null when Source claims use the same shape; it differs only when applicability is deterministically composed into the scoreable value (for example, a boolean unit claim becomes `confirmed | advertised_unconfirmed | none` for one Floor Plan). One complete entry, since the subsystems must agree on this shape:

```json
{ "key": "beds", "label": "Number of bedrooms", "category": "unit",
  "fact_scope": "floor_plan",
  "value_schema": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 0, "maximum": 5 },
  "claim_value_schema": null,
  "default_options": [
    { "match": {"op": "eq", "value": 2}, "delta": 0.5 },
    { "match": {"op": "eq", "value": 1}, "delta": 0.0 },
    { "match": {"op": "eq", "value": 0}, "delta": -0.5 }
  ],
  "extraction_hint": "Count distinct bedrooms; a studio is 0.",
  "requires_tool": null,
  "refresh_class": "listing_details",
  "escalation_policy": "decision_relevant",
  "conflict_policy": "standard_ladder" }
```

The v1 seed set (`refresh_class` values map to the TTL table in [§14](#14-caching-and-refresh-strategy)):

| key | category | value type | fact scope | requires_tool | refresh_class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| beds | unit | int 0–5 | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| baths | unit | number 1–4 (0.5 steps) | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| sqft | unit | int | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| patio_balcony | unit | effective enum: confirmed, advertised_unconfirmed, none (boolean Source claim) | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| private_entry | unit | effective enum: confirmed, advertised_unconfirmed, none (boolean Source claim) | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| in_unit_laundry | fittings | set: in_unit, hookups, on_site, none, advertised_unconfirmed | mixed | — | listing_details |
| pets_policy | tenancy | enum: cats_and_dogs, cats_only, dogs_only, none | property | — | listing_details |
| all_in_monthly | cost | number (composed, [§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)) | composed | — | pricing |
| security_deposit | cost | number | floor_plan | — | pricing |
| availability_date | tenancy | date (ISO; phrase-only immediate availability extracts as transient sentinel `available_now`, rewritten in EXTRACT to the run date live or corpus `saved_at` on the bench — §20 2026-07-16; explicit Property/Floor Plan dates always win) | floor_plan | — | pricing |
| kitchen_quality | fittings | int 1–5 (anchored, [§10.8](#108-vision)) | floor_plan | vision | images |
| flooring_quality | fittings | int 1–5 (anchored) | floor_plan | vision | images |
| parking | unit | set: garage, carport, covered, dedicated_lot, street_only, none, advertised_unconfirmed | mixed | — | listing_details |
| cooling | fittings | set: central, window_units, none, advertised_unconfirmed | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| dishwasher | fittings | effective enum: confirmed, advertised_unconfirmed, none (boolean Source claim) | floor_plan | — | listing_details |
| min_lease_months | tenancy | int (shortest offered term) | mixed | — | pricing |
| grocery_proximity | location | number (minutes, walking or driving per the hunt's `proximity_mode` setting) | property | maps | location |
| management_reviews | management | number 1–5 + summary text | property | maps (Places reviews) | reviews |
| location_safety | location | enum letter grade `A+`…`F` (13 grades incl. +/−), low-confidence by design (R8). v1 is a **placeholder**: ENRICH emits no value (the criterion scores unknown); the A+–F override dropdown is the input path until the dedicated safety module lands ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog), [§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18) | property | web_search | reviews |

Policy defaults are independent of category and scope. Gate/cost/availability/pet-eligibility Criteria use `decision_relevant + standard_ladder`. Preference-only pool/fitness/clubhouse/service conveniences, package handling, general internet readiness, and the first objective unit-feature tranche use `available_sources_only + verified_positive_preferred`; making any such Criterion Gate-bearing changes its effective escalation policy to `decision_relevant` for that Hunt.

P3-SC3 adds the first Property tranche: `pool` (`indoor | outdoor | indoor_and_outdoor | unspecified | none`, private pools deferred), `fitness_center` bool, `clubhouse` bool, `emergency_maintenance` (`emergency_service | 24_hour_emergency | none`), `maintenance_on_site` bool, `management_on_site` bool, `online_payments` bool, `online_maintenance_requests` bool, `package_handling` (`locker | secured_room | office | unsecured_area | none`), `smoking_policy` (`smoke_free_property | designated_areas_only | permitted`), `property_types` array, `unit_types` array, and `internet_readiness` bool. Generic `resident_portal` remains display evidence; the two capabilities are the scoreable Criteria. `internet_readiness` means only that Source listing evidence advertises general internet/high-speed/fiber readiness across the Property; silence is unknown, and it does not assert provider serviceability, speed/type, Floor Plan coverage, or inclusion in rent. Property/Unit type vocabulary is `apartment | condo | townhome | duplex | single_family | loft | other`; array operators are `contains_any` (non-empty intersection) and `contains_all` (extracted set contains every configured value). `property_types` reconciles explicit Property claims and separately identified Floor Plan types without silently replacing one with the other; disagreement remains provenance-visible.

P3-SC6 adds `walk_in_closets`, `pantry`, `disposal`, `fireplace`, `ceiling_fans`, and `stainless_steel_appliances` as `fittings`-category scoped unit presence Criteria. Their boolean Source claims compose to `confirmed | advertised_unconfirmed | none`, their default deltas are `+0.25 | 0 | 0`, and their Catalog policies are `available_sources_only + verified_positive_preferred`.

P3-SC7 adds mixed-scope objective `flooring_materials`, a controlled non-empty set of `carpet | hardwood | engineered_wood | laminate | vinyl | tile | concrete | other`. Exact Floor Plan and explicit `all_units` claims expose that set to `contains_any`/`contains_all`; `select_units` and `unit_scope_unspecified` remain provenance-visible advertised evidence but score as unknown for a concrete Floor Plan. It may coexist with subjective `flooring_quality`, but enabling both produces a non-blocking Rubric-editor warning about possible double-weighting. Storage, basement, granular/externally verified internet, private pools, individual-unit inventory, inferred diagram fields/OCR, and cross-Source Floor Plan identity remain deferred ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)).

The §9.5 utility/fee fields (`utilities_included`, `mandatory_fees`, `pet_costs`, `heating_type`) are extraction fields feeding the `all_in_monthly` composition, not standalone criteria.

**Deliberately absent** (documented so they aren't "helpfully" added later without revisiting the reasoning): **base rent** — owned by `floor_plans` and scored through `all_in_monthly`; a separate rent criterion would double-count the number the composition already weighs. **last-renovated year** — a separate renovation-year field remains absent; **`year_built`** and **`is_renovated`** were added 2026-08-02 (§20) as Owner-requested catalog Criteria with rubric double-weight warnings when VISION kitchen quality is also enabled. **Amenities catch-all** (gym, pool, clubhouse…) — an unscoreable grab-bag as one criterion; any specific amenity someone cares about is exactly what custom criteria exist for. **Floor level** and **commute-to-address** — the canonical custom-criteria examples, per §9.2.
- **utility_baselines** — `geo_level (city | county | state), state, region_name, beds_bucket, utility, monthly_high, monthly_median, sources jsonb, refreshed_at`. Primary key `(geo_level, state, region_name, beds_bucket, utility)`; for state rows `region_name = state`. Regional; 120-day TTL with all 24 rows required for freshness; `monthly_high` is the winter-weighted peak-month figure ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)).
- **fetch_adapter_registry** — `site_domain, required_tier, adapter_config jsonb, last_success_tier, last_outcome, updated_at` ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)).

Per hunt:

- **hunts** — `id, name, owner_id, domain (rent | buy, v1 supports rent only), rubric_version int, settings jsonb, archived_at, created_at`. `settings` is the **hunt settings object** — Owner-edited ([§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions)), API-validated, with every key defaulted so an empty object is always valid. Its shape is pinned here because the API (validation), the engine/composer (scoring inputs), the planner (source policy), and the frontend (settings panel) all read it:

```json
{ "default_source_policy": "tiers_1_2_3",
  "cost_estimate_mode": "conservative",
  "min_confidence": "medium",
  "min_vision_confidence": "low",
  "generalized_vision_policy": "full_rubric",
  "proximity_mode": "driving",
  "occupants": 1,
  "cats": 0,
  "dogs": 0 }
```

  Key semantics, each owned by the section cited: `default_source_policy` — seeds the per-submission Source Policy selector ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)); editing it affects **future submissions only** — existing listings keep their persisted per-listing policy. `cost_estimate_mode` (`conservative | median`) — which baseline figure estimated utility components use in the all-in composition ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)). `min_confidence` (`low | medium | high`) — non-VISION Extractions below this confidence score as unknown ([§9.3](#93-scoring-engine)); default `medium`. `min_vision_confidence` uses the same vocabulary but defaults to `low`: visual estimates at or above it remain available for display and points. A low-confidence `vision:*` fact never enters Gate values even when admitted by this threshold; medium/high VISION may enter Gates subject to `generalized_vision_policy`. `proximity_mode` (`walking | driving`) — travel mode for location-proximity criteria such as `grocery_proximity`.

  Household keys (§20 2026-07-10): `cats`/`dogs` (0–10) — how many of each species move in; they multiply species-specific pet rent into the all-in composition ([§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost)). `occupants` (1–20) — reserved reader is P3-9 utility-baseline scaling; named now so the settings shape doesn't churn twice.

  Edit effects follow from what each key feeds: `cost_estimate_mode`, `min_confidence`, `min_vision_confidence`, `generalized_vision_policy`, `cats`, and `dogs` are **scoring inputs**, so editing them takes the exact rubric-mutation path ([§9.2](#92-rubric-system)) — bump `rubric_version`, enqueue the free hunt-level rescore; `rubric_version` is honestly "the version of how points are computed," and these settings are part of that. `proximity_mode` changes what ENRICH computes, so editing it enqueues a field-scoped refresh of location-class criteria ([§14](#14-caching-and-refresh-strategy)) — cheap Maps calls, no LLM. `default_source_policy` triggers nothing. `occupants` is also a scoring input since P3-9 utility scaling and follows the same rescore path.
- **hunt_shared_filters** — `hunt_id (pk), filters jsonb, updated_by, updated_at` ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-19). The hunt-wide Overview filter set: **view state, deliberately outside the pinned `settings` contract** — filters are not scoring inputs and must not ride settings' bump-and-rescore edit semantics. Owner/Curator write (curation precedent, like `listing_unit_group_states`), all members read; writes go through `PUT /v1/hunts/{id}/shared-filters` under the user JWT with RLS double-enforcing role. `filters` stores an Overview filter-state object the frontend **sanitizes on read** (unknown keys drop, missing keys default), so the stored shape may lag the filter registry without migration churn. Seed-on-open semantics live in [§13.2](#132-key-views-mantine-mapping); not on the [§13.3](#133-realtime) channel list by design.
- **user_profiles** — `user_id, default_display_name, default_color, created_at, updated_at`. Created by mandatory onboarding; defaults are inherited by every Hunt whose membership has no override. `default_color` (§20 2026-07-18, refined 2026-07-19) is required and uses the same vocabulary as `hunt_members.color`; onboarding omits it so a DB trigger assigns the first globally unused ordered palette token (wrapping when all six are taken). Edited on the `/profile` page. RLS permits self read/write and reads by users sharing a Hunt.
- **user_notification_prefs** — `user_id, event_type, channel, enabled, updated_at`, self-owned under RLS. Missing rows are meaningful defaults, not silence: checkpoint waiting and run failed email are on; Listing score changed, comment added, and rating changed are off. Hunt invitation is absent because it is mandatory and addressed to an email, not a member preference.
- **hunt_members** — `hunt_id, user_id, role hunt_role, color nullable, display_name nullable`. `display_name` and `color` are Hunt-specific overrides; effective collaboration identity is `coalesce(hunt_members.display_name, user_profiles.default_display_name)` and effective color `coalesce(hunt_members.color, user_profiles.default_color)`. Changing a Hunt override never changes the account default, while changing the default immediately affects only Hunts without overrides. Existing non-null values remain overrides. `color` is either one of the ordered palette tokens (`moss`, `ochre`, `brick`, `olive`, `stone`, `plum`) or a canonical custom `#RRGGBB` value; invite acceptance assigns the first unused token within the Hunt (wrapping when all six are taken) to users without a `default_color` (membership inserts inherit the default — color null — when one exists), and members may later choose any valid hex color per hunt. Every per-hunt RLS policy keys off this table.
- **hunt_notification_prefs** — `hunt_id, user_id, event_type, channel, enabled, updated_at`. A row is an explicit per-Hunt override; no row inherits the account value. RLS permits only that member to read/write the row while membership exists.
- **invites** — `hunt_id, email, token, role_granted, created_by, expires_at, accepted_by`. Single-recipient email invitations; acceptance may grant Member or Curator.

Private notification infrastructure has no browser grants: `notification_events` is the immutable event/dedupe/context ledger, `notification_deliveries` is one recipient/channel attempt with queue lock, bounded retry, provider id and delivery state, and `notification_webhook_events` deduplicates signed Resend callbacks by `svix_id`. Revoking an Invite cancels its unsent delivery; dispatch re-checks that an Invite is active or that a product-notification recipient is still a Hunt member before sending.
- **invitation_links** — `hunt_id, token, created_by, created_at, name nullable (1–80 trimmed chars), max_uses nullable, expires_at, deleted_at nullable, deleted_by nullable`. `name` is an optional Owner-facing label and is not unique. `max_uses = NULL` means unlimited. A link is active exactly when it is not deleted, has not expired, and its successful-join count is below its non-null limit. Deletion is soft and irreversible through the ordinary UI; deleted rows never appear in Owner lists and the token is refused.
- **invitation_link_joins** — append-only `(invitation_link_id, user_id, display_name, joined_at)`, unique per link/user. This is both the atomic usage counter and the Owner-visible attribution ledger; `display_name` is snapshotted so removal from the Hunt does not erase who joined.
- **hunt_listings** — `hunt_id, property_id, added_by, submitted_source_id, status (active | archived), source_policy text, single_source_reason text nullable, pins jsonb, created_at, unavailable_at, all_in_components jsonb`. `submitted_source_id` preserves the Listing's submitted Source as a same-Property foreign key; refresh always includes it, then adds current contributing Sources allowed by `source_policy`. `all_in_components` (P3-9, [§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18) is the display plan's [§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost) composition detail — components with `actual | estimated | unknown` tags plus badges — written by ingest projection and rescore in the same transaction as `scores`; **display metadata only**, the pinned `scores.breakdown` ([§9.3](#93-scoring-engine)) stays the scoring truth. `unavailable_at` is set when an ingest/refresh finds **no available floor plans** even after cross-validation — a legitimate result, not an error: the listing persists and renders as a **dimmed, null-score** row (distinct from a failed job and from a still-ingesting one), and the marker clears when a later refresh finds plans (reversible). It is orthogonal to `status` (a no-availability listing is still `active`). `source_policy` is the Source Policy chosen at submission ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)) — text + check constraint, defaulted from `hunts.settings.default_source_policy`; refresh jobs read it so a trusted-link listing never silently grows sibling sources. `single_source_reason` is nullable and constrained to `trust_link | discover_exhausted | discover_failed`; it records why the Listing currently lacks a cross-check. `pins` maps a Unit Group key (`"{beds}-{baths}"`) to a `floor_plan_id` — a manual pin is **per Unit Group**, not per Listing, because one Listing typically holds several groups. Filter-selected Floor Plans are derived client-side and never enter this object. `status = archived` is reversible hiding, not deletion. Permanent deletion removes this row and every FK-owned Hunt-local child; because Unit Groups are derived rather than stored, all of this Listing's Unit Groups disappear with the association. It also removes Visits and Hunt-scoped custom Extractions for the same `(hunt_id, property_id)`, which are structurally owned by that association even though their immediate keys are Hunt + Property. It never deletes the Property or global truth, so another Hunt's Listing is unchanged. A minimal deletion tombstone survives without an FK to the deleted row.
- **hunt_listing_refresh_status** — `hunt_listing_id, refresh_class, last_success_at, producer_job_id nullable`, primary key `(hunt_listing_id, refresh_class)`. Listing-scoped assurance marker for `pricing | listing_details | images | reviews | location`; the worker advances it only after the requested class projection succeeds. It is readable by Hunt members and service-role-writable only. Failed or underfilled partial work leaves the prior timestamp untouched; a cap-saturated IMAGE_FETCH (`MAX_STORED_IMAGES` photos stored) advances `images` even when lower-priority candidates failed to download ([§10.8](#108-vision)).
- **rubric_criteria** — `hunt_id, catalog_key nullable, custom_def jsonb nullable, enabled, options jsonb, unknown_delta numeric, non_negotiable jsonb nullable, is_bonus bool (derived), position`. Option shape (one of two places code snippets are warranted — this object is load-bearing):

```json
{ "match": {"op": "eq|lt|lte|gt|gte|range|in|bool|contains_any|contains_all", "value": ...},
  "delta": 0.5,
  "dealbreaker_set_score": null }
```

- **overrides** — `hunt_listing_id, criterion_key, target_scope (property | floor_plan), floor_plan_id nullable, applicability nullable, value jsonb, user_id, note, created_at`. Append-only history; current identity is `(Listing, Criterion, concrete target)`, newest `(created_at, id)` wins, and a null value is that target's revert tombstone. A Property target with null applicability overrides a true Property fact; a Property target with `all_units` overrides the unit claim for every Floor Plan; an exact Floor Plan target overrides only that plan. Human select/unspecified applicability correction is deferred until demonstrated Curator need.
- **utility_overrides** — append-only, per-Listing utility corrections: `hunt_listing_id, utility (electric | gas | water | sewer | cooling | heat | trash), included nullable bool, monthly_amount nullable numeric, user_id, note, created_at`. Current identity is `(Listing, utility)` and newest `(created_at, id)` wins. `included` manually corrects whether rent covers that utility; `monthly_amount` replaces an extracted or regional monthly figure when billed; both null is the independent revert tombstone that returns only that utility to extracted/baseline truth. It is deliberately separate from Criterion Overrides and `fee_checklist`: utilities are named composition inputs whose fallback may be a regional estimate, while `fee_checklist` owns non-utility recurring charges and move-in fees.
- **fee_checklist** — `hunt_listing_id, fee_slot, amount, value_state, entered_by nullable, evidence_ref nullable, updated_at`.
- **scores** — `hunt_listing_id, floor_plan_id, total numeric, breakdown jsonb, rubric_version, computed_at, all_in_components jsonb`. Breakdown records per criterion: matched option, delta, gate firings. `all_in_components` ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18) is **this plan's** [§9.5](#95-utilities-and-all-in-monthly-cost) composition detail — the cell/drawer render the composition of the plan the row displays, not the listing-level display plan's; display metadata only, the pinned breakdown stays the scoring truth.
- **comments** — `hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key nullable, user_id, body, created_at, edited_at nullable, deleted_at`. `unit_group_key = NULL` is a Listing-wide comment; otherwise it uses the same stable `"{beds}-{baths}"` Unit Group key as `hunt_listings.pins`. Authors may edit the body, stamping `edited_at`, or soft-delete; ordinary collaboration reads exclude deleted rows. Owner moderation is deferred unless real use demonstrates a need.
- **ratings** — `hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key, user_id, rating numeric(2,1)`, primary key `(hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key, user_id)`. Values are Hunt opinion from 1 through 5 in half-star steps (`1, 1.5, …, 5`). A rating belongs to the derived Unit Group represented by a table row, not to an individual Floor Plan; the Unit Group key must exist on the Listing.
- **listing_unit_group_states** — `hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key, interest_status nullable, visited bool, updated_by, updated_at`, primary key `(hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key)`. The row attaches Hunt opinion to a derived Unit Group without pretending the group is stored. `interest_status = NULL` means undecided. Only Owners and Curators mutate; every Hunt member reads. RLS validates that the key currently exists on the Listing. Pipeline availability remains separate: manual `unavailable` is Curator knowledge about one Unit Group, while `hunt_listings.unavailable_at` is the worker's Listing-wide no-plans result.

Visits ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)) — Hunt opinion about a tour, structurally parallel to comments/ratings and equally outside the fact store:

- **visits** — `id, hunt_id, property_id, created_by, scheduled_for nullable, started_at nullable, ended_at nullable, reopened_at nullable, cancelled_at nullable, cancel_reason nullable, template_version int, prefilled_from nullable self-reference, created_at`. **State is derived from the timestamps, never stored**: `cancelled_at` → cancelled, else `ended_at` **not superseded by a later `reopened_at`** → completed, else `started_at` → in progress, else planned. `reopened_at` holds only the most recent reopen and **never clears `ended_at`** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.37): when a tour ended is a fact about a real afternoon, and ending a reopened tour simply stamps a new, later `ended_at`. A status column would be a second truth that drifts from the times the UI already has to display, and a "completed" row with no `ended_at` is a bug the schema should not be able to express. One row per *tour*, so a first showing and a serious second look are distinct records; `prefilled_from` records that a Visit was seeded from an earlier one at the same Property so only changes had to be re-entered. `template_version` is frozen at creation — a later template edit must not retroactively change what a finished Visit asked or what its completion percentage means. A `(hunt_id, property_id)` pair must have a `hunt_listings` row; RLS enforces it, the same cross-scope guard the cross-property Floor Plan override already uses.
- **visit_units** — `id, visit_id, label text, floor_plan_id nullable, beds smallint, baths numeric(3,1), display_order, created_by, created_at`, plus `unit_group_key` **generated** as `beds || '-' || baths` — the same stable `"{beds}-{baths}"` key `hunt_listings.pins`, `ratings`, `comments`, and `listing_unit_group_states` use, so a Visit rolls up through the identity the rest of the app already compares on. `beds`/`baths` are copied from the Floor Plan when one is chosen and typed when it is not; `floor_plan_id is null` marks a unit the listing never advertised, which the UI shows as such rather than implying it was found. When set, the Floor Plan must belong to the Visit's Property. Every label is human-entered — there is no advertised unit list to select from — so the schema deliberately has no notion of a unit existing independently of somebody having walked through it.
- **visit_template_items** — global, seeded, service-role-writable like the Catalog: `key, version, section_key, display_order, tier (quick | standard | thorough), kind (fact | check | question | impression), scope (property | unit), label, help nullable, value_schema jsonb, is_critical bool`.
