The canonical source is a Python module in the API package with a parity test asserting the table matches it — the arrangement `criteria_catalog` already uses. It cannot live in `shared/`, which stays domain-blind; a tour checklist is irreducibly rental-specific. `tier` drives the Quick/Standard/Thorough depth dial, which is how a ~160-item document stays usable on a rushed showing without deleting anything.
- **visit_custom_items** — `id, visit_id, section_key, kind, scope, label, created_by, created_at`. Per-Visit, and always rendered as visibly custom so a housemate's addition is never mistaken for the built-in list. Hunt-wide recurring custom items and Rubric-derived ones are deferred ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)).
- **visit_entries** — the append-only answer store: `id, visit_id, item_key, is_custom bool, visit_unit_id nullable, owner_user_id nullable, author_user_id, value jsonb, answer_text nullable, note nullable, prev_entry_id nullable, created_at`. Current identity is `(visit_id, item_key, visit_unit_id, owner_user_id)` and newest `(created_at, id)` wins, centralized in the `current_visit_entries` view rather than re-derived per caller. `visit_unit_id is null` ⇔ a `property`-scoped item; `owner_user_id is null` ⇔ a shared item (Fact/Check/Question), set ⇔ an Impression keyed to that member. `author_user_id` is always who wrote the row, which is why shared items still attribute ("Dan changed this 12s ago") without a second table. `prev_entry_id` is the row the author believed was current — the offline-conflict mechanism in [§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist), not an audit nicety. Append-only buys the byline history, the conflict fork, and consistency with `extractions`/`overrides` in one shape. Open forks are read through the derived `visit_entry_conflicts` view (one row per distinct competing answer, keyed by `fork_parent_id`), which owns the definition of a conflict so no caller re-derives it; there is no conflict table and no resolved flag, because resolution is an ordinary answer.
- **visit_defects** — `id, visit_id, visit_unit_id nullable (null = the building, not an omission), from_item_key nullable, title, note nullable, severity smallint 1–5, promised_in_writing bool, resolution nullable, created_by, created_at, deleted_at nullable`. `from_item_key` records that the row was promoted from a failed Check, so the defect log fills itself during the walk instead of being a separate table nobody transcribes into afterwards.
- **visit_fee_proposals** — `id, visit_id, visit_unit_id nullable, hunt_listing_id, target (fee_slot | override), target_key, amount numeric(10,2), status (pending | accepted | rejected), decided_by nullable, decided_at nullable, created_by, created_at`. `target` exists because the two destinations are genuinely different tables: recurring/move-in charges upsert `fee_checklist` in place, while `base_rent`/`all_in_monthly` append to `overrides`. One proposal shape, two accept paths, and rejection writes only `status`.

Pipeline:

- **jobs** — `id, hunt_id, hunt_listing_id, type, state, current_stage, plan jsonb, payload jsonb, warnings jsonb, attempts, error, cost_actual_usd numeric (summed from provider usage fields by the LLM client), locked_by, locked_at, created_at, started_at, finished_at`. `warnings` (default `[]`) is the run's non-fatal degradations — `{stage, code, message, detail}` per entry, rewritten from the RunState on every persist-before-advance save, so a stage owns its own entries and a resume replaces rather than duplicates them. It is exposed on `JobResponse` and rendered on the live and history task cards. A warning is categorically not an `error` and not a checkpoint: it never changes what the pipeline does, it only says the run finished slightly short of its inputs (§10.8). `hunt_id` scopes every job to its hunt directly — listing-scoped jobs also carry `hunt_listing_id`, hunt-level jobs (rescore) do not — so the Tasks query and P2-1 RLS filter on one indexed column instead of reaching through a jsonb payload (NOT NULL, enforced by migration 0005). `started_at` (nullable, stamped on the first claim only via `coalesce(started_at, now())`, so a reclaim/resume never resets it) splits queue-wait (`created_at`→`started_at`) from run-time (`started_at`→`finished_at`); it is exposed on `JobResponse` so running job cards can display elapsed time while remaining useful for operational metrics. The queue (claimed via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), the live task state, and — because rows are never deleted — the task history (FR10). Crash recovery: the claiming worker refreshes `locked_at` as a heartbeat between stages; a `running` job whose `locked_at` is older than 5 minutes is considered orphaned and reclaimable by any worker, which resumes it from `current_stage` — safe precisely because stages persist before advancing (NFR3). Orphan reclaim caps at `MANZIL_JOB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5): a job reclaimed that many times is dead-lettered to `failed` rather than re-queued, so a crash loop cannot churn the queue forever — a manual retry (which resets `attempts`) is the only way back.
- **job_events** — `job_id, stage, event (started|completed|failed|checkpoint_asked|checkpoint_answered|checkpoint_auto_resolved|escalated_tier), detail jsonb, at`. Feeds the per-job timeline in the History tab and the realtime Tasks view.

### 8.3 RLS Strategy

Global tables (`properties`, Catalog `extractions`, `floor_plans`, image metadata, …) are readable by any authenticated user and writable only by the service role (worker/API) — regular clients never write facts. Hunt-scoped custom-Criterion Extractions (`hunt_id is not null`), their candidate links, and their rows in current-value views are visible only to that Hunt's members, so neither candidate provenance nor resolutions leak through the global namespace. The centralized current-candidate/current-resolution views are `security_invoker`; they must preserve the base tables' RLS rather than bypass it. Floor Plan diagram bytes stay in private Storage and signed-URL access is granted only in an authenticated Hunt context containing that Property.

Visit tables follow the per-hunt pattern: every member of the owning Hunt reads them, and the write policies mirror [§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions) — any member creates a Visit and answers its items, while cancel/delete narrows to the creator or the Owner and Fee Proposal decisions reuse the Override permission, because accepting one *is* a cost write. `visit_template_items` is global read-only reference data like the Catalog. `visit_units.floor_plan_id` and `visits.property_id` are both validated against the Visit's Hunt at the policy layer, so neither a foreign Property nor another Hunt's Floor Plan can be attached by a direct PostgREST write. `current_visit_entries`, `visit_entry_conflicts`, `visit_unit_impressions` and `visit_unit_group_scores` are `security_invoker`, preserving the base tables' RLS as every current-value view here must.

Permanent Listing deletion is a narrow function boundary, not a table privilege. `authenticated` has no direct `DELETE` policy on `hunt_listings`; the Owner RPC locks the Listing, re-checks exact Owner membership, refuses Demo Accounts, requires `status = archived`, compares the confirmation string to the exact Property name, refuses `queued | running | waiting_user` Jobs, writes the deletion tombstone, removes Hunt-scoped custom Extractions, and deletes the Listing in one transaction. A composite `visits(hunt_id, property_id) → hunt_listings(hunt_id, property_id) ON DELETE CASCADE` foreign key makes Visit cleanup and the no-concurrent-orphan rule structural. The Ghost View route calls the same private core only after proving the actor is a Site Admin and not a Hunt member; its Admin Audit Log entry shares the deletion transaction, so audit failure rolls the deletion back. A Site Admin who is a Hunt member cannot select Ghost View to escape that membership's assigned role.

The Hunt activity feed is a deliberate two-boundary exception ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.60). Its live, cross-Hunt union is `private.hunt_activity_all`, a `security_barrier` view with no client grants and no caller-aware predicate. Hunt Owners read one Hunt at a time through the bounded `get_hunt_activity(hunt_id, limit, before)` RPC, which verifies the exact Owner role before touching the union. Site Admins do not inherit that RPC permission: the Admin API reads the private view only after its live `AdminUser` check. Curators, Members, Demo Accounts and anonymous callers can neither read the raw relation nor successfully call the Owner RPC. There is no public raw activity view.

Per-hunt tables are readable/writable per the [permissions matrix](#42-roles-and-permissions), expressed as policies through the security-definer `private.member_role(hunt_id)` helper to avoid recursive `hunt_members` policy evaluation. Invitation Links and their join ledgers are Owner-readable only; an authenticated token holder joins solely through the narrow service-role transaction, never by direct table access. Hunt SELECT also admits the authenticated user recorded in `owner_id`, narrowly allowing PostgREST to return a newly inserted Hunt; the atomic Owner-membership trigger establishes ordinary authorization. Realtime respects RLS, so subscription security is automatic. The API runs with the user's JWT for user-initiated writes and the service role only inside the worker and explicitly named privileged operations.

Notification preferences follow the same boundary: account defaults are self-only; Hunt overrides require both the same `user_id` and current Hunt membership. The event, delivery, and webhook ledgers live in `private` with all browser grants revoked. The Resend webhook verifies the raw Svix signature before it can mutate a delivery, and out-of-order callbacks cannot regress a delivered or terminal failure state. Email templates autoescape member/Hunt content; subjects strip line breaks. The API key, webhook secret, and From address are server-only, and production startup fails unless product mail is explicitly in `resend` mode with all three configured.

---

## 9. Business Logic

### 9.1 Hunts, Membership, Invites

Accounts are provisioned only by a Site Admin and enroll through Supabase's password-setup/recovery email. Password recovery, existing-account magic-link login, six-digit email OTP verification, and security-change notices use Supabase Auth through its Resend-backed SMTP configuration; the link and OTP share the same email and return destination and never create an account. Every authenticated user without `user_profiles` completes display-name onboarding before Hunt creation or invitation acceptance. Hunt creation makes the creator Owner and opens the rubric wizard (skippable; first URL submission is blocked until a rubric exists). Email invites are single-recipient, carry a role (`member` default; Owner may grant `curator`), and are **Manzil product mail sent through Resend HTTPS**. Their CTA is the bare, expiring `/invite/{token}` capability — never an Auth action link and never a session — so the ordinary auth guard owns sign-in and return-to-invite.

Notification recipients are deterministic. A checkpoint waits on the Listing submitter; a failed Job goes to the submitter plus the Hunt Owner, deduplicated when they are the same account; one completed Job produces at most one score-change summary per changed Listing, addressed to that Listing's submitter; a comment or rating goes to every other Hunt member whose effective preference enables it. Invites always email the literal invite address and bypass preferences. Account defaults are checkpoint/run-failed on and score/comment/rating off; a nullable per-Hunt override wins over the account default. Delivery failure never changes Job state or rolls back a successful Job.

Invitation Links are separate reusable resources and always grant Member. Creation defaults to a seven-day expiration and unlimited successful joins; the Owner may set and later edit either value, plus an optional non-unique display `name`. Expired or exhausted links remain visible in an inactive section and may be reactivated by extending expiration or raising/removing the limit. Lowering a limit to the existing join count or below exhausts the link immediately. Soft-deleted links stay in Postgres with their join ledger but are hidden from ordinary reads and can never be used again.

Following an Invitation Link is auth-gated and automatically attempts the join after sign-in. The database locks the link, checks deleted/expired/exhausted state first, and only then checks membership: an active-link visit by an existing member redirects to the Hunt Overview without consuming a use, while any invalid link shows its appropriate error to everyone. A successful new membership and its ledger row commit atomically, preventing concurrent final-use requests from exceeding the limit. Acceptance assigns the first unused token from the ordered member palette (`moss`, `ochre`, `brick`, `olive`, `stone`, `plum`), wrapping from the start once every token is already in use in that Hunt; a member may later replace it with another token or any valid `#RRGGBB` color. Owner transfer is a single mutation; a hunt always has exactly one Owner.

### 9.2 Rubric System

The catalog defines what *can* be scored; the rubric defines what *is* scored and how. Fact Scope, acquisition-escalation policy, and conflict policy are Catalog truth, not Owner-editable Rubric settings. A Gate makes the Criterion decision-relevant for the Hunt and therefore upgrades its effective escalation policy to `decision_relevant`; removing the Gate restores the Catalog default.

Validation: every option `match` must satisfy the criterion's scoreable `value_schema` (backend-enforced; frontend renders widgets from the same schema — one schema, two validators, zero drift). Options are ordered; first match wins; overlapping scalar matches and overlapping array/set matches produce a save-time warning. `contains_any` means the extracted array intersects the configured set; `contains_all` means it contains every configured member. Exact array equality is deliberately absent from v1 because it turns harmless additional advertised types/materials into a mismatch. Enabling both objective `flooring_materials` and subjective `flooring_quality` produces a separate non-blocking warning that the two independent scores may double-weight flooring. `is_bonus` is backend-derived and true only when options are non-empty, every option delta and the unknown-delta are ≥ 0, and no option is a dealbreaker. A dealbreaker can never be presented as a bonus. Any rubric mutation bumps `hunts.rubric_version` and enqueues **one hunt-level `rescore` job** that fans out over the hunt's listings internally — atomic per rubric version, no LLM, effectively free. Scoring-affecting hunt-settings edits (`cost_estimate_mode`, `min_confidence`, `min_vision_confidence`, `generalized_vision_policy` — [§8.2](#82-tables)) take this same path. `cost_estimate_mode` defaults to `conservative`. `generalized_vision_policy` defaults to `full_rubric`: `full_rubric` lets generalized `vision:*` facts affect points and Gates, `points_only` lets them affect points while Gates see unknown, and `unknown` persists/displays them but excludes them from scoring. Exact Floor Plan VISION facts ignore the generalized policy, but low-confidence exact or generalized VISION facts never enter Gate values. Overrides and non-VISION facts are unaffected by the VISION-specific rules.

Custom criteria: name → description → **acquisition** (`extracted` or `manual`) → for an extracted Criterion, automatic routing classification (`requires_tool`) via one cheap LLM call and **user confirms or corrects the routing with one click** → chooses `property` or `floor_plan` Fact Scope and a boolean, numeric, or controlled-enum value schema → options defined like any catalog criterion. The definition is versioned JSON with an opaque stable key; changing its acquisition semantics (acquisition, description, route, scope, or value schema) mints a replacement key so prior Extractions cannot silently acquire a new meaning. Label and Rubric-option edits keep the key and remain a free re-score.

A **manual** custom Criterion has no producer: nothing extracts it, nothing refetches it, and its only value path is an ordinary Override written by a person on a Listing. Its `requires_tool` is therefore null, its `refresh_class` is the inert `manual` class, and no routing classification runs — there is nothing to route. It is scoped, schema'd, and scored exactly like any other Criterion: unanswered reads as unknown and takes `unknown_delta`, and — as for every Criterion — an unknown value cannot satisfy a non-negotiable, so a manual Gate fires on every Listing until someone answers it. Because the Override is the sole input, the API validates a manual Override's value against the definition's `value_schema` (an unvalidated value would score as "matched nothing" and be indistinguishable from a real answer).

P3-10 v1 confirms only page-text (`requires_tool = null`) and Maps routes for extracted Criteria; the `manual` acquisition adds a third, producer-free kind. Saving a manual Criterion enqueues the ordinary free re-score and **no** CUSTOM_MATCH backfill, because no cached evidence can answer it. Maps custom Criteria are Property-scoped; page-text Criteria may target a Property or Source-local Floor Plan. Saving a new or replacement custom Criterion immediately queues bounded `CUSTOM_MATCH → SCORE` work for every active Listing using cached current evidence, in addition to the ordinary free Rubric re-score. Vision and web-search custom routing remain classified but cannot be confirmed until their independent quality/security contracts are approved ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)).

### 9.3 Scoring Engine

Pure, deterministic, LLM-free, lives in `shared/`: `score(rubric, point_values, floor_plan, gate_values=point_values) → {total, breakdown}`. The separate Gate map is internal input only. The pinned score-breakdown field shapes are unchanged except `GateFiring` gains gate-pass `value` and `matched`; gated `criteria` semantics change from empty to informational (§20 2026-08-03).

1. *Effective value* per Criterion and Floor Plan: exact-target override ▸ all-units override ▸ exact current resolved Extraction ▸ applicable generalized current resolved Extraction ▸ unknown. True Property Criteria resolve only from the Property target. For boolean unit-presence Criteria, exact positive/negative evidence composes to `confirmed`/`none`; `all_units` applies to every current Floor Plan; `select_units` and `unit_scope_unspecified` compose only to `advertised_unconfirmed` unless an exact claim exists. Exact evidence for one Floor Plan never creates generalized evidence for its siblings. Typed scoped Criteria (`in_unit_laundry`, `parking`, `cooling`) instead compose a unique set: the union of applicable exact and explicit-all positive variants plus `advertised_unconfirmed` when an additional select/unspecified positive remains; confirmed `none` is exclusive and specificity wins. Their ordered singleton `contains_any` Rubric options preserve first-match scoring and uncertain evidence never satisfies a confirmed-feature Gate. `heating_type` uses the same claim mechanics; multiple confirmed heating variants cause conservative unknown-heating utility composition. `flooring_materials` remains the controlled-set exception: independently scoped exact and explicit-all claims union their material sets, while select/unspecified claims remain displayable evidence but contribute unknown to both point and Gate inputs for a concrete Floor Plan. Competing generalized claims use the same confidence/freshness rules as reconciliation but never manufacture exactness. Non-VISION Extractions below the Hunt's `min_confidence` setting are unknown; `vision:*` Extractions instead use `min_vision_confidence` (default `low`). Point-values and Gate-values are assembled separately: every low-confidence VISION fact is absent from Gate-values even when retained for points, so it cannot satisfy a non-negotiable or trigger/pass a value-bearing Gate as known evidence. Floor Plan columns remain the authoritative inputs for `beds`, `baths`, `sqft`, deposit, rent, and availability; `sqft` takes the conservative range end (`sqft_min` when present). Current values come only through the centralized contract in §8.2, never an ad-hoc latest-row query.
2. *Gate pass:* evaluate all non-negotiables and dealbreakers first. Any firing → `total = min(set_scores fired)`, breakdown records the gates with each gate's gate-pass `value` and first-matching `matched` option (null when unknown or no match). (Min: multiple gates must not average up.) A dealbreaker fires when the first-matching option carries a `dealbreaker_set_score`; a non-negotiable fires unless the value is known and its first-matching option is acceptable per the [§3 Gate definition](#3-glossary-and-domain-model).
3. *Delta pass:* start at 10; apply first-matching option's delta per enabled criterion; a known value matching no option contributes 0; unknown → `unknown_delta`. When gates fired, the delta pass still runs for informational `criteria` — those deltas do not affect `total`.
4. Clamp to [0, 15] (bonuses may exceed 10).

Match semantics (settled with the engine, P0-3; inclusive one-sided operators added v3.5): `range` is inclusive on both ends; `lt`/`lte`/`gt`/`gte`/`range` compare numbers with numbers or strings with strings (ISO dates order correctly as strings); `lte` and `gte` include equality; `contains_any`/`contains_all` use set membership as defined in §9.2; object values such as `management_reviews` `{rating, summary}` compare on their `rating` field; type mismatches never raise — they simply don't match.

The `breakdown` persisted with every score is a contract between the engine, its golden tests, and the detail-panel UI — its shape is fixed here:

```json
{ "base": 10, "total": 9.5, "rubric_version": 4, "clamped": false,
  "gates": [],
  "criteria": [
    { "key": "beds", "value": 2,
      "matched": {"op": "eq", "value": 2}, "delta": 0.5 },
    { "key": "in_unit_laundry", "value": null,
      "matched": null, "delta": -1.0, "unknown": true }
  ] }
```

When a gate fires, `gates` carries `{key, kind: "dealbreaker"|"non_negotiable", set_score, value, matched}`, `total` equals the minimum set-score, and `criteria` is populated with the same per-criterion rows as a normal score — informational only; the UI names the matched option in a gate alert and still shows the breakdown below.

```json
{ "base": 10, "total": 0, "rubric_version": 2, "clamped": false,
  "gates": [
    { "key": "pets_policy", "kind": "dealbreaker", "set_score": 0,
      "value": "none", "matched": {"op": "eq", "value": "none"} }
  ],
  "criteria": [
    { "key": "beds", "value": 2, "matched": {"op": "eq", "value": 2}, "delta": 0.5 },
    { "key": "pets_policy", "value": "none", "matched": {"op": "eq", "value": "none"}, "delta": 0 }
  ] }
```

Rationale for keeping gates rather than refusing to ingest mismatched listings: a dealbreaker-zeroed row preserves the "why we rejected it" record; a table filter (`hide score < N`) provides the decluttering without the data loss. Silent non-ingestion would confuse (URL in, nothing out).

### 9.4 Floor Plans and Unit Groups

Floor Plans are first-class; the table row is a derived Unit Group per `(beds, baths)`. The grouping is a compact navigation and comparison bucket, **not an assertion that its Floor Plans are equivalent**. Group-level presentation is limited to honest, explicitly labeled aggregates such as Floor Plan count, rent/sqft ranges, and the set-union of the group's Floor Plan `unit_types`. That union means “types represented among these plans,” not that every plan has every type. Plan-specific facts — score, all-in composition, deposit, Criterion values, and, once scoped Criteria land, amenities and other layout facts — come from exactly one **Display Floor Plan**. Outside filtering, the Display Floor Plan is the Hunt's valid manual per-Unit-Group pin when present, otherwise the highest-scoring Floor Plan with a known base rent; it falls back to the group's highest-scoring Floor Plan only when no scored plan has a known base rent. The row visibly identifies `Pinned: <plan>`, `Filter-selected Floor Plan: <plan>`, or `Preferred Floor Plan: <plan>` and shows a quiet `×N`/“varies by Floor Plan” affordance when the group contains alternatives; no union of sibling-plan amenities is presented as group truth.

**Each Floor Plan is scored independently; the Unit Group displays the Display Floor Plan's score, never a naked aggregate.** A stacked-layers indicator appears on the score cell whenever >1 scored plan exists, with per-plan breakdown in the detail panel. A per-hunt, **per-Unit-Group pin** on a specific plan switches that group's plan-specific display and score to the pinned plan (stored in `hunt_listings.pins`, keyed by the group). A pin stabilizes which choice the row represents across score/availability changes; without one, the highest-scoring plan with a known base rent is selected, falling back to the highest-scoring plan only when every scored plan lacks base rent; this selection may legitimately change after refresh or Rubric edits and the label changes with it. Within that rent-known set, on an **exact score tie only**, a plan with a known per-plan all-in total wins before stable Source order, so a Listing never hides a known cost behind an otherwise equivalent unpriced duplicate. This never borrows a sibling plan's figure or fabricates a total when no plan has one. Rationale: `min` lies about the unit you'd take; an optimistic union lies about every plan; a range with no per-plan scores makes comparison mushy; best-with-indicator is honest and decision-relevant.

Overview and Map filters preserve that same honesty boundary. Property/Unit-Group filters (name/address, city, beds/baths, Interest Status, visited) apply once to the row. Floor-Plan filters (score, rent, sqft, all-in cost, availability state/date, and Criterion-backed values) are evaluated against every current Floor Plan, and **one Floor Plan must satisfy every active Floor-Plan predicate** — sibling plans never combine their favorable facts into a match. For an unpinned Unit Group, any matching plan keeps the row visible and the highest-scoring matching plan becomes the ephemeral Filter-selected Floor Plan; if it is already the ordinary best-scoring plan, no extra marker is needed. Unknown values retain the §13.2 pass-through rule. A valid manual pin remains authoritative: the pinned plan alone decides whether the Unit Group is visible. When it fails but another Floor Plan matches, the row stays hidden and the filter bar separately counts that “N pinned Unit Groups may have another Floor Plan that meets these filters.” Changing or clearing filters recomputes/clears only the ephemeral selection; it never mutates or clears a manual pin. Compare is unaffected because filter selection is view state, not Hunt opinion.

Collaboration follows the same row identity without pretending a Unit Group is a stored entity. Member ratings are per Unit Group, keyed by the stable `"{beds}-{baths}"` key, so different layouts at one Property can receive different judgments. Comments default to Listing-wide because management, location, and shared amenities apply across layouts; an author may instead scope a new comment to one Unit Group. The UI labels scoped comments and marks an edited comment, while each Overview row counts its general comments plus comments scoped to that row.

Every Floor Plan in the alternatives list is clickable. It opens a nested desktop modal (full-screen on mobile) while the parent Listing draft remains mounted and shows that plan's fields, effective Criterion values, exact/generalized applicability and evidence, score/breakdown, pin state, Source provenance, and any associated Floor Plan diagrams. Deep links are deferred. Diagram association is Source-local and evidence-led: structured/native IDs, explicit cards, or unambiguous labels may link one image to one or several Floor Plans; ambiguity leaves the diagram in an unmatched Property gallery and never attaches it to every plan. Cross-Source Floor Plan matching is deferred.

Interest Status and `visited` use that same per-Listing Unit Group identity. They are shared Curator state, not per-member ratings and not global Property facts. Status is the current freely correctable disposition rather than an enforced transition workflow. The v1 vocabulary distinguishes application rejection, a hunter-declined offer, and a counterparty-rescinded offer instead of the ambiguous bare `rejected`. If simultaneous applications to multiple Floor Plans within one Unit Group become real, that demonstrated need triggers a separate Application/entity decision; the current contract does not pretend Unit Group state identifies an individual unit.

### 9.5 Utilities and All-In Monthly Cost

The criterion that matters most gets its own machinery.

*Extraction:* structured fields `utilities_included` (list: water, sewer, trash, gas, electric, cooling, heat, internet, cable), `mandatory_fees` (name+amount), `pet_costs`, `heating_type` (gas|electric|unknown). Fee language is exactly where listings get vague, so the evidence audit ([§10.5](#105-verification-agent)) applies in full. `pet_costs` is species-shaped (§20 2026-07-10): `cat_rent_monthly` / `dog_rent_monthly` when the page distinguishes species (pages often state only "cat rent $35, dog rent $50" — a bare "pet rent" field extracts nothing there), `pet_rent_monthly` when it doesn't; the generic figure applies to either species when the specific one is absent. Extracted pet rents land as `pet_rent_cat` / `pet_rent_dog` / `pet_rent` checklist slots (state `extracted`); a `manual` slot entry is never overwritten by extraction.

*Manual corrections:* the drawer shows **one Cost & fees section** ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-28 v3.25), not the former Cost Breakdown / Fees split. Every line in it is overrideable, so a read-only arithmetic panel beside an editable fee list only made the same charge appear twice. The section opens with two summary tiles (all-in monthly, cash at move-in), then three grouped ledgers — Monthly, Utilities, Move-in — sharing one row grammar: state dot · label · status icons · reason subline · amount · one action. The included-utilities summary keeps its three-level color (gray for none, `scoreHigh` for minor-only — sewer, trash, cooling, internet/cable — `scoreHighest` when any major utility is included) but rides the Utilities group header instead of occupying its own banner.

Presentation rules, chosen so the exceptions stay loud: qualifiers that are the normal case (mandatory, non-refundable, included in rent, credited, refundable) render as icons or plain typography rather than chips; a charge that is shown but not counted dims its row and strikes its figure; a human's figure carries a dotted underline instead of a "manual" chip; an unsaved draft edit is visibly provisional (dashed dot, hatched row) and never looks like a saved Override. Edit affordances appear on hover or keyboard focus, but a revert is always visible — an overridden or unsaved figure must never hide its way out. Chips and color are reserved for the states that can mislead: not counted, unknown, sources disagree, pending, and Incomplete.

Utilities remain the correction surface they were. Electric, gas, water, sewer, cooling, heat, and trash each have one row and an Override popover with an **Included in rent** checkbox plus a monthly amount where the cost model has an independent component. The amount may replace an extracted charge or regional estimate; blank keeps that source figure. Cooling has inclusion only because its cost is already inside the electric baseline. A revert appends that utility's all-null tombstone, so both inclusion and amount return to machine truth without changing any other utility. The effective inclusion list is the resolved Extraction plus active per-utility decisions; each save enqueues the ordinary free hunt-level rescore. Water/sewer and valet-trash checklist slots remain persistence inputs for extracted combined charges but do not render again as generic monthly-fee rows; their utility rows are the sole editing presentation, avoiding duplicate fee rows.

*Estimation fallback:* non-included utilities draw from `utility_baselines` — regional, state-qualified (zip-level rejected: more calls for precision a leasing-office quote overrides anyway). Baseline identity is `(geo_level, state, region_name)` where `geo_level` is `city | county | state`; for state rows `region_name = state`. SCORE selects exactly one scope from the Property's persisted locality — city+state when both present, else county+state when city is null, else state-only when city and county are null — and does **not** fall through to broader scopes when a city pass is missing or failed ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-23). 120-day TTL (self-healing, not churn-driven), populated by one scheduled structured LLM pass per selected region over utility-rate knowledge, with `sources` marked `"search": false`. A region is fresh only when **all 24 rows** (4 beds buckets × 6 utilities) exist and are within TTL. P3-5's provider-search seam is now available, but moving baselines onto it is a separate output-affecting change and remains unimplemented. `monthly_high` is the winter-weighted peak month — a Midwest January, not an annual average. Electric heat uses the electric-heat winter figure; unknown heating takes the worse of the two and flags it. `occupants` scales the per-person utilities (water/sewer) by `clamp(occupants / max(1,beds), 1.0, 2.0)` — the factor never drops below 1, so scaling only ever raises the estimate.

*Composition:* `all_in = rent + mandatory_fees + pet_monthly + Σ(estimated non-included utilities)`, every component tagged `actual | estimated | unknown`. County- and state-scoped estimates carry a display note naming the regional scope (e.g. `Wayne County, MI regional estimate`); city-scoped estimates need no extra note. `pet_monthly = cats × (cat_rent ?? generic) + dogs × (dog_rent ?? generic)` from the hunt's household settings ([§8.2](#82-tables)); a species with no stated rent contributes 0 and stays visible as an unfilled checklist slot — surfaced, never fabricated. **Defaults to conservative** (`monthly_high` for all estimated components): the tool must never make an apartment look cheaper than the worst realistic month. The hunt's `cost_estimate_mode` setting can relax to median. Fully unknown utilities → the criterion's `unknown_delta`, never a fabricated number, plus a "fees unverified" badge. *Composition v1 shipped 2026-07-10 (§20): rent + pet_monthly, ingest and rescore in lockstep (rescore reads slot amounts, so manual fee edits and household-settings edits both re-score); mandatory fees and utility estimation remain P3-9.* ***Full composition shipped 2026-07-18 (P3-9, [§20](#20-decision-log)):*** *mandatory fees (extracted block → checklist slots + `mandatory_fees` extraction; a billed water/sewer or valet-trash fee suppresses that utility's estimate — an actual beats a baseline, never double-count) and utility estimation via the graduated rule: a Property whose selected baseline scope has no rows yet composes the v1 slice (badge `utilities_not_estimated`); a scope with baselines but a missing needed row withholds the total — `unknown_delta` + `fees_unverified` badge, never a fabricated number. A page silent on utility inclusions estimates everything (conservative — only ever raises the figure) with the `fees_unverified` badge. A successful regional baselines write enqueues one hunt-level rescore per hunt with an active listing in that scope ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18, scope predicates refined 2026-07-23): a region's first listing always scores before its baselines exist — the pass is triggered by that very listing — so without the trigger it would wear `utilities_not_estimated` until an unrelated rescore.*

*One-time fees ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18):* move-in costs — application fees, admin fees, one-time pet deposits/fees — extract as their own `one_time_fees` block: `{name, amount, basis: per_application | per_person | per_pet | flat, refundable?}`. `refundable` is set only when the page says so. They **never compose into `all_in_monthly`** (all-in is a monthly figure); they persist as a `one_time_fees` extraction plus the one-time checklist slots, and the checklist shows an *estimated move-in fees* line for the hunt's household (`per_person × occupants`, `per_pet × (cats + dogs)`) — display arithmetic, excludes the security deposit (a floor-plan figure).

*Per-charge decisions ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-28 v3.25):* `fee_checklist` carries four nullable decisions beside the amount — `counted`, `required`, `refundable`, `credited_amount`. All are tri-state on purpose. `counted` NULL is the machine default (counted when an amount is known); an explicit `false` keeps the charge visible but out of `all_in_monthly`, and because that changes the scored figure it takes the ordinary free hunt-level rescore like any other correction. `required` and `refundable` NULL mean **no Source said**, which is exactly what drives the move-in Incomplete state; neither may ever be read as `false`. `credited_amount` is the portion applied to first month's rent, so only the remainder becomes cash at move-in. Base rent is overrideable too, as a Floor-Plan-scoped `base_rent` Override in the ordinary append-only ledger: a quoted rent corrects the plan it was quoted for, not every layout at the Property.

*Fees checklist:* standard fee slots in two groups (§20 2026-07-18) — **monthly** (water/sewer billing, valet trash, parking, pet rent — plus species slots `pet_rent_cat`/`pet_rent_dog`, §20 2026-07-10 — insurance program) which compose into all-in, and **one-time / move-in** (application fee, admin, pet deposit, one-time pet fee) which never compose — with states extracted / manual / unknown. Any permitted user ([§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions)) fills slots after a leasing-office call; manual entries carry a person-pencil icon + attribution on hover — unmistakable from agent-sourced values. Unknown slots keep contributing unknown, so an unfilled checklist never silently improves a score.

*Estimated move-in cost (P3-SC8):* required cash from application through occupancy for the selected Floor Plan, using one full realistic first month when the Source gives no more exact/prorated payment schedule:

`first_month_all_in + security_deposit + application/admin fees + one-time pet deposits/fees + other required one-time charges + explicitly required prepaid/last-month rent + the non-credited portion of holding deposits`.

`first_month_all_in` already includes rent, mandatory monthly fees, first-month pet rent, and utility estimates; none is added twice. Explicitly required move-in/community fees, key/fob/garage-device or parking deposits, utility connection deposits, and renters-insurance initiation charges also qualify. Optional charges do not. Refundable amounts still affect cash required and are included, but refundable and non-refundable subtotals display separately. A materially unknown required component yields a known subtotal plus an **Incomplete** badge and the `estimated_move_in_cost` Criterion remains unknown; the subtotal is never presented or scored as a conservative lower bound.

*Shipped 2026-07-28 ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.25).* The composer is worker-local beside the all-in composer (the shared engine stays domain-blind) and runs on both paths, SCORE and rescore, so the two never drift. Its output persists as `move_in_components` — per plan on `scores`, the display plan's copy on `hunt_listings` — the exact sibling of `all_in_components`, display metadata beside the pinned breakdown. `estimated_move_in_cost` is a Catalog Criterion with fact scope `composed`, never extracted, and overrideable like `all_in_monthly`. Household multipliers follow the stated `basis`: `per_person × occupants`, `per_pet × (cats + dogs)` — which is zero for a petless household, so pet deposits show but do not count, the same rule that keeps pet rent out of that household's all-in. Extracted move-in charges default to **required**, the conservative reading, and a human unticks the genuinely optional ones. Refundable, non-refundable and refundability-unstated amounts subtotal separately; the third bucket exists because folding unknown refundability into either would be a claim the Source never made.

### 9.6 Overrides and Provenance

Overrides display over Extractions with a badge; the original value, applicability, concrete target, evidence, Source, model, and timestamp remain one tap away in the detail panel. Overrides are append-only (history of who changed what); **the newest row per `(Listing, Criterion, concrete target)` wins, and a null-valued row is that target's revert tombstone** — "back to original" appends null and falls through to the resolved Extraction, never deleting history. A Floor Plan override corrects that plan only; an all-units override targets the Property with `all_units`. Human select/unspecified corrections remain deferred. `base_rent` and `estimated_move_in_cost` join `all_in_monthly` as composed-value Override keys ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-28 v3.25); `base_rent` is Floor-Plan scoped and replaces the advertised conservative rent at the head of the composition. `all_in_monthly` is overrideable like any Criterion: a live override beats the §9.5 composition (the scored value is the human's figure, the stored composition is marked `overridden`, and the UI shows the manual-entry icon in place of the estimated portion); reverting restores the composed figure. Manual fee-checklist entries revert the same way in spirit — back to the extraction-derived amount when one exists, else to unknown.

Utility corrections use the dedicated §8.2 `utility_overrides` ledger rather than this Criterion Override identity, because a utility is a component input rather than a scoreable fact. Its per-utility all-null tombstone has the same append-only provenance and revert semantics.

Every resolved Extraction records `resolution_rule`, `disputed`, and the complete candidate set through `extraction_resolution_candidates`; a suspicious score three weeks later is traceable without re-running anything. Available-sources-only disagreement uses the quieter **Sources disagree** presentation at the affected fact/Floor Plan, not the listing-level Problematic badge unless ordinary decision-relevant reconciliation also leaves a dispute.

### 9.7 Visits and the Visit Checklist

A **Visit** is a tour of a Property, recorded collaboratively on a phone while it happens and read on a desktop afterwards. It replaces `docs/apartment-tour-checklist.md` as something you operate rather than print. It is Hunt opinion throughout: no Visit datum becomes an Extraction, and none of it reaches the scoring engine.

**Four kinds of answer.** Whether an answer is shared is a property of the *item*, not of the checklist — the number of bathrooms is a fact, how the bathroom feels is not. **Facts** are objective and shared, one value, last write wins, quietly attributed. **Checks** are physical tests somebody performed, tri-state (untested / fine / problem), and marking one a problem promotes it into the defect log with its unit and originating item already attached, because a separate defect table is the part nobody fills in mid-tour. **Questions** are for the agent and cannot be marked asked without a typed answer — the answer *is* the checkmark, which incidentally yields a transcript of the leasing office's claims that can be compared against what Extraction found. **Impressions** are subjective and keyed per member; the group sees an aggregate and can open the split. Conflict behavior falls out of the kinds: Impressions cannot collide, Questions append a follow-up rather than overwrite, so only Facts and Checks can ever fork.

**Scope, and why a Visit spans units.** A Visit covers every unit walked on one occasion, because that is what a tour is: one agent, one conversation, one set of lease terms, several doors. Making Visits per-unit would not remove the complexity, it would relocate it — property-level answers would then need sharing *across* Visits, which is an ownership-and-merge problem of the kind [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution) already needed dispute checkpoints for, rather than a presentation problem. A single-unit tour is a free degenerate case of the spanning model; a multi-unit tour is not representable in the per-unit one.

The dangerous failure is therefore not a missing answer but an answer filed against the wrong unit, so checklist sections carry a **Visit Item Scope** mirroring [Fact Scope](#3-glossary-and-domain-model): `property`-scoped items are answered once per Visit, `unit`-scoped items once per Visit Unit, and the unit switcher exists **only** on the latter. Section 21 of the source document is genuinely mixed and splits accordingly: price/value, condition, size and layout, noise, and light are per unit, while felt safety, location and commute, management impression, and common areas are recorded once — you would answer those identically for every unit, and asking three times invites sloppy answers. Ambiguous items default to `property` per the glossary rule.

**Visit Units are labels, not inventory.** [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog) defers individual-unit numbers and individual-unit inventory, and that deferral stands. There is no advertised list of units to choose from — a Floor Plan is a marketed layout, not a physical unit, and a Unit Group is derived and unstored — so **every unit label is human-entered.** Ticking a Floor Plan in the create flow only pre-seeds a label to name on site. What §18 defers is the app *knowing* what units exist at a Property, from a Source, as fact; it does not prevent a member writing down which doors they walked through, any more than Interest Status pretends a Unit Group is stored. Consequently there is no "advertised unit versus custom unit" distinction to maintain: one *add a unit* control asks what you will call it and what it is — a known Floor Plan, or typed beds/baths that mint an ad-hoc grouping — which covers both an unadvertised unit inside a group and a unit outside every group.

**Local-first, and conflicts surfaced rather than resolved.** Basements and concrete cores lose signal, and losing twenty minutes of tour notes is the one failure that would end use of the feature, so writes are optimistic and local with a visible pending count; Realtime is an enhancement over local-first, never the storage path. Each queued write carries `prev_entry_id`, the row its author believed current. If the server has moved on by the time it flushes, the write still lands — nothing is discarded — and the fork becomes visible as both values with who and when; resolving appends a row whose `prev_entry_id` is the chosen one, collapsing the fork. Append-only storage is what makes that cheap. This deliberately reuses the **dispute checkpoint** idiom rather than introducing a second conflict vocabulary.

A fork requires **disagreement**, not merely a shared parent ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.33): two writes claiming the same row with the same answer are agreement, and asking a human to choose between a value and itself would teach people to dismiss the picker. The fork stays open until some row descends from one of its branches — which covers both endings, the picker choosing one and somebody simply answering the item again having seen the current value. A fork surfaces **one option per distinct answer**, and the losing branch is never removed.

**Roll-up to the Overview.** A Visit does not feed scoring ([§9.3](#93-scoring-engine) stays pure and deterministic); it appears *beside* the score. Overview remains one row per Unit Group, and its Unit column remains the group descriptor. A unit's score reaches its row through its Floor Plan, and: several units in one group show the **best** of them, the same shape as the [Display Floor Plan](#94-floor-plans-and-unit-groups) rule, marked with the existing `×N`-style affordance; the **same unit toured twice takes the latest Visit** before best-of applies, since a second showing is a correction while two units are two things to compare, and best-of across time would let a rosy first impression outrank the careful second look; and a described unit matching no advertised group gets **no row at all** rather than a minted Unit Group, staying visible on the Visit and in the drawer. The column is the team's mean of **unit-scope** Impressions only — building-scope figures are identical across every row of a Property and would compress the differences between rows without adding information.

**Fee Proposals.** A tour produces the best cost data in the system: figures a human confirmed aloud. They still do not write themselves. A confirmed figure surfaces on the Listing as a proposal carrying the same staged-override presentation an unsaved manual edit already uses, with accept and reject both immediately available; accepting performs the ordinary human write (`fee_checklist` upsert or `overrides` insert) so provenance markers keep working unchanged, and rejecting leaves the Visit's own record intact — what the agent said is still what the agent said.

Accepting writes the **same scope the drawer would** for the same edit ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.34), or the proposal path would quietly reach further than typing the figure by hand: a confirmed base rent corrects the Floor Plan of the unit that was walked ([§9.6](#96-overrides-and-provenance)), falling back to the Property only for a described unit that matched no advertised plan, while `all_in_monthly` stays Property-wide. The accepted value lands as `manual`, not a new "from a visit" provenance, because a person confirmed it and a person accepted it — minting a third state would fork every downstream marker. A proposal may only name a real fee slot or cost Criterion, and only one offer per destination is live at a time: re-confirming a figure updates the standing offer rather than asking the reader the same question twice with different numbers. Proposal rows are the single sanctioned exception to the one-icon action slot, because accept and reject are both primary there and neither may hide behind a hover; the pencil/revert slot stays single everywhere else.

---

## 10. Agent Pipeline

### 10.1 Job Lifecycle

```
RECEIVED → PLAN → VALIDATE_URL → FETCH → VALIDATE → EXTRACT → DEDUPE
        → DISCOVER → FETCH → VALIDATE → EXTRACT (×sibling source)
        → VERIFY (×source) → RECONCILE
        → IMAGE_FETCH → VISION → ENRICH (maps · reviews · safety · utilities)
        → CUSTOM_MATCH → SCORE
        → DONE | FAILED | CANCELLED
   (any stage may pause into WAITING_USER — a checkpoint — and resume)
```

Ordering note (§20 2026-07-12): DEDUPE consumes EXTRACT's `property_identity` block — a URL-only submission has no name/address until the submitted page is fetched and extracted — so the submitted source runs FETCH → VALIDATE → EXTRACT first, DEDUPE resolves canonical identity, and DISCOVER's sibling sources fan out behind it.

Escalation note (§20 2026-07-13): RECONCILE may extend a live run — unresolved decision-relevant fields append a bounded escalation round (official-site extraction, then one sibling round, [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)) to the plan manifest's stage list, persisted before advance like any transition, so an escalated run resumes mid-round.

Each stage is a function `(RunState) → RunState` that persists outputs *before* advancing `jobs.current_stage` — this single discipline yields resumability (NFR3), deploy-safety, and cheap checkpoints. Every transition writes a `job_events` row, which powers both the live Tasks view and the permanent History tab (FR10): plan manifest, per-stage timeline, checkpoint Q&A, tier escalations, errors, and actual cost.

Failure semantics: stage retries with backoff (`attempts`); a dead source is skipped and the run continues; hard failure only when zero sources are fetchable. Cancellation is a state flip checked between stages.

### 10.2 Implementation Patterns

"Agent" in this document is vocabulary, not architecture. Every stage resolves to one of four concrete patterns — knowing which one before implementing prevents the classic failure mode of building an autonomous loop where a single function call belongs. **These patterns govern workflow mode — the shipping baseline and critical path.** Agents mode ([§10.11](#1011-agents-mode-learning-track)) deliberately suspends P3's restraint as a flag-isolated learning exercise, judged by evals rather than by this section's rules.

| Pattern | What it is | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **P1 — Forced-schema call** | One LLM call whose output shape is compelled | VALIDATE, EXTRACT, CUSTOM_MATCH (text), VERIFY check 4, RECONCILE equivalence |
| **P2 — Deterministic code (+ optional tiny call)** | Plain Python; a model appears only for one narrow judgment | PLAN, VALIDATE_URL, IMAGE_FETCH, VERIFY checks 1–3, RECONCILE ladder, SCORE (no LLM at all) |
| **P3 — Bounded tool loop** | Model iteratively calls tools until it answers or hits a turn cap | DISCOVER; location-type custom criteria in ENRICH — **nothing else** |
| **P4 — Vision call** | P1 with images as input content blocks | VISION |

**P1 — Forced-schema call.** Define one tool whose `input_schema` *is* the desired output schema, and force the model to use it (`tool_choice` pinned to that tool) — the model cannot answer except by emitting schema-shaped JSON. No prose parsing, no "respond in JSON" prompting. EXTRACT's schema is generated at runtime from `criteria_catalog`: Property claims and sparse unit-feature claims carry value, confidence, evidence, applicability, and response-local Floor Plan references, alongside the explicit Floor Plan/identity blocks. A missing sparse claim means “not stated,” never false. Typed scoped fields may emit one claim per distinct positive value; boolean fields remain one value per concrete target. `specific_floor_plans` requires at least one valid response-local key. If the model supplies no valid key, the schema boundary reduces specificity to `select_units` only for explicit select/some/certain wording and otherwise to `unit_scope_unspecified`; it never invents a ref or promotes evidence to `all_units`. `unit_types` remains a Catalog Criterion but is derived from the canonical `floor_plans[].unit_types` value, never emitted as a redundant top-level claim. The current one-call schema's 42-field acceptance is an empirical result for the 2026-08-02 `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` / OpenRouter forced-tool request; it is neither a Google-wide limit nor a field-only contract. Its nested shape, supported keywords, model, and adapter all matter, so re-run the live boundary smoke test whenever any of those changes. The Catalog remains the source of truth and the schema is never hand-maintained; target/applicability composition and Source-local ID expansion happen deterministically after the call. On validation failure: retry once with the validation error appended to the conversation; a second failure is a Job error, not another retry. Provider usage fields from every call feed `jobs.cost_actual_usd`.

**P2 — Deterministic code.** VERIFY is the exemplar and the discipline to hold: the evidence audit is a fuzzy substring match (e.g., `rapidfuzz.partial_ratio(evidence_quote, cleaned_text) > threshold`), plausibility is comparisons against the band table, conformance is Pydantic — roughly 150 lines of plain Python wrapping exactly one cheap P1 call (cross-field contradictions). Resist making P2 stages "smarter"; their testability and determinism are the point.

**P3 — Bounded tool loop.** The canonical shape — small enough to specify exactly, and worth specifying because unbounded variants are how agent systems fail:

```
messages = [user_task]
for turn in range(MAX_TURNS):                # budget is not optional
    r = llm(messages, tools)
    if r.stop_reason != "tool_use": return r
    for call in r.tool_calls:
        result = REGISTRY[call.name](**call.input)   # execute
        log_job_event(call, result)                  # observability
    messages += [r.assistant_turn, tool_results]
raise AgentBudgetExceeded                     # a real outcome, handled by the stage
```

Every tool invocation and result is a `job_events` row — this is how the Tasks history later shows "searched X, fetched Y, judged Z." Provider-hosted search is recorded through the same event sink. OpenRouter may omit its native-search count and citations from the OpenAI-compatible response; when that happens, a DISCOVER turn that was offered the required search tool records one opaque search event, while OpenRouter's reported total cost remains authoritative.

**Tools.** A custom tool is an async Python function in a name→callable registry; a small `@tool` decorator derives its JSON schema from the signature and docstring, so registry and schemas cannot drift. Planned custom tools: `geocode`, `places_nearby`, `commute_time`, and `fetch_page` — the latter routes through the tier ladder ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)), so any tool-using agent inherits the adapter registry, rate limits, and politeness for free rather than fetching on its own. DISCOVER uses OpenRouter's native `openrouter:web_search` server tool (maximum three searches and 12 total results per model turn; $0.01/search at landing), which executes provider-side with no local handler. Tools available to a stage are explicitly allow-listed per stage; extraction stages get **none** — that zero-tool property is a security control ([§16](#16-security-and-privacy)), not an omission.

**The runner.** Stages are async functions `(RunState) → RunState` over one Pydantic state object; the runner walks the **plan manifest's stage list** by name from a resume cursor (§20 2026-07-12 — a state with no manifest, i.e. any pre-Phase-3 snapshot, resumes through the legacy fixed list, selected at the dispatcher so an old cursor is never reinterpreted against a longer list), and the single load-bearing rule is **persist before advance**:

```
for name in plan.stages[state.cursor:]:   # legacy fixed list when plan is None
    state = await REGISTRY[name](state)
    persist(state, job)        # ← resumability, deploy-safety, checkpoints
    state.cursor += 1
```

The Phase 0 CLI and the Phase 1+ queue worker are two entry points calling the same `run_job` — the CLI is not throwaway work. Before dispatching a manifest stage, the runner checks `plan.skipped`; a skipped stage advances durably without calling its implementation or opening a model cost tally.

**The client seam.** No pipeline code imports a provider SDK directly. Everything goes through the thin client ([§11.1](#111-provider-seam)) exposing `call_structured(stage, schema, content)`, `call_agent(stage, task, tools)`, and `call_vision(stage, schema, images)` — the one place where prompt caching, per-stage model pinning, cost accounting, and test-fixture record/replay live.

### 10.3 Stage Catalog

| Stage | Purpose | Model | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| PLAN | Build run manifest: stages to run, sources to (re)fetch, cache/TTL decisions, cost estimate | tiny LLM assist; mostly deterministic | Manifest persisted as `jobs.plan`. A manifest builder, **not** an open-ended agent — that restraint is deliberate ([§20](#20-decision-log)) |
| VALIDATE_URL | Is the submitted URL worth fetching at all? | none | Deterministic (v2.4): scheme, public host (private/loopback refused — §16), not a binary; normalizes the URL (whitespace/fragments) before anything else sees it |
| VALIDATE | Is this a rental listing page? | small | Runs on the **fetched, cleaned text** of the submitted source — after FETCH (v2.4). Heuristics first (no listing signal → rejected at zero LLM spend), LLM to confirm |
| DEDUPE | Name+address → geocode → match `properties` | small | <100 m AND name-similar → merge; gray zone → checkpoint |
| DISCOVER | Find + store the official site (link only — displayed, not fetched by default); build the tier-diverse sibling slate + ranked candidate pool | judgment-tier | Bounded to six turns and three native searches; exact same-Property filtering precedes one sibling per permitted census tier, each from a distinct syndication family; escalation draws later sources from the pool ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)); skipped entirely under `trust_link`, tier-capped by the run's Source Policy ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)) |
| FETCH | Tier ladder per adapter registry | none | [§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem) |
| EXTRACT | Full-Catalog structured extraction from cleaned text | workhorse | Schema generated from `criteria_catalog`; Property claims are explicit and unit claims are sparse with applicability + response-local Floor Plan references (§10.2). Non-Catalog blocks in the same call: `floor_plans` and `property_identity` (name/address/official URL → `properties` row at persist, §20 2026-07-10). Immediate-availability phrasing ("Available Now" / "Now" / "Immediately") extracts as transient sentinel `available_now` **only when the target Property/Floor Plan has no explicit availability date** (prose or `[EMBEDDED DATA]`); EXTRACT rewrites the sentinel via `ctx.today()` before VERIFY/persist so scored values remain ISO dates only — live ingest uses wall-clock today; the bench freezes `today` to that page's corpus `meta.json.saved_at` (§20 2026-07-16) |
| VERIFY | Per-source audit | workhorse | [§10.5](#105-verification-agent) |
| RECONCILE | Merge sources via resolution ladder; owns the bounded escalation ladder | workhorse (equivalence only) | [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution) |
| IMAGE_FETCH | Discover/download listing images, normalize to content-addressed WebP, compare the complete hash set | none | Runs after DEDUPE so paths use the canonical Property; writes `plan.skipped.VISION` when unchanged/unusable |
| VISION | Kitchen modernness, floor/carpet quality from images | vision-tier | Prompt anchored with reference images from real listings Yusuf has rated; reference set versioned with the prompt |
| ENRICH | Maps distances/commutes, ratings per the [§10.12](#1012-ratings-sources) source ladder (Places first), safety placeholder (grades via override until the [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog) safety module — [§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18), utility baseline application | small + APIs | Safety data is knowingly weak — do not over-invest ([§17](#17-risks-and-concerns-register)) |
| CUSTOM_MATCH | Match hunt custom criteria per `requires_tool` routing | small | |
| SCORE | Deterministic engine, per floor plan | none | |

### 10.4 Planner

Deterministic-first: TTL lookups, hash checks, refresh-scope resolution, and adapter-registry reads are plain code; a single small-model call handles genuine judgment when DISCOVER returns more than three same-Property candidates, ranking them by trustworthiness before the deterministic tier/family slate builder runs (§20 2026-07-21). Output is an explicit manifest enabling three things: Tasks UI shows planned-vs-completed, debugging starts by reading the plan, and cost is estimated before spend. Its shape:

```json
{ "job_type": "refresh", "trigger": "ttl:rent_expired",
  "source_policy": "tiers_1_2_3",
  "sources": [
    { "source_id": "…", "action": "fetch", "tier": 1 },
    { "source_id": "…", "action": "skip", "why": "hash_fresh" } ],
  "stages": ["FETCH", "EXTRACT", "VERIFY", "RECONCILE", "SCORE"],
  "skipped": { "VISION": "images_unchanged", "ENRICH": "location_immutable" },
  "est_cost_usd": 0.03 }
```

RECONCILE's escalation ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)) may append stages to `plan.stages` mid-run; the round is recorded under an optional `plan.escalation` key (trigger fields, sources added, rung reached) so the manifest remains the single stage list the runner walks and the Tasks UI shows exactly what the run bought and why (§20 2026-07-13).

### 10.5 Verification Agent

Runs per Source after extraction. Four checks: **(1) Evidence audit** — every value and asserted applicability must be supported by its `evidence_quote`; every fragment must fuzzy-match text actually present in the fetched page. Unlocatable evidence demotes to `low`. Quotes may stitch up to a few verbatim fragments with an ellipsis (models do this for facts stated in two places, and for `[EMBEDDED DATA]` JSON); each fragment must locate independently, with a whitespace-stripped retry so compact-JSON spacing never demotes (§20 2026-07-10 — a bounded loosening: characters must still be present on the page, so the guarantee "fact absent → demoted" holds). **An amenity mention alone cannot verify a Floor Plan association**; exact mapping requires Source-local native/card/label evidence. Prefer a missing exact association over a wrong one. This is simultaneously the anti-hallucination and primary anti-prompt-injection control ([§16](#16-security-and-privacy)). **(2) Schema conformance** re-checked post-parse, including Fact Scope/target/applicability combinations and same-Property Floor Plan identity. **(3) Plausibility rules** — data-driven bands, not LLM vibes: rent percentile band for the metro, sqft/bed ratio, deposit ≤ ~2× rent, availability not in the past. Bands are **self-derived** from the metro's accumulated listings (rolling percentiles per beds bucket); cold start (<~8 listings in metro) routes out-of-band values to a checkpoint whose confirmed answers seed the band. **(4) Cross-field consistency** — e.g., exact "in-unit laundry" against only a Property-level "laundry facility on site" claim. Failures demote confidence; Gate-relevant Criteria escalate to a `confirm_value` checkpoint instead of silently scoring on suspect data.

### 10.6 Conflict Resolution

RECONCILE receives one verified extraction set per **slate** source ([§3](#3-glossary-and-domain-model), [§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)): the submitted URL — a permanent slate member, always fetched and extracted, never substituted out; its extraction rows are append-only provenance, though the *reconciled* value may still disagree with it — plus one sibling per permitted census fetch tier, each from a distinct syndication family. DISCOVER also finds the **official site**, which is stored (`property_sources.is_official`) and displayed among the listing's sources but **not fetched by default** (§20 2026-07-13, reversing the 2026-06-28 "official beats aggregators" posture): official pages are the least standardized extraction surface, so they enter the ladder as escalation's arbiter rather than as baseline spend. A one-member slate (`trust_link`, or DISCOVER found no siblings) short-circuits the ladder — every field resolves `single_source` and the listing carries the single-sourced badge with the reason recorded.

**Votes are counted per syndication family**: sources sharing an upstream feed collapse to one vote (the freshest fetch speaks for its family), because three aggregators echoing one feed are one observation, not a majority.

Each Catalog Criterion declares an acquisition `escalation_policy` and a `conflict_policy`. `decision_relevant` may buy the bounded escalation ladder; `available_sources_only` never fetches a page solely for that Criterion, though every Source already fetched for another reason still extracts the full Catalog. A Criterion becomes decision-relevant in a Hunt when it (a) bears a Gate, (b) has any option with `delta < 0` or an `unknown_delta < 0`, or (c) is an all-in cost component. A Gate always upgrades effective escalation to `decision_relevant`. Property and exact Floor Plan targets reconcile independently; generalized unit applicability never votes itself into an exact association.

Deterministic ladder, evaluated top-down per criterion; the LLM's only role is semantic equivalence normalization before comparison ("W/D in unit" ≡ "in-unit laundry"):

1. Manual override always wins (outside the pipeline entirely).
2. Numeric tolerance collapse: within 3% (rent) / 5% (sqft) = agreement; take the conservative figure in the band (never look cheaper, [§2.3](#23-goals)).
3. Supermajority: ≥2/3 of family-deduped votes agree (a unanimous two-vote slate counts) → take it.
4. *Escalation, decision-relevant fields only:* fetch + extract the official site (once per job, at the tier the Source Policy permits — [§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)); an official value at medium-or-better verified confidence settles the field. The arbiter rung — and the highest-yield single fetch for missing values, since fees and specials often exist only there.
5. *Escalation, decision-relevant fields only:* one sibling round — up to 3 new sources from DISCOVER's ranked candidate pool, tier-diverse and family-distinct from everything already used (a tier with no remaining candidate substitutes from the next cheaper tier); rungs 2–3 re-run over the widened vote set. Round sources extract the full catalog (append-only, globally reusable) but **vote only on fields still unresolved** — settled fields are never reopened within a run.
6. Majority: >50% of family-deduped votes; ties break by higher verification confidence, then fresher fetch.
7. Still contradictory under `standard_ladder` → conservative value stored marked `disputed`, all candidates retained through the provenance join, confidence low; if the Criterion is Gate-bearing in any Hunt using the Property → `resolve_dispute` checkpoint with one-click pick-the-value resolution. Such a decision-relevant dispute derives the listing-level **Problematic** badge ([§13.2](#132-key-views-mantine-mapping)). Under `verified_positive_preferred`, one medium-or-better verified positive may establish presence against missing or negative available-source claims, but the resolution remains `disputed` and the UI says **Sources disagree** quietly; it does not trigger more fetches under `available_sources_only`. An explicit authoritative negative may still defeat the positive. This policy is for advertised amenity presence, never prices, fees, dates, or Gate semantics.

The winning rule is stored on the extraction (`resolution_rule`). Escalation is bounded — at most one official fetch and one sibling round per job — and appends its stages to the plan manifest (persist-before-advance, so escalated runs resume mid-round and the Tasks UI shows the round, [§10.4](#104-planner)); worst case is ~7 extractions against a ≤3-extraction baseline ([§15](#15-cost-model-and-optimization)).

### 10.7 Fetching Subsystem

**Per-domain adapter registry + three-tier ladder.** The registry records what each domain requires; the ladder is climbed once per domain, not once per fetch.

- **Tier 1 — httpx:** plain HTTP with sane headers. Covers most official complex sites and the tier-1 aggregators that anchor the slate's cheap end.
- **Tier 2 — Playwright:** realistic-fingerprint headless browser for JS-rendered but unprotected pages. Slow, polite, per-domain rate-limited. The fetcher retains an opt-in screenshot capability, but the shipping workflow does not request or persist captures (§18).
- **Tier 3 — managed unblocker:** a swappable provider adapter (Bright Data Web Unlocker default; ScrapingBee alternate) for hostile aggregators, **free plans only** — paid escalation requires a new [§20](#20-decision-log) decision, not an env change ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-07). Fallback, never default: the ladder only reaches tier 3 after tiers 1–2 classify shell/blocked, and tier 3 stays off the ladder entirely until a provider key is configured. Switching provider is one env var (`MANZIL_TIER3_PROVIDER`); adding one is a single adapter entry in `fetching/tier3.py`. The Apify structured-actor variant (aggregator actors returning parsed JSON, bypassing FETCH+EXTRACT with their schema mapped onto ours) remains **deferred** — it is a second data path, not a fetcher, and needs its own decision. When every tier fails, FETCH's error retains a deterministic URL-slug search hint for operator diagnostics; DISCOVER now supplies the automatic sibling-source path.

**Source Policy (cross-check control).** Every submission carries a Source Policy governing how aggressively the pipeline corroborates the submitted link against other sites. The default is full cross-check — the behavior described everywhere else in this document. The options:

| Policy | DISCOVER | Sibling-source tier cap |
|---|---|---|
| `trust_link` | skipped | — (no siblings; single-source run) |
| `tier_1` | runs | siblings limited to Tier 1 domains |
| `tiers_1_2` | runs | siblings limited to Tiers 1–2 |
| `tiers_1_2_3` **(default)** | runs | no cap — current full behavior |
| `tier_1_plus_official` | runs | sibling aggregators limited to Tier 1; the official complex site — fetched only when escalation calls for it ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)) — allowed at whatever tier it requires |

Semantics, precisely: the policy constrains **discovered sibling sources only — never the submitted URL**, which is always fetched at its registry-required tier (the user brought it; refusing it would make the submission meaningless). "Tier" here is the domain's required fetch tier per the adapter registry — a cost/hostility cap, not a source-quality ranking. A sibling whose domain requires a tier above the cap is not fetched-and-escalated; it is skipped at plan time and recorded in the manifest (`"action": "skip", "why": "policy_tier_cap"`). A sibling that unexpectedly classifies `shell`/`blocked` at the cap is likewise skipped rather than escalated, and the registry still records the outcome so the ladder's self-tuning is unaffected. The §15 source-count discipline (tier-diverse baseline slate; escalation only for unresolved decision-relevant fields — [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)) applies *beneath* the cap — policy is an upper bound, not a fetch mandate. A tier above the cap contributes no slate slot; the tier-3 slot additionally requires a configured provider key (the [§19](#19-implementation-phases) census gate) — a missing tier substitutes from the next cheaper one, recorded in the manifest. The official site is never baseline spend ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)); when escalation calls for it, it is fetched at whatever tier it requires under the default and `tier_1_plus_official` policies, and obeys the cap under `tier_1`/`tiers_1_2`.

The policy is selected at submit time (FR3), defaulting from `hunts.settings.default_source_policy` (itself defaulting to `tiers_1_2_3`), persisted on the Listing (`hunt_listings.source_policy`) so refresh Jobs honor the same constraint, and recorded in the plan manifest ([§10.4](#104-planner)). Owner or the Listing submitter may change it later. The atomic database capability updates the policy and enqueues a `refresh` Job with `scope: discover` only when the sibling tier cap increases, including leaving `trust_link`. Enabling only `tier_1_plus_official`'s later arbiter-fetch carve-out is metadata-only because DISCOVER already finds the official link under every non-trust policy; P3-6 will decide whether unresolved facts warrant fetching it. Tightening likewise changes metadata without a Job; already-known Source links remain append-only and are merely excluded by future plans. Under `trust_link` the run is single-Source and the Listing carries a quiet **"single source — not cross-checked"** badge. `discover_exhausted` means a completed search found no usable sibling; `discover_failed` means the bounded loop exhausted its budget or could not return a usable result. The latter is intentionally not mislabeled as evidence that no sibling exists. VERIFY and the deterministic truth layer are untouched in every policy — Source Policy narrows *which pages feed the pipeline*, never *what counts as correct*.

**Tier-detection heuristics (fetch outcome classification).** Every fetch is classified `success | shell | blocked | not_listing | error` by layered checks, cheapest first. *HTTP-layer signals:* status 403/429/503, challenge headers and cookies (Cloudflare `cf-chl`/`cf_clearance` flows, PerimeterX `_px` cookies), redirects to captcha paths. *Challenge fingerprints in the body:* "Pardon Our Interruption", "Verify you are human", "Enable JavaScript and cookies to continue", `cf-challenge` markup. *Positive content signals:* cleaned text — which includes the mined `[EMBEDDED DATA]` digest ([§20](#20-decision-log) v2.6) — above a length threshold **and** at least one of: a currency amount pattern, bed/bath tokens, an address fragment, or rental-fact JSON keys (`"priceLow":`, `"bedCount":` — state blobs carry prices as bare numbers); JSON-LD `schema.org` blocks (`ApartmentComplex`, `RealEstateListing`, `Offer`) are a strong positive that also short-circuits doubt. *Negative body signals, checked only after the positives fail:* a body that is nearly all script tags (the JS-shell signature), rendered text under a size floor (~5 KB). Positive content deliberately outranks the JS-shell heuristic: a script-heavy page whose state blob carries the listing's floor plans is a usable page, not a shell — this is what lets tier 1 settle domains (zumper, padmapper) that render an empty DOM but server-ship their data as JSON. Routing: `shell` → escalate one tier; `blocked` → escalate and record in the registry; `not_listing` → the VALIDATE verdict (surface to user); `success` → clean, hash, proceed. Outcomes update `fetch_adapter_registry` so the ladder self-tunes: a previously easy domain that starts failing simply resumes climbing from the next tier.

### 10.8 Vision

IMAGE_FETCH preserves Source/page order and image context (alt/title/caption, containing Floor Plan card, native plan ID, nearby label, discovery mechanism), downloads through the SSRF guard, normalizes to ≤1024 px WebP, stores up to 30 exact-hash-distinct images source-balanced, and computes a deterministic 64-bit difference hash. Unambiguous Source-local context may associate an ordinary photo or diagram to a Floor Plan; visual similarity and cross-Source matching may not.

`IMAGE_CLASSIFY` follows IMAGE_FETCH and is now **ONNX-only** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.52). It generates unstored ≤384 px WebP thumbnails for up to `MAX_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_IMAGES = 30` and sends them to the pinned 97 MB unsigned-int8, vision-only OpenAI CLIP ViT-B/32 ONNX export in a short-lived, single-thread CPU subprocess. The complete artifact digest is verified before inference. Per hash the canonical narrow result is the highest-probability MIT Indoor scene, `kitchen_score` plus its calibrated threshold verdict, and `diagram_score` plus its calibrated threshold verdict. It persists under versioned `property_images.vision_assessment.classification` with backend, artifact digest, thresholds, and assessment; a pre-promotion `classification_shadow` record is promoted on refresh and a replaced LLM record is retained as `classification_llm_legacy` for audit. Detailed mechanics and operation: [`docs/onnx-image-classification.md`](docs/onnx-image-classification.md).

The former structured LLM classifier is disabled on the workflow path but its code and reconciliation tests remain for rollback/reference. `IMAGE_CLASSIFY` never calls the LLM seam and never silently falls back to it.
