Missing configuration, artifact/inference failure, or a response whose hashes do not exactly equal the request fails the Stage and follows the ordinary Job retry policy. `MANZIL_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ONNX_DIR` is the canonical artifact setting; the old `MANZIL_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ONNX_SHADOW_DIR` is a bounded compatibility alias, not a second mode. The former Gemini pin and tolerant generated-batch contract are historical only.

This promotion deliberately **narrows** rather than fabricates the missing LLM fields. ONNX does not claim kitchen/flooring/bathroom assessability, framing/usability, irrelevant-asset status, or calibrated general confidence. Deterministic `kind = other` and `kind = floor_plan_diagram` remain categorical exclusions, as does canonical ONNX `diagram_predicted`; ONNX may promote a visually detected diagram but never demotes deterministic diagram evidence.

Kitchen target selection ranks every remaining canonical ONNX-classified `listing_photo` by descending `kitchen_score`, with Source/page order then content hash as deterministic tie-breakers, and takes at most `VISION_TARGET_QUOTAS["kitchen_quality"] = 3`. It does not require the kitchen threshold to pass: the anchored quality VISION call remains responsible for returning `not_visible`, so a low-probability gallery yields honest rejection rather than invented assessability or random choice. The selector no longer clusters near-duplicates or prioritizes exact Floor Plan coverage/framing; this is explicit Owner-accepted target-set debt. The total quality cap remains `MAX_VISION_TARGET_IMAGES = 8`; future intended allocation is kitchen 3, flooring 3, bathroom 2.

Kitchen quality uses one call containing five deterministic 1024×1024 rating sheets plus at most three full targets. Each sheet is a 3×2 grid containing five contain-fitted normalized anchors and one neutral blank cell; files and membership are hashed. The criterion-specific manifest permits 3–5 anchors per level, makes source URLs optional, and can approve kitchen while flooring remains absent. The call returns per-target rating, confidence, visibility, and short visual rationale; unknown/duplicate hashes and ratings for `not_visible` targets are rejected. The Drawer gallery exposes each selected image's assessment and rationale on demand as image-specific provenance, never as a Property-wide claim.

Aggregation is a deterministic weighted median: high confidence weighs 2, medium 1, low/not-visible is excluded, and an exact half-weight tie chooses the lower rating. One high assessment yields medium aggregate confidence; two or more within one star yield high when at least one is high; a two-star spread yields medium; a spread greater than two emits no scored aggregate. VISION writes a generalized Property-target `kitchen_quality` Extraction with `unit_scope_unspecified` and one exact Floor Plan Extraction per reliably represented plan. Exact values win through §9.3; if exact is absent/below threshold, the gallery estimate is the fallback. Resolution rules are `vision_weighted_median_exact` and `vision_weighted_median_gallery`, with contributing assets linked through `extraction_images`.

Classification caches independently. Quality reruns only when the selected-target digest, exact associations, quality prompt/model, or reference version changes; a new irrelevant image causes classification only. **Strict-complete** IMAGE_FETCH (every admitted candidate downloaded or skipped by cap) may append not-found replacements for unsupported prior visual facts and retire Source-local diagram associations that disappeared. **Cap-saturated partial** work (photo gallery full, some lower-priority candidates failed) advances the `images` freshness marker and may retire stale photos from refreshed Sources, but diagram unlink and vision tombstones are withheld until a strict-complete pass. **Underfilled partial** work is additive only and retires nothing. Kitchen quality is enabled with reference profile v1, VISION prompt v1, and `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6` by the Owner's 2026-07-28 benchmark override. The external quality benchmark remains required follow-up evidence but is not a runtime gate. Flooring and bathroom remain disabled until their own Catalog decision, references, bench, quota activation, and Decision Log entry. Detailed procedure: [`docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md`](docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md).

### 10.9 Custom Criteria Routing

`acquisition` and `requires_tool` are fixed and human-confirmed at authoring time ([§9.2](#92-rubric-system)); at run time CUSTOM_MATCH simply dispatches on them. A `manual` Criterion is filtered out before dispatch and never reaches the stage at all — it has no producer, so there is no route, no turn budget, and no warning to emit. For the remaining `extracted` Criteria: `null` → one zero-tool, forced-schema match per custom Criterion over the bounded current Source slate; `maps` → the bounded location tool loop, with only Maps tools offered. Page silence is unknown, never an invented negative. Custom candidates/resolutions carry the Hunt ID and therefore never become global Property truth. No runtime routing intelligence exists or should be added.

The classifier may suggest `vision` or `web_search` so a misroute is visible rather than coerced into text, but P3-10 v1 refuses confirmation of either. Arbitrary visual Criteria lack the anchored reference/evaluation contract that makes [§10.8](#108-vision) safe, and workflow-mode web search is confined to DISCOVER; both routes remain [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)-deferred.

### 10.10 User Checkpoints

Any stage may pause into `WAITING_USER` with a structured prompt `{kind, question, options: [yes, no, other:<input>], default, context_ref}` persisted on the job; the Tasks UI renders the buttons with a compact context panel containing the extracted value, evidence quote, and Source link when those exist. The answer lands in `jobs.payload` and the stage resumes with it. Answerable by the listing's submitter, any Curator, or the Owner. Full-page checkpoint screenshots are deferred (§18): they are only available on browser-tier fetches and, without evidence coordinates, make the disputed value harder rather than easier to locate.

**Unanswered checkpoints auto-resume after 24 hours** with the prompt's declared default. Auto-resolution writes a permanent `checkpoint_auto_resolved` Job event and places an indicator badge (clock glyph) on the Listing's Overview rows. The pre-default RunState snapshot is retained on the immutable original Job. The checkpoint can be reopened from the Listing drawer; a late answer creates a new correction Job from that exact Stage boundary, leaving the original History intact, and the correction continues through SCORE. Checkpoint kinds in use: `confirm_value` (default `yes`, accepting the extracted value at the Hunt's minimum confidence), `resolve_dedupe` (default `keep_separate`), and `resolve_dispute` (default `Leave unknown`, per §20 v3.21). All three participate in the timeout sweep.

### 10.11 Agents Mode (Learning Track)

The pipeline has **two implementations behind one flag** (`--mode=workflow | agents`). They share the RunState/persistence contract, stage semantics, and — non-negotiably — the **deterministic truth layer**: VERIFY checks 1–3, the RECONCILE ladder, and SCORE run identically in both. The invariant in one line: *agents mode may change who does the work, never what counts as correct.* All agents-mode code lives in `worker/src/manzil_worker/agents/` and imports the truth layer; nothing on the critical path imports back.

The named architectures it implements, each chosen because it fits Manzil naturally rather than being bolted on:

- **Orchestrator–workers** (cross-source extraction): an orchestrator receives the URL, decides which sibling sources to check, spawns a parallel extraction worker per source — each with its own context window and the fetch-ladder tools — and hands the structured briefs to the reconciler. Genuinely parallel work, so the benefit observed is real, not simulated.
- **Extractor + critic** (proposer–critic): a critic receives the raw page and the extracted record and hunts for discrepancies before anything is written. The eval question is precise: does the critic catch errors that deterministic VERIFY misses, and at what token cost? Keep or kill on that data.
- **LangGraph graph + Postgres checkpointer**: agents mode implements the pipeline as a LangGraph graph; §10.10's checkpoints map exactly onto interrupt → persist → resume — the strongest natural fit in the whole track, since the design converged on that model before the framework entered the picture. Comparing it against the hand-rolled runner is itself a lesson in what frameworks buy.
- **Explicit triage routing**: a classifier step that grades task difficulty and dispatches the model tier, turning §11.2's implicit workhorse/judgment split into a named, built architecture.
- **Deep-dive investigator** (FR12): a spawnable crew — one worker on reviews and management-company reputation, one on scam signals and cross-listing price checks, one on the area — synthesized by an orchestrator into a brief. New `job_type: investigate`; entirely off the critical path, so its failure modes break nothing.

**Evaluation is the point.** Both modes run against the bench set; the harness compares accuracy, tokens, and latency, and the honest expectation going in is a 5–15× token multiplier for agents mode. Adoption rule: an agents-mode component becomes part of the default path only with eval evidence plus a decision-log entry — the same standard as any design change. The track's final deliverable is a written eval report in `docs/` stating what multi-agent bought and what it cost.

### 10.12 Ratings Sources

Property reputation scores through one criterion — `management_reviews` (§8.2: rating 1–5 + summary) — but its *sources* arrive as a deliberate ladder, cheapest-and-cleanest first (§20 2026-07-10):

1. **Google Places rating + reviews — stage 1, the priority.** One Places Details call per property, geocode-keyed and cached forever (§12): structured rating, near-universal coverage of complexes, effectively free at this scale. The reviews summary is one small-model synthesis over the returned snippets. This is the ENRICH default and lands with the Maps tooling (P3-3 → P3-8); nothing else in the ladder starts until it is live.
2. **apartmentratings.com — stage 2, later.** Renter-specific signal Places lacks (management responsiveness, maintenance patterns). Needs its own property lookup on a third-party site, the fetch ladder (the census says which tier), and a small ratings extraction. Its result lands as a second `management_reviews` extraction row with source provenance — RECONCILE's ladder (§10.6) owns disagreement, so no bespoke blending formula exists until real disagreement data motivates one.
3. **General ratings sites (Yelp et al.) — stage 3, deferred (§18).** Thin apartment coverage, hostile fetch surfaces, and mostly duplicate signal. Reconsidered only if stages 1–2 leave demonstrated gaps.

The ladder is per-source additive, not a migration: each stage appends provenance-carrying extractions; nothing downstream changes shape when a stage arrives.

---

## 11. LLM Strategy

### 11.1 Provider Seam

All model calls go through a thin client in `worker/pipeline/llm/` with a per-stage model map in one settings module (`worker/pipeline/llm/config.py` — pinned OpenRouter model slugs, per-stage assignment, cache-control markers). The seam speaks one OpenAI-compatible API via **OpenRouter** (`openai` SDK → `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`); swapping a stage's model — any vendor — is a config change in `llm/config.py`. Upstream provider pinning (`provider.order` + `allow_fallbacks: false`) keeps routing deterministic and cache hits sticky across calls (§11.3). Structured output uses forced tool use (`tools` + pinned `tool_choice`); explicit `cache_control` on the stable prefix passes through to Anthropic models and is harmless elsewhere. This seam exists so the bench in Phase 0 can settle model choices empirically — it is **not** a mandate to go multi-vendor on day one. A non-Anthropic default pin is adopted only if the bench shows material savings at an equal verification pass-rate (P0-14 + a §20 entry).

### 11.2 Model Roles and Candidates

Baseline plan (all-Anthropic via OpenRouter): **Claude Haiku 4.5** (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`) as the workhorse (extract, verify, reconcile-equivalence, validate, custom-match, plan-assist), **Claude Sonnet 4.6** (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6`) for the taste-tier (vision, DISCOVER same-property judgment).

**Current pins (2026-07-28, §20 v3.23):** the workhorse role is `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` — EXTRACT/VERIFY were already there (P0-14, then the P3-6 waiver), and VALIDATE, CUSTOM_MATCH, ENRICH_REVIEWS, UTILITY_BASELINES, `plan_assist`, and `reconcile_equivalence` now follow, so the workhorse tier is one model rather than a split. **DISCOVER stays on `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`** (its native-search behavior was separately observed to degrade on Gemini — §20 2026-07-22) and the taste tier stays `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6`. Every seed replay recording re-keys on a pin change by design; the committed set was re-recorded with this pin.

Alternatives explicitly evaluated per Yusuf's request — indicative list pricing as of mid-2026, **verify before committing** (prices move quarterly). OpenRouter slugs shown; list prices in `MODEL_PRICES` are the cost-tally source of truth:

| Model (OpenRouter slug) | ~$/MTok in / out | Candidate for | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` | 1.00 / 5.00 | workhorse (baseline) | Strong structured extraction + evidence fidelity; prompt caching (−90% cached input) via OpenRouter passthrough |
| `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6` | 3.00 / 15.00 | vision + judgment (baseline) | Highest-confidence vision assessments |
| `google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite` | 0.10 / 0.40 | VALIDATE, CUSTOM_MATCH, possibly EXTRACT | ~10× cheaper than Haiku on input; supports vision + structured output; the question is evidence-quote fidelity and fee-language extraction quality — bench it |
| `google/gemini-2.5-flash` | 0.30 / 2.50 | VISION replacement | Credible vision at ~⅙ Sonnet price; implicit caching via Google AI Studio |
| Gemini 3 / 3.1 Flash-Lite tier | 0.25–0.50 / 1.50–3.00 | mid-tier alternative | Newer family; promoted to first-class sweep candidates 2026-07-17 — current cost lean is `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` |
| DeepSeek V3.x | ~0.14 in | text-only stages | Cheapest text; no fit for vision; weaker structured-output ergonomics — likely not worth a third provider for pennies |
| GPT-5 Mini / Nano tier | mid/low range | same slots as Gemini Flash | Competitive on paper; adds a provider without a distinct advantage here |

**Recommendation:** start all-Anthropic (Phases 0–2). In Phase 0, run the extraction bench (the 10 labeled listings, same schema; §20 2026-07-17) across the full priced pool in `MODEL_PRICES` — the Haiku 4.5 baseline, the Gemini 2.5 and 3.x Flash tiers (current cost lean: `google/gemini-3-flash-preview`), and the other priced entries — measuring verification pass-rate, gate-criterion accuracy, and cost. The realistic upside of a Gemini split is cutting the two biggest LLM lines (EXTRACT and VISION) by 5–10×, taking per-listing cost from ~$0.10 toward ~$0.03 — real money at zero scale is still ~nothing, so quality wins any tie. OpenRouter's ~5.5% platform fee is cents at this scale (§15).

**Bench outcome (P0-13/P0-14, 2026-07-21 — [§20](#20-decision-log)).** Ran; `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` won the EXTRACT/VERIFY pair (criterion accuracy 0.904 vs Haiku 0.862, tied gate accuracy, 0 failures, ~63% cheaper, ~2× faster) and is pinned for those two stages via `EXTRACT_VERIFY_MODEL` — the "possibly EXTRACT" question above resolves to it. Other workhorse stages stayed Anthropic at that decision. The Owner's later P3-6 waiver ([§20](#20-decision-log), 2026-07-28) explicitly extends Gemini to `plan_assist` and `reconcile_equivalence` without claiming new bench evidence; that missing stage-specific comparison is technical debt. DISCOVER remains Haiku and TASTE remains Sonnet. `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` — the "~10× cheaper, bench it" candidate — was disqualified on reliability (7/10 schema-validation failures); six priced slugs that could not route or honor forced tool use were pruned from `MODEL_PRICES`.

### 11.3 Cross-Cutting Techniques

- **Structured outputs everywhere:** extraction schema generated from `criteria_catalog`; every field `{value, confidence, evidence_quote}` — the evidence requirement measurably suppresses hallucination and feeds VERIFY.
- **Prompt caching:** stable per-stage prefixes (schema, catalog, rules, few-shots; ~3–6k tokens) marked cacheable → ~90% off on hits.
- **Model pinning and migrations:** model IDs pinned in config and recorded per extraction; upgrading a model is a deliberate migration — re-extract from stored cleaned text, diff against prior values, review the diff. Never let a silent model change wobble scores.

## 12. External Integrations

| Integration | Used for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| OpenRouter | All LLM stages | Single gateway (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`); OpenAI-compatible API; prompt caching passthrough is load-bearing for NFR1; Anthropic Message Batches API unavailable — accepted (§15) |
| Google Maps Platform | Geocoding (dedupe), Places (grocery/mosque proximity, reviews), Routes (commute criteria) | Within monthly free credit at this scale; geocodes and distances cached forever |
| Supabase | Postgres, Auth, Storage (images and future retained artifacts), Realtime | The single stateful system |
| Resend | Product/Hunt email through HTTPS; Supabase Auth/security email through separately configured SMTP | One provider, two ownership paths. Product sends use durable delivery ids as idempotency keys and signed webhook receipts; Auth templates and sessions remain GoTrue's responsibility |
| Bright Data (default) / ScrapingBee (alternate) | Tier-3 fetching | **Free plans only** (§20 2026-07-07); provider swap = one env var. Apify structured actors still deferred |
| Render / Cloudflare Pages | Hosting | Blueprints in `infra/` |
