## 13. Frontend Design

### 13.1 Routes

```
/login                  password sign-in + existing-account magic link/email code
/signout                clear this browser's session and return to login
/auth/callback          email confirmation / magic-link callback
/auth/reset-password    password recovery completion
/onboarding             required default display-name setup
/profile                account profile — default display name + default color
/                       current member's Hunts · create hunt · Site-Admin-only panel card
/h/:huntId              Overview — table + detail panel
/h/:huntId/map          Map — scored Unit Groups plotted, sharing the Overview filter set (§13.2)
/h/:huntId/rubric       Rubric (view default; single-page editor for Owner; read-only for others)
/h/:huntId/tasks        Tasks — Active | History tabs
/h/:huntId/compare      side-by-side Unit Group compare (personal set, up to 3 — §13.2)
/h/:huntId/visits       Visits — every tour of this hunt's properties, by state (§9.7, §13.2)
/h/:huntId/visits/new   create a Visit: property → plans → units → pre-visit
/h/:huntId/visits/:id   the Visit Checklist (phone-first; desktop read/edit — §13.2)
/h/:huntId/settings     Members, roles, invites, colors, hunt settings (§8.2 — Owner-edited,
                        member-viewable), danger zone (Owner)
/invite/:token          Invite acceptance (auth-gated)
/join/:token            Invitation Link auto-join (auth-gated; post-login return)
/admin/demo             Site-Admin Demo Hunt publication, freshness, and kill switch
```

**Resuming an interrupted invitation** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.74). `/invite/:token` and `/join/:token` are the only routes worth returning to after authentication, so they are the only ones the app will resume: the guard redirects to `/login?next=<target>`, and the login page, the magic-link callback, `/auth/reset-password` and `/signout` all pass the same value through and all filter it through the same allow-list — anything else lands on `/`. The target rides in the **query string**, not router state, because state dies on a reload or in a second tab and a lost target turns an invitation into a silent trip to the homepage. `/onboarding` already carried `?next=`, so a first-time account completes its display name and continues into the Hunt. The two route guards additionally treat a **Demo session as authenticated**: the demo principal has a server-minted token and deliberately no `auth.users` row ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.55), so a guard that reads only GoTrue would bounce every demo visitor back to the page they entered from.

### 13.2 Key Views (Mantine mapping)

**Overview table** (`mantine-react-table` or Mantine Table + TanStack): one row is a Unit Group plus its explicitly labeled Display Floor Plan ([§9.4](#94-floor-plans-and-unit-groups)). Beds/baths identity, Floor Plan count, and rent/sqft ranges summarize the group; score, all-in cost/composition, deposit, and Criterion-backed values belong to the Display Floor Plan. The optional Available column remains the group's **earliest availability summary** and is labeled/treated as such; the Display Floor Plan's availability remains the value in its score breakdown. A quiet `×N` multi-plan affordance (absorbed the former Info column — §20 2026-07-18) opens the alternatives, `Pinned: <plan>`, `Filter-selected Floor Plan: <plan>`, or `Preferred Floor Plan: <plan>` identifies the representative, and “varies by Floor Plan” appears when plan-scoped facts differ; the row never shows an optimistic union of sibling-plan amenities. The coming stale / auto-resolved / single-source / Problematic badges land on the Property cell. Other columns: name, Unit Group, rent range, sqft range, all-in cost (estimated portion visually distinct, e.g. `$1,845 (~$210 est.)`; **composition warnings collapse to one amber triangle whose hover lists the warning titles, except `heat_unknown`** — the total already uses the costlier gas/electric variant, so it remains ledger/drawer provenance rather than repeated table noise; chips-per-warning rejected as clutter, details stay in the drawer), a single curation cell (Interest Status `Select` + visited checkbox sharing one column), **Unit-Group-specific** rating dots per member color, and the count of Listing-wide plus current-Unit-Group comments. Machine tokens in selects render sentence-cased. Owner/Curator edits status and visited inline; members see disabled read-only controls. The detail panel labels Unit-Group-specific comments and edited comments; comment authors can edit their body or soft-delete it. Filter bar above the table: active-filter Pills + expandable panel; filters include a free-text name/address search (its input always visible beside the Filters button — §20 2026-07-19), score range, rent/sqft/beds/baths bounds, normalized city (including Unknown; options display as `City, ST` when state is known — §20 2026-07-23), Interest Status, visited, derived availability, and (§20 2026-07-19) laundry, parking, pets policy, cooling, dishwasher, max all-in cost, and available-by date. The extensible predicate registry classifies each predicate as Property/Unit-Group or Floor-Plan scope and follows §9.4's one-plan conjunction/filter-selection rule; Criterion-backed predicates read each candidate plan's score breakdown, so a Rubric-disabled Criterion filters as unknown. **Unknown values always pass a filter** — pending is not a verdict — and while any filter is active the bar shows Unit Group row counts as `N visible · M filtered out`, plus the separate manual-pin alternate-match count when nonzero. Pending/unscored rows are not coerced to zero. **Hunt-wide filters** (§20 2026-07-19): an Owner/Curator can *Apply hunt-wide* the current filter set (persisted in `hunt_shared_filters`, [§8.2](#82-tables)); it **seeds every member's filter state on Overview open**, after which members deviate freely — a badge reads "Hunt-wide filters" while local state matches and "Hunt-wide · modified" once it doesn't, with a *Reset to hunt-wide* action for any member. Publishing cleared filters retires the hunt-wide set; nothing re-applies mid-session (deliberately not Realtime-synced). The submit-URL control carries a Source Policy `Select` preset to the hunt default — visible but not in the way, since the default is right for most submissions. A view-options cluster beside the filter bar sets row density (compact / normal / comfortable — spacing only, persisted per browser) and **column visibility** (§20 2026-07-19): Sqft, All-in, Status, and People are togglable and on by default; City (shows `City, ST` or county/state when city is absent), Available (earliest Unit Group availability), Deposit, and Added are off by default — the default set is exactly the pre-picker table. Sortable headers cover score, property name, rent, all-in cost, earliest Unit Group availability, and added date (availability defaults ascending; unknowns always sink). Each row's actions menu carries open details, open listing page, copy address/link, **Send to Compare** (toggles to Remove; disabled with a tooltip when the compare set is full), and **archive** (§20 2026-07-18, 2026-07-19). **Archiving is the soft delete** (§20 2026-07-19 — the former "delete" wording renamed to what the action always did): an Active | Archived Overview toggle exposes `status = archived` listings with restore, both directions Owner-only via `PATCH /v1/listings/{id}/status`, nothing destroyed. A checkbox column drives **bulk actions** over selected Unit Group rows — set Interest Status (Owner/Curator), send to Compare (fills to the limit and says so), archive (one confirm per batch). The submit control runs a **duplicate pre-check**: a URL already on one of the Hunt's Properties (official or Source URL, normalized host + path) warns before enqueueing — Submit anyway remains, since the pipeline stays the authority on identity.

**Permanent deletion** is distinct from archive and appears only in the Archived view, never in a row's ordinary Active actions and never as a bulk action. The Owner or a non-member Site Admin in Ghost View sees a destructive `Delete permanently` action. Its modal names the Property, previews grouped impact counts (Unit Groups, scores, manual/collaboration/task records, Visits and their records, and Hunt-scoped custom Extractions), states that the global Property and its facts survive, and enables deletion only after the actor types the exact Property name. Active Jobs block confirmation and the server re-checks every condition under lock. Success removes stale detail and Compare references as well as the Archived row.

**Deletion is always confirmed, and the confirmation always names the subjects** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.73). Every delete in the app — an account or a membership in the Admin panel, a Listing, an invite, an Invitation Link, a comment, a Visit defect — passes through one shared confirmation that lists what is about to go, by name, in a pane that scrolls rather than truncates. An **irreversible** deletion additionally requires the actor to type the subject's own name back when there is one subject, or the fixed phrase `permanently delete all selected <plural noun>` when there are several. A **reversible** removal (a membership that can be re-added, an invite that can be re-sent) shows the same list and asks only for the click, because demanding typing for an undoable act trains people to type without reading. A subject the server would refuse is listed with its reason and skipped rather than hidden, and the actor's typing is discarded whenever the selection changes.

**Detail panel** — right `Drawer` on desktop and mobile (full-width on narrow viewports): collected listing photos in a scroll-snap carousel above the score breakdown (signed URLs from the private P3-7a bucket; clicking a photo opens a full-screen lightbox with keyboard navigation and fit ↔ zoom), Criterion breakdown with evidence quotes, scoped applicability/target, a quiet **Sources disagree** marker where applicable, override controls **and a revert-to-original control on any overridden target** ([§9.6](#96-overrides-and-provenance)), separate **Cost Breakdown** (tagged composition, all-in result/Override, read-only included-utilities banner) and **Fees** (utility plus recurring/move-in correction controls) cards per §9.5, numeric values with their units inline (grocery proximity in minutes per `proximity_mode`), `Image` gallery with vision assessments, Floor Plans with pin control, Sources with `last_fetched_at` — the official site's link always shown, fetched or not ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)) — plus the Listing's Source Policy (changeable here — relaxing it enqueues a refresh that discovers the newly allowed Sources), comments, a **Location** card near the bottom (§20 2026-07-26 — a small interactive Google map centred on the Property's [§12](#12-caching-and-refresh-strategy) cached geocode, its pin carrying the displayed Unit Group's band colour so the map and the score agree, plus an "Open in Maps" deep link; a Property with no geocode yet renders the card's address-only fallback rather than an error, since coordinates arrive with a run, not at submission) that also carries the **Contact** row when one is known (§20 2026-07-27 — the resolved leasing phone as a `tel:` link and/or contact page, each labelled with where it came from, since a Places number and an official-site number are not equally trustworthy; the row is absent entirely when no rung yielded a value, because the Sources card and `official_url` already tell the reader how to proceed and an empty state would add noise without adding an action), score history across Rubric versions, any open/auto-resolved Checkpoints for this Listing, and an **Investigate** action (FR12) that enqueues an `investigate` Job and renders the returned brief when complete. Each Floor Plan opens the §9.4 nested/full-screen detail modal with its effective features, evidence, score, pin state, lifecycle fields, associated diagrams, and Source links; ambiguous diagrams appear in a separate unmatched gallery. Typed scoped Criteria render one value chip per variant and label each chip independently (`this plan`, `all units`, `select units`, or `units unspecified`), so Parking may truthfully show both “Carport · all units” and “Garage · select units.” A boolean exact claim on one plan leaves siblings Unknown unless separate generalized evidence exists. Deep links are absent in v1. Open/close animate via Mantine transitions; selection stays mounted through the exit so the slide-out completes.

**Map** (`/h/:huntId/map` — §20 2026-07-26) — every scored Unit Group in the hunt plotted on a Google map. **One pin is one Listing**, because the Unit Groups of a Property share a building; the pin carries every scored Unit Group the current filters kept. A pin whose groups land in **different score bands is sliced into those bands** (equal sectors clipped to the pin's silhouette, best first from 12 o'clock, hairline dividers) rather than averaged — the map must never invent a score nobody computed. Solid and sliced pins share one silhouette, size, and anchor, differing only in fill: a marker's *shape* never encodes anything, so it can't be misread as a second kind of thing on the map. Clicking a single-group pin opens the detail Drawer straight away; a multi-group pin first asks **which Unit Group**, since the Unit Group — not the Listing — is the entity the rest of the app compares and curates ([§9.4](#94-floor-plans-and-unit-groups)). The map reads the **same filter state as the Overview** (hunt-wide seed included), lifted to a hunt-scoped provider so the two views can never disagree about what is in view; the filter bar sits above the map unchanged. Best-scoring pins paint last and carry the highest z-index. The view re-fits bounds only when the plotted set changes, so a user's pan survives a repaint. A legend names the bands present. Listings that are filtered in but not plottable are stated as a footnote rather than silently dropped — separately counting those **not scored yet** and those **without a mapped location** (no `properties.lat/lng` yet, [§12](#12-caching-and-refresh-strategy)). Basemap and marker colours come from the `theme.ts` tokens in both schemes; the browser Maps key is a **separate, HTTP-referrer-restricted key** from the worker's server key, and with it unset every map surface renders an explanatory placeholder instead of breaking the page.

**Compare** (`/h/:huntId/compare`, P3-13's compare half — §20 2026-07-19) — side-by-side detail for the Unit Group rows sent from the Overview. The compared entity is the **Unit Group row** (`{hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key}`), not the Listing — a 2 bd/2 ba and a 1 bd/1 ba of the same property are different decisions. The selection set is **personal, per browser** (localStorage per hunt; a shared set would let one member's Clear wipe another's exploration), capped by a `COMPARE_LIMIT` constant (3 for now; raising it is a one-line change), auto-pruning entries whose listing or group vanished. Layout is a transposed table: attribute labels in a sticky first column, one column per compared group, horizontally scrollable in its own container. Sections: photo carousels (the drawer's carousel component), header facts (name/address with per-column remove + listing link, score, unit group, rent, sqft, all-in with estimated portion distinct, deposit, earliest availability, interest status/visited), the monthly-cost composition rows (union of `all_in_components` component names, cells tagged actual/estimated/unknown — pet rent, parking fees, and utilities appear here for free), and a **criteria section iterating the union of score-breakdown entries** (effective value + points delta per column) — which means custom criteria (P3-10) appear automatically when they land, no Compare change needed. Controls: Clear compare, and its own row-density menu persisted per browser; the nav link carries a count badge. Columns reorder by dragging their headers (§20 2026-07-19 — native HTML5 drag, no dnd dependency at three columns; the drop rewrites the stored entry order).

**Tasks** — Active tab: live job cards (Realtime on `jobs`/`job_events`) with stage progress, a loader + counting-up elapsed timer on running jobs (`started_at`, exposed on JobResponse), checkpoint prompts rendered inline with extracted value/evidence/Source context when available, cancel/retry per permissions. **History tab (FR10):** every past run, newest finished first (then newest created for legacy ties), filterable by listing/member/outcome; independent Inspect plan and Inspect timeline controls reveal the plan manifest and chronological `Timeline` of stage events, checkpoint Q&A including auto-resolutions, errors, and actual cost.

**Rubric** — view mode default: enabled criteria as cards with an options table (match label left, colored points text right; bonus and non-negotiable badges on the card header). Match labels omit assumed operators (`eq`/`bool`), use `<`/`≤`/`>`/`≥` for inequalities, and carry the criterion's display unit on numeric values ("between 10 and 20 min"). Owner edits via a single-page editor (two-column card grid; all catalog criteria with enable toggles); first-run hunts (no saved rubric) open directly in edit mode. Widgets rendered from `value_schema`. Editor card anatomy (§20 2026-07-18): options are fixed grid rows (match | points | dealbreaker | remove) that never wrap; numeric ops order along the number line (`<` `≤` `=` `≥` `>` between) with word glosses in the dropdown; points are spinner `NumberInput`s suffixed "pts"; the dealbreaker is an `ActionIcon` toggle whose armed state swaps the points input for a red "→ sets score" input; boolean criteria render fixed True/False text rows (never a switch); the unknown-value delta is a labeled "If unknown" row and the non-negotiable gate row aligns its set-score input in the points column. Read-only view for non-Owners (Phase 2).

**Visits** (`/h/:huntId/visits`, [§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)) — three surfaces sharing one nav item placed between Compare and Rubric. The **list** is one row per Visit: property, when, who went, the Visit Unit labels as chips (units the listing never advertised are visibly marked), and a state pill — planned with prep progress, in progress with a live percentage, completed with its visit score or a count of unanswered critical questions, cancelled kept at reduced emphasis rather than deleted, because an agent no-show is information about the property and the prep survives a rebooking. A state filter sits beside a *New visit* action. The **create flow** is four steps: a searchable property `Select` scoped to this hunt's listings (a visit to a property with no Listing has nothing to check the agent against), Floor Plan checkboxes grouped by derived Unit Group, the unit builder (§9.7 — ticking a plan pre-seeds a label to name on site; one *add a unit* control handles every other case), then the pre-visit page. **Pre-visit is a state, not a screen**: a planned Visit is one whose tour has not started, so only the `property`-scoped "before you go" section is open and *Start visit* stamps the time and unlocks the rest.

The **checklist** is phone-first and thumb-driven: a persistent unit switcher on `unit`-scoped sections with a scope bar naming what is being recorded into, a Quick/Standard/Thorough depth dial, the four entry controls (tri-state Check at a full 44px target on the thumb side, answer-gated Question card, per-member Impression rating, Fact inputs), a floating capture action absent where there is nothing to capture, and one collapsed activity ribbon for presence instead of toasts — you are holding the phone in one hand and a tape measure in the other. Section order is deliberately not the document's: you tour room by room while agent questions arise whenever the agent is beside you, so there are three doors in and no forced sequence. On desktop the same Visit reads as a document — the 21 sections restored in a rail, per-member comparison columns with a computed spread bar showing where the team disagrees most, and **your own column editable while others' are not**. Both layouts are token-driven and audited in both schemes; a member-colour dot marks personal answers, and a neutral scope tag marks what an answer is *about*, the two being orthogonal axes that must not be conflated.

Integration points: the **listing drawer** gains a Visits list for that Property plus [§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist) Fee Proposal rows in Cost & fees — the staged-override hatch and dashed dot, with ✓/✕ in a two-slot action area that is the one sanctioned exception to the single-icon rule — and the **Overview** gains a `Visit` column beside Fit, out of 5 and starred to mark it a human judgement, never merged with the Rubric score.

**Color scheme** — Mantine light/dark with `auto` default (system preference); explicit toggle in the app header and on public routes (login, hunt switcher); choice persisted in localStorage. All styling stays Mantine-token-based in both schemes.

**Visual identity — "Dusk & clay"** (2026-07-09): the palette and type carry the app's warmth. Primary is a muted dusk indigo (`dusk`); all neutrals are warm (stone `gray` in light mode on a warm-paper body, charcoal `dark` in dark mode); `clay` is the lone warm accent, reserved for waiting-on-user; status hues are earthy (moss/olive/ochre) under the unchanged `semantic` role names. Headings are Literata (serif), body is Source Sans 3 (sans), both self-hosted; tables render tabular numerals. Everything flows through `theme.ts` — no bespoke CSS layer.

### 13.3 Realtime

Supabase channels per hunt on: `hunt_listings`, `scores`, `comments`, `ratings`, `listing_unit_group_states`, `hunt_listing_refresh_status`, `jobs`, `job_events`, `visits`, `visit_units`, `visit_entries`, `visit_defects`, `visit_fee_proposals`. Events invalidate TanStack Query caches — one consistency mechanism, no bespoke socket handling. RLS applies to Realtime, so subscription security is automatic.

Visits add the one **Presence** channel in the app ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)): who is in a Visit right now and which section they are in. It is scoped to the Visit route rather than held hunt-wide, since presence is only meaningful while several people are walking the same apartment. Presence is ambient — an avatar ring and one collapsed ribbon — and never blocks, because the checklist has to stay usable one-handed. Realtime remains an enhancement over the local-first write path, never the storage mechanism: a Visit must work with no signal at all.

---

## 14. Caching and Refresh Strategy

| Data class | TTL | Re-runs |
|---|---|---|
| Rent, availability, deposit, fees | 24 h | FETCH(contributing Sources) → EXTRACT → VERIFY → RECONCILE → SCORE |
| Listing details (beds, laundry, pets, sqft) | 14 d | same |
| Images / vision | 30 d or new images | image FETCH → VISION → SCORE; a full photo gallery (`MAX_STORED_IMAGES`) satisfies the class even when extra candidates failed |
| Reviews | 30 d | ENRICH(reviews) → SCORE |
| Utility baselines | 120 d | scheduled regional pass (city, county, or state scope) |
| Location facts (geocode, distances) | immutable | address correction only |

**Content-hash gating is the keystone:** `cleaned_text_hash` compared on every fetch; when every Source is unchanged, text-only refresh skips EXTRACT, VERIFY, RECONCILE, and SCORE, so steady-state cost rounds to a fetch and a comparison. The planner maps optional `fields` to **refresh-class tokens**, not literal Extraction fields; changed page bytes still run the full text Catalog. Pricing and listing-details checks use the submitted Source plus every current contributing Source permitted by the Listing's Source Policy, collapsed by Syndication Family — never official-only. Hunt-wide refresh fans out one Job per Listing under a process-shared per-domain rate limiter (politeness doubles as bot-detection avoidance). A worker scheduler tick runs about every five minutes and coalesces all due classes for one Listing into one Job; an active ingest/refresh suppresses another scheduled Job.

A failed class-scoped refresh remains due, but the scheduler retries that class with durable exponential backoff: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, then at most every 24 hours, counted since that class's last successful freshness marker. A successful class projection resets the count. The backoff is derived from Job history, survives worker restarts, and does not prevent a person from requesting a manual retry. Committed development fixtures use the reserved `*.seed.example` Source namespace and are never scheduled: those URLs exist only behind the dev seed's injected fixture fetcher, not on the public network.

Refresh authority is Source-local. Only a successful, complete Source refresh may append disappearance observations for formerly current claims, mark missing Floor Plans inactive, or retire Floor Plan-diagram associations. Unchanged content preserves current rows; partial/failed work is additive at most and cannot turn silence into `none` or retire prior truth. Reappearing identical diagram content reactivates the content-hash asset/association; changed bytes append a new asset/association version. Inactive evidence and bytes remain while the Property exists unless an explicit purge removes unreferenced objects.

---

## 15. Cost Model and Optimization

Baseline (all-Anthropic via OpenRouter) per-listing budget, first ingestion, 3 sources, ~8 images — naive: **~$0.26**. With the levers below: **~$0.10–0.12 new · <$0.01 refresh · $0 re-score**, meeting NFR1. A 60-listing hunt with two months of daily refreshes: roughly $8–12 total inference (plus OpenRouter's ~5.5% platform fee — cents at this scale). A Gemini split per [§11.2](#112-model-roles-and-candidates) could roughly triple the headroom; take it only on a quality tie.

Levers, in order of impact:

1. **Prompt caching** on stable stage prefixes → ~40–50% off workhorse input spend.
2. **Content-hash gating** → refreshes stop costing extraction at all; without this, refreshes quietly become 80% of the bill.
3. **Vision discipline:** classify up to 30 unstored ≤384 px thumbnails locally with the pinned ONNX artifact, rank kitchen targets by ONNX probability, then send only three targets (total quality cap 8) at ~1024 px to anchored quality VISION. `MAX_STORED_IMAGES = 30` remains about 1.8 MB per typical Property. Classification and quality have independent content/config digests, so unchanged inputs make zero calls. ONNX removes the classifier model call; anchored quality VISION remains the only LLM image call.
4. **Source-count honesty:** baseline slate is ≤3 extractions (submitted link + one sibling per permitted tier, family-distinct); the official site is a stored link, not baseline spend; escalation (official arbiter, then one sibling round) triggers only for unresolved decision-relevant fields — worst case ~7 extractions (~$0.20), rare by construction ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)).
   DISCOVER itself is capped at three native web searches ($0.01 each at landing), six model turns, five results per search, and twelve total results per turn; its plan estimate reserves $0.04 before model tokens.
5. **Global property reuse:** re-adding a known property to a new hunt costs a $0 SCORE plus whatever TTLs say is stale.
6. **Not worth optimizing:** Maps (inside free credit), Postgres-as-queue ($0), and anything requiring new infrastructure — complexity is the real budget.

*Dropped lever — Anthropic Message Batches API (−50% on non-interactive work):* unavailable through OpenRouter. New-listing ingestion is latency-bound (NFR2), so batch never applied there; content-hash gating (lever 2) already zeroes unchanged refreshes; the realistic savings over a full hunt were ~$1–3. NFR1 is met without it.

Fixed monthly: Cloudflare Pages $0 · Supabase $0 (cleaned-text caching + image cap keep free tier viable indefinitely at this scale) · Render API $0–7 · Render worker ~$7 (paid, so jobs never wait on cold starts; Phase 1 may run the in-process loop instead, [§5](#5-system-architecture)) · Maps $0 · Tier-3 services $0 unless the gate opens, then ~$5–30 pay-per-use. **Realistic: $7–14/mo + usage.**

**Learning-track spend is bounded by design:** agents mode runs against bench-set-sized workloads, not the live hunt, so its expected 5–15× token multiplier applies to ~20 listings per eval run (order of a few dollars), not to daily operation. Investigator runs are on-demand and estimated at roughly $0.30–0.80 each. Langfuse makes every trace's cost visible, which is both the budget control and part of the curriculum.

---

## 16. Security and Privacy

- **RLS is the boundary.** Every per-hunt table policy joins through `hunt_members`; global fact tables are client-read-only. API mutations run under the user JWT; only the worker holds the service role.
- **Permanent Listing deletion is fail-closed and Hunt-bounded.** There is no authenticated table `DELETE` path. The narrow Owner RPC and audited non-member Site Admin route require an archived Listing plus an exact Property-name confirmation, refuse active Jobs, and perform the tombstone, Hunt-scoped custom-Extraction cleanup, Visit cascade, and Listing cascade atomically. The global Property graph is outside the deletion target. The Site Admin audit row commits with the Ghost View deletion; a failed audit rolls the operation back. This is intentionally absent from bulk actions.
- **Account provisioning:** public Supabase sign-up is disabled. The service-role Admin API is the only account-creation path and is gated by the Site Admin check plus an Admin Audit Log entry. The frontend may not call `signUp`; magic-link/OTP requests set `shouldCreateUser: false`, so an unknown email cannot become an account through the auth provider.
- **Demo Mode is enforced in the database, not the app** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.53). A Demo Account holds a genuine Supabase session in an untrusted browser, so every control assumes the JWT will be driven directly against PostgREST, Storage, and GoTrue with the API bypassed entirely. Five controls, in the order they matter:
  0. **There is no account to attack.** The demo principal has no `auth.users` row, so GoTrue answers `user_not_found` to every operation on it — `/user`, `updateUser`, `logout`, `reauthenticate`. This was settled empirically against a running stack, because the two obvious alternatives both fail: a real account with a self-minted sessionless token, and the same account *banned*, each still permitted `PUT /auth/v1/user` to change the password. A ban blocks password sign-in and nothing else. No configuration flag can substitute for the subject simply not existing.
  0a. **Identity is the signed claim, not the configuration row** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.56). Every control below asks `private.demo_identity()`, which answers `none`, `scoped` or `dark`. A minted token carries `manzil_demo`; the marker row in `demo_accounts`, the enabled switch and a matching generation are what turn that into `scoped`. Anything inconsistent — marker deleted, duplicated, naming a different subject, generation stale, demo off — is `dark`: zero non-reference reads and every write refused. The inversion this replaces is the point. The predicates used to read `not is_demo_account(auth.uid()) or <scoping>`, so *losing* the marker row made a live demo token indistinguishable from an ordinary member and handed it every Property, Source and image in the project, with the kill switch bypassed entirely. Configuration loss must remove access, never confer it. The claim is safe to trust because PostgREST verifies the project signature first — and it is used only to take privilege away, never to grant it. Two predicates that route around `member_role()` are guarded the same way: the `owner_id = auth.uid()` branch of `hunts_member_select`, and `private.is_site_admin()`.
  1. **Reads are scoped by restrictive policy.** The global fact tables (`properties`, `property_sources`, `floor_plans`, `property_images`, `property_contacts`, `floor_plan_images`, `extraction_images`, and global `extractions`) are permissively readable by any authenticated user. A restrictive `SELECT` policy `AND`s onto that, confining a Demo Account to rows reachable from the Demo Hunt. `criteria_catalog` and `utility_baselines` stay open — they are reference data. **Hunt-scoped** tables are covered by a different mechanism: `private.member_role()` itself is demo-aware, so hunts, listings, comments, jobs, scores and every `visit_*` table inherit the scoping and the kill switch without forty separate policies.
  1a. **Private Storage requires a Hunt context — for everyone.** The `property-images` bucket authorized every authenticated user for every object, relying on object-path secrecy, which stops being a control once a shared public account can list the bucket. v3.54 narrowed it for the demo principal and left every other account with the original posture; v3.56 finished the job, because [§8.3](#83-access-and-rls) has always said signed-URL access is granted only in an authenticated Hunt context containing that Property. Object keys are `properties/<property_id>/<hash>.webp`, so `private.can_read_property_image()` maps the object back to a Property and requires `private.member_role()` to be non-null for some Hunt that lists it. The demo case then needs no branch of its own: `member_role()` is already demo-aware, so the demo principal reaches the Demo Hunt's images and inherits the kill switch for free. Unparseable keys fail closed; the worker writes with the service role and is unaffected.
  2. **Cleaned Source text is column-revoked in *both* of its homes** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.58). This section has always said cleaned Source text is service-role-only; until v3.53 the code did not implement it at all, and the v3.53 fix revoked `property_sources.cleaned_text` and then repeated the claim — while the same page bodies sat in `jobs.payload.run_state.sources[]` and `jobs.payload.auto_resolved_checkpoint.snapshot`, member-selectable, 5330 characters of it readable with an ordinary member's JWT on the dev database. The front door was locked and a side door left open. Both are now revoked. `jobs` carries a stored generated column `payload_public` — `payload` with every `cleaned_text` key removed at any depth — and `authenticated`/`anon` hold SELECT on every column of `jobs` except `payload`. Three things this required that a revoke alone does not give: the two `authenticated`-executable RPCs that returned `setof jobs` now return an explicit projection, because **column ACLs do not apply to composite rows returned by a function** and PostgREST serialises every column of an RPC result; `answer_job_checkpoint` merges the answer into the Job's own stored payload instead of accepting a replacement, because otherwise the API's read-modify-write would write the redacted projection back and silently empty every Source body; and the transform is *total and recursive* rather than two literal paths, because a generated column that raises makes a row unwritable and the worker is not the only writer. The text is present-but-unreadable rather than absent — see R14. Separately, `job_events.detail` no longer records a 2 KB raw page prefix for `fetch_page` tool calls; a length and a SHA-256 go there instead.
  3. **Writes are refused by trigger.** A statement-level `before insert or update or delete` trigger on every `public` table raises `42501` when `private.is_demo_account(auth.uid())`. Chosen over amending ~20 migrations of write policies, many of which admit "any member" and would therefore admit a demo Curator. Its coverage limits are known and asserted: `public` only, no TRUNCATE unless separately attached, and views only via `instead of` triggers — so a companion self-check refuses any `public` view writable by `authenticated`.
  4. **Money-spending RPCs guard the subject.** `submit_listing`, `answer_job_checkpoint`, `set_listing_source_policy`, and `correct_auto_resolved_checkpoint` are `grant execute to authenticated` and enqueue Jobs, so they are reachable from a browser without touching the API. Each rejects a Demo Account explicitly rather than relying on the trigger alone. The service-role paths (`accept_hunt_invite`, `join_hunt_invitation_link`, `transfer_hunt_ownership`) run with `auth.uid()` null, where the trigger cannot see them, so they guard their `p_user_id` **parameter** instead.
  5. **The kill switch is an RLS fact, and disabling revokes.** `site_settings.demo_enabled` participates in both the restrictive read policies *and* `private.member_role()`, so disabling the demo darkens PostgREST and Realtime — verified: with the switch off, a live token reads zero rows from properties, hunts, comments, jobs and visit entries. Every toggle in either direction also increments `site_settings.demo_generation`, which is minted into each token and must match; so an operator who disables during an incident, remediates and re-enables does **not** hand access back to the tokens that were live before it ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.56). Without that, "disable" was a pause lasting until `exp`. An API-layer check exists too (`require_not_demo`, registered app-wide so a new route cannot forget it), but only as a fast, legible `403 demo_read_only` — it is not the boundary. Turning the demo **on** requires a ready current Demo Release, a still-current input fingerprint, and the selected Hunt still being owned by the acting Site Admin; `private.demo_preflight()` and the Admin Audit Log entry remain in the same transaction as the switch. Turning it **off** never preflights, never deselects the Hunt or release, and never blocks on the ledger — an emergency shutoff must not be refusable by the conditions that made it an emergency, so a failed audit write there is logged loudly and the shutoff stands.
  5a. **Publishing is explicit, owner-consented, reviewed, bounded, and atomic** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.72). The Admin panel offers only Hunts whose `owner_id` is the acting Site Admin; being a Site Admin or the primordial admin does not permit publishing another Owner's Hunt. Preflight inventories active/archived Listings and collaboration history, requires explicit per-Hunt display names for every human member, runs the standing database security checks, and returns a ten-minute, single-use confirmation bound to the actor, Hunt, and canonical source fingerprint. The actor must then type the exact Hunt name. A durable worker build is capped at 50 Replay Captures (1 MiB sanitized payload each) and 100 mapped Properties, retries three times, and re-checks fingerprint, ownership, Site Admin status, and generation after external map calls. Only a complete build atomically updates `demo_hunt_id` + `demo_release_id`; otherwise the old release remains public. Hunt changes do **not** auto-publish: the Admin page marks derived assets stale and requires **Publish updates**, avoiding invisible Google spend and public-content changes. Active Listings remain live database reads by design; archived replay payloads and map stills are release-frozen. Disabling retains the selection and release. The selected Hunt cannot be deleted until another Hunt is promoted, while individual Listing archive/delete changes are picked up by the next explicit publication.
  5b. **Release assets have no browser-to-Storage authority.** `private.demo_publications`, captures, and confirmations have no `anon`/`authenticated` grants. The private `demo-assets` bucket has no read policy at all. A current Demo token with a matching generation may ask the API for the current release, one declared capture ordinal, or one map object whose exact key appears in that release's manifest; the API streams map bytes with its server credential and sends `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. Leaking an object path, an old release id, or a previous token therefore grants nothing. This deliberately differs from `property-images`, whose ordinary Hunt-context Storage policy remains unchanged.
  6. **Sessions are minted, short-lived, release-gated, and rate-limited in the database.** `POST /v1/demo/session` mints the token; `GET /v1/demo/config` answers whether to offer the button at all. Both require not merely `demo_enabled`, but a ready release for the selected Hunt whose current Owner is still a Site Admin; the same condition lives in `private.demo_identity()`, so ownership transfer or admin-grant removal darkens already-issued tokens. Those two are the only unauthenticated public routes, and both are edge-rate-limited in front of the API. The ceiling lives in Postgres because Render runs more than one instance and an in-process counter resets on restart, and the decision is taken under an advisory transaction lock — counting, deciding and recording without one is not atomic however few statements the caller sees, and racing instances would all read the same below-limit count and all issue. State is a **fixed-size counter table**, not a row per request: client keys hash into 4096 buckets, so an hour of traffic has a hard row ceiling no caller can exceed, and a refusal increments an integer. TTL is bounded at both ends (60s–30min, validated at startup rather than clamped at mint). No IP address or HMAC is stored, only the bucket number. A disabled or incomplete demo answers `404`, not `403`, so it is not discoverable.
- **Prompt injection via scraped pages** (untrusted input into prompts): extraction agents have *zero tool access* — worst-case injection is a bad value, not an action; VERIFY's evidence audit rejects values without genuine page evidence; plausibility bands catch absurd numbers; the fields most worth lying about (rent, fees) are exactly the cross-source-reconciled ones. Reduces injection from "compromise" to "one bad field that other sources outvote" — and votes count syndication families, not domains ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)), so a poisoned page cannot vote twice through feed echoes. A `trust_link` Source Policy ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)) removes the outvoting layer by explicit user choice; the zero-tool, evidence-audit, and plausibility controls still apply in full, and the permanent single-source badge keeps the reduced assurance visible.
- **DISCOVER search results are untrusted input.** Provider snippets, titles, and URLs are claims rather than instructions. Exact same-Property filtering precedes persistence; any model-selected page fetch still routes through VALIDATE_URL and the fetcher-layer SSRF guard. A bad result can therefore add a rejected or low-value candidate, not grant a new network capability.
- **Invite tokens:** random, expiring, single-role, revocable; accepting requires an authenticated session. The product email contains this bare capability, not a magic link: forwarding it can grant Hunt membership after sign-in but cannot grant an account session. Invitation Link tokens are Member-only bearer capabilities. Their database transaction locks the link, enforces deletion/expiration/use limits before revealing the Hunt, and commits membership plus attribution together; RLS prevents token enumeration and non-Owner management.
- **Secrets:** all API keys server-side only (Render env); the frontend holds nothing but the Supabase anon key, which RLS renders safe.
- **PII posture:** the system stores members' emails, names/colors, comments, and searched addresses (commute targets). **Property-level business contact** — a leasing office phone number and a generic contact/inquiry URL — is extracted and stored (§8.2 `property_contacts`, P3-21). **No individual's contact details are:** not a named agent, broker, or manager, not their direct line or personal email, and never a person's name or role. The control is structural, not just prompted: the extraction block and the `property_contacts` table have no column for a person, so an individual's identity has nowhere to land even when a page supplies one. Cleaned Source text may embed page content and is service-role-only wherever it is stored — `property_sources.cleaned_text` and the copy inside `jobs.payload`, both column-revoked (control 2 above; [§20](#20-decision-log) v3.58). Bounded excerpts are a deliberate exception and stay member-readable: `extractions`, `floor_plans.raw` and checkpoint context expose only the evidence quotes the drawer already renders.

---

## 17. Risks and Concerns Register

| # | Risk | Severity | Mitigation / posture |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Anti-bot blocking on major aggregators | High | Tier ladder + registry ([§10.7](#107-fetching-subsystem)); tier-diverse slate — a blocked domain costs one slot, substituted from a cheaper tier ([§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution)); Phase 0 gate decides whether Tier 3 is needed at all. One good source suffices by design |
| R2 | ToS exposure from scraping | Medium | Personal, low-volume tool; unblockers outsource the blocking fight, not the legal posture; never becomes a public *service*. Demo Mode ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.53) makes the app publicly **viewable** without changing the fetch posture by one request: a Demo Account cannot enqueue a Job, so no visitor can cause a fetch, and fetch volume stays a function of the Owner's own submissions. The residual is reputational rather than volumetric — a public URL makes the scraping visible, where before it was merely private |
| R12 | Public exposure of a codebase written for two trusted accounts | High | Demo Mode is the first time an untrusted party holds a valid session, which converts every "only trusted people have accounts" assumption into a live control. The controls are layered and DB-first ([§16](#16-security-and-privacy)): restrictive `SELECT` scoping on the global fact tables, a write-refusing trigger on every `public` table, subject-guards inside the `security definer` RPCs that spend money, and a kill switch that lives in RLS rather than only in the API. The standing risk is the *next* table or view: the guard is asserted by an in-migration self-check that fails CI when something is added without it |
| R3 | Hallucinated / wrong extractions poisoning decisions | High | Evidence quotes + VERIFY audit + plausibility bands + cross-source reconcile + override with provenance |
| R4 | Prompt injection via listing pages (incl. steering a tool-loop `fetch_page` at internal hosts — read-SSRF) | Medium | See [§16](#16-security-and-privacy); zero-tool extraction + evidence audit; fetcher-layer SSRF guard with tier-1 IP-pinning closes the HTTP path ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-12), tier-2 browser residual gated on a manual smoke test before a live loop |
| R5 | Dedupe false merges corrupting shared data | Medium | Dual-condition auto-merge; gray-zone checkpoints; `split_property` op from day one |
| R6 | Extraction inconsistency / model drift wobbling scores | Medium | Hash gating; pinned models; migrations as reviewed diffs; versioned vision reference set |
| R7 | Stale data presented as current | Medium | `last_fetched_at` on every row; TTL-based stale badges; conservative all-in defaults |
| R8 | Safety-data quality is inherently poor | Low | Ship `location_safety` as an override-first A+–F placeholder ([§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18); commercial safety APIs rejected on cost (hundreds of $/month); the dedicated ARCGIS/police-report module is [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)-deferred and possibly a standalone project; do not sink a week here |
| R9 | Supabase/Render free-tier ceilings | Low | KB-scale text artifacts; 20 ordinary photos plus separately capped Floor Plan diagrams; paid worker when Playwright arrives |
| R10 | Scope creep (the real killer of personal projects) | High | [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog) is a parking lot, not a promise; NFR5's new-moving-part rule; phases with exit criteria |
| R11 | Learning track starving shipping ("architecture astronautics") — the sneaky variant of R10, and there is a lease deadline | High | Hard gating rule in [§19](#19-implementation-phases): L-milestones start only after their shipping dependency is green and never delay the next shipping milestone; agents code flag-isolated in one module; eval reports force empirical honesty about what the ambition actually bought |
| R14 | Cleaned Source text is unreadable rather than absent | Medium | Accepted deliberately ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.58). The page bodies stay in `jobs.payload` because removing them means rewinding a resume that starts *past* FETCH, and an emptied Source is **skipped** by EXTRACT rather than raising — silent wrong results, worse than the exposure. The control is a column revoke plus the DB-maintained `payload_public` projection, so the data is one grant away from readable: a future definer view, admin export, support tool or `select *` reintroduces it. Three standing guards — the migration's catalogue self-check, `private.demo_preflight()`, and a CI test asserting `has_column_privilege('authenticated','public.jobs','payload','SELECT')` is false — exist because a blanket `grant select on all tables` is exactly how DM-1 was undone twice. Revisit when the resume path is reworked for other reasons. Two narrower residuals ride along: `property_sources.cleaned_text_path`/`screenshot_path` are reserved and unpopulated, so a future Storage-backed implementation inherits the same problem with none of these controls; and the `job_events` fix is per-tool, so the next body-returning tool re-creates the hazard unless it declares its own event summary — a general fix belongs with the tool registry |

---

## 18. Deferred Features and Backlog

Explicitly **not** in scope until pulled in deliberately (coding agents: do not implement):

- **Apify actor adapters for hostile aggregators** — gated on Phase 0 findings; may be prioritized up or dropped entirely based on how much inventory actually lives behind hostile domains.
- Automated listing discovery / alerts.
- Off-market detection beyond stale badging.
- Zip-level utility baselines; traffic-window-aware commute times.
- Per-member weighted rubrics / multiple rubrics per hunt.
- CSV/Sheets export.
- **Public read-only hunt sharing** — an Owner minting a link that shows *their* Hunt to someone without an account. Still deferred, and **Demo Mode is not it** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.53, v3.72). The two look alike and are not: sharing is per-Hunt, Owner-initiated, and would expose an arbitrary collaboration space; Demo Mode is one globally configured Hunt, initiated by a Site Admin who also owns and deliberately curates that Hunt, behind a short-lived virtual-principal session and an explicit publication review. Demo Mode makes sharing *easier* to build later — the restrictive read-scoping predicate is the same shape — but it grants an ordinary Owner no public-link ability, and a contributor must not extend the demo predicate into a sharing feature without its own ruling.
- Screenshot-based extraction as a last-resort path for text-unrecoverable pages.
- **Checkpoint page screenshots.** P3-11 uses the extracted value, evidence quote, and Source link instead. Full-page captures are browser-tier-only, add private-Storage/signing/retention machinery, and do not locate the disputed value without a separate coordinate/highlighting system. `property_sources.screenshot_path` stays reserved so this can be revisited if real checkpoint usage shows the compact context is insufficient. This is distinct from screenshot-based Extraction above.
- **General ratings sites (Yelp et al.) as rating sources** — stage 3 of the ratings ladder ([§10.12](#1012-ratings-sources)): thin apartment coverage, hostile fetch surfaces, mostly duplicate signal. Revisit only if Places + apartmentratings.com leave demonstrated gaps.
- **Push notifications.** P3-22's email delivery, account/Hunt preferences, and checkpoint attention count landed in v3.75. Native/browser push remains deferred and is an additional channel only; it must never become a prerequisite for the email path or reuse email consent implicitly.
- **Account deletion** — self-service removal of a user account and the personal data attached to it. Deferred because the hard part is not the delete: a departing user may **own** Hunts, and a Hunt always needs an Owner, so deletion must first force transfer-or-archive for every owned Hunt, then decide per artifact what survives. The shape when it is pulled in: owned Hunts blocked until transferred or archived; `user_profiles` and Hunt memberships removed; comments and ratings **tombstoned rather than erased** (a rating that silently vanishes changes a Hunt's history for everyone else, which is the other members' record too, not only the leaver's); `feedback` rows kept but detached; `auth.users` deleted last so the cascade order is observable. Until it exists the Account surface says so plainly and an operator does it by hand. Tracked as IMPLEMENTATION §7 **P3-23** (deferred, not started).
- **Changing the email address on an account** — **declined, not deferred** (2026-07-29). An account is tied to the address it was created with; a different address is a different account. Supabase Auth can re-verify an address change, but the flow has to survive the window where the old address is still authoritative, and at this scale the feature buys one rare convenience in exchange for a permanent identity seam. The Account surface states the constraint as a fact rather than presenting a control that has not been built.
- **Location-safety assessment module** (the future producer behind the `location_safety` placeholder — [§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-18): grades locations A+–F from public primary sources — ARCGIS/open-data crime layers, local police department reports, FBI UCR/NIBRS — rather than a commercial safety API (quoted in the hundreds of $/month; building beats buying at this scale). Scope note: implement for **SE Michigan first** with metro-by-metro expansion later; the module may well become its **own standalone project** consumed here as a data source. Until it exists, ENRICH emits nothing for `location_safety` and the A+–F override dropdown is the input path. Tracked as IMPLEMENTATION §7 P3-17 (gated, not started).
- Cross-Source Floor Plan matching; automatic matching is Source-local in v1.
- Private pools, granular per-Floor-Plan internet readiness, storage, and basements. Pool remains Property-scoped; internet readiness is listing-advertised Property-level only until revisited.
- Floor Plan deep links; Curator diagram association/disassociation UI; user-selected primary diagrams.
- Floor Plan diagram OCR/interpretation, inferred room dimensions, vision-based association, floor/view/orientation, individual-unit numbers, and individual-unit inventory. **Unchanged by Visits** ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist), [§20](#20-decision-log) v3.30): a `visit_units` row is a human-typed label on a tour, Hunt-scoped, in the same category as Interest Status. The app still cannot enumerate a Property's units, never learns them from a Source, and holds no unit as a fact. What remains deferred is the app *knowing* what units exist; writing down which doors you walked through is not that, and a future reader should not mistake the one for the other.
- **Visits phase 2 — voice notes and transcription** (2026-07-30 ruling): recording an answer aloud on the tour — as an alternative *or* an addition to typed text on a Question, and as a spoken comment on a Visit — plus transcription of the recording. Deferred with photos because it needs the same new Storage infrastructure (private bucket, signed-URL reads, storage RLS), and because a tour with typed answers already works.
  Two things a future reader must not discover late. First, **this is the first LLM surface the Visits feature would have**: [§19](#19-phased-delivery-plan) records Visits as having none, and that is true of v1 only. Transcription is a model call, so it inherits the whole contract — the client seam in `worker/pipeline/llm/` rather than a provider SDK at the call site ([§16](#16-security-and-privacy-controls)), Langfuse tracing on every call (NFR6), and a cost line. It also makes Visits the first surface where **a member's voice** is sent to a third-party model, which is a privacy question the photo bundle does not raise.
  Second, it forces a ruling §9.7 currently answers only for text: a Question is marked asked by its **typed answer**, and nothing else. Whether an untranscribed recording satisfies that gate, whether a transcript counts as the answer or as evidence beside it, and what happens when transcription fails or is wrong, all have to be decided **before** any of it ships — a recording that silently ticks a critical question off without anyone reading it back would defeat the gate the checklist exists to enforce.
- **Visits phase 2 — visit photos** (2026-07-30 ruling, Q3): camera capture on Checks and Defects, a private Storage bucket with signed-URL reads, upload UI, and storage RLS. Deferred as a bundle because it is the only part of the Visit feature needing new Storage infrastructure, and a defect note without a photo is still worth having. The Visits v1 UI omits the affordance rather than showing a control that does nothing.
- **Rubric-derived Visit checklist items** — generating a Check or Question from each custom Rubric Criterion, so a hunt's stated obsessions appear on the tour automatically. Explicitly deferred at ruling time (R4): the built-in template ships fixed, with per-Visit custom items as the manual path. Also deferred alongside it: **hunt-wide recurring custom items** (Q6 — a custom item is per-Visit in v1).
- **Custom-Criterion VISION and web-search producers.** P3-10 v1 classifies these routes but refuses confirmation. Arbitrary VISION needs a criterion-specific reference/evaluation contract rather than borrowing kitchen-quality anchors, and workflow web search remains exclusive to DISCOVER until a non-loop scoped-search producer has its own security and cost contract.
- **Buy-domain support (house hunting / purchasing).** The `domain` seams exist ([§8.2](#82-tables)); the engine, pipeline, and collaboration layer are domain-blind, and unit groups degrade gracefully to single-plan houses. What activating it requires: a buy catalog slice (price, taxes, HOA, insurance, roof/HVAC age, lot, schools, price history), a PITI-style cost composer taking hunt-level financing settings (down payment, rate, term — user inputs, not extractions), listing-status transitions (pending/sold, price cuts), and — critically — Tier-3 fetching from day one, since individual homes have no official site and the canonical source *is* the hostile aggregator. Keep `shared/` free of rental assumptions (cost composition behind an interface) so this stays an addition, not a rewrite.

---

## 19. Implementation Phases

**Phase 0 — Prove the pipeline (CLI, no UI, no auth).**
Monorepo scaffold; `shared/` domain models + catalog seed + scoring engine with tests; migrations; CLI `ingest <url>` running VALIDATE_URL → FETCH(T1–T3) → VALIDATE → EXTRACT → VERIFY → SCORE against a hardcoded rubric, printing the breakdown. Run against 10 real listings (trimmed from ~20 — [§20](#20-decision-log) 2026-07-17), **hand-labeled first**: ground-truth values for every gate-bearing criterion (and rent/fees) per bench listing, stored as `fixtures/bench/labels/` (a **local eval asset** alongside the corpus snapshots it describes — gitignored and backed up, never repo content; [§20](#20-decision-log) v2.8) — accuracy metrics are meaningless without them (labeling ran far past the original one-evening estimate — hence the trim). For `availability_date`, label an explicit Property/Floor Plan ISO date when present (including in `[EMBEDDED DATA]`); label `"available_now"` only for phrase-only immediate wording — the harness resolves that sentinel to the UTC date of the listing's corpus `meta.json.saved_at`, not the bench run day. The hardcoded Phase 0 rubric is Yusuf's real criteria (2 br, in-unit laundry, balcony, cats allowed, all-in < $2,000 conservative), checked in as a fixture. Also: **Langfuse tracing wired from the first LLM call** (NFR6), the **eval harness skeleton** (FR11 — it doubles as the bench runner), the **model bench** ([§11.2](#112-model-roles-and-candidates)), and the **hostile-domain census** (which target-market sources demand Tier 3?).
*Exit criteria:* extraction verification pass-rate acceptable on gate-bearing criteria; fetch tier requirements known per relevant domain; per-stage model choices settled; per-listing cost measured. **Decision gate:** ruled early, 2026-07-17 ([§20](#20-decision-log)) — tier 3 stays and Bright Data remains the provider (free plan) regardless of bench outcome; the Apify actor path stays deferred. The last Phase 0 decision — the model pin — ruled 2026-07-21 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): EXTRACT and VERIFY moved to `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` on bench evidence (the benched pair only); the other workhorse stages and the TASTE tier stay on their Anthropic baselines.

**Phase 1 — Replace the spreadsheet (single user).**
API + queue + worker (in-process loop acceptable here); Overview table, detail panel, rubric builder, overrides, fees checklist, Tasks Active tab. Supabase Auth for one user.
*Exit:* Yusuf's real hunt runs here, spreadsheet retired.

**Phase 2 — Collaboration.**
RLS policies + permissions matrix (Curator role), invites, realtime sync, comments, ratings, colors, Tasks History tab.
*Exit:* second real user active; permissions verified at the RLS layer by tests.
*Exited 2026-07-18 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): both criteria met — partner active in the real hunt, §4.2 matrix green in CI. P2-12 (permission-aware UI) remains an open follow-up, explicitly not an exit condition.*

**Phase 3 — Full agent system.**
Planner manifests; DISCOVER slate + official-link capture, RECONCILE family-deduped ladder + bounded escalation; VISION with reference set; Maps/reviews/safety ENRICH; utility baselines job; custom-criteria routing; checkpoints incl. 24 h auto-resume; refresh TTLs + hash gating; compare view; property contact retrieval (P3-21 — leasing phone + contact URL by provenance precedence, §20 2026-07-27); optional worker-process isolation if the §5 operational triggers are observed; mobile sheet polish. Tier-3 adapters if — and only as much as — the Phase 0 gate demands.
The scoped-Criteria work is a distinct `P3-SC1`…`P3-SC8` series rather than renumbering established Phase 3 tasks. Two branches may proceed independently after P3-SC1: existing P3-5 DISCOVER, and P3-SC2 scoped persistence → P3-SC3 Property Catalog/dev Rubric → P3-SC4 migration/bench. They join at existing P3-6, which must reconcile the final Property/Floor Plan identity and therefore depends on both P3-5 and P3-SC4. P3-SC5 Floor Plan detail/diagrams depends on P3-SC2 and P3-7a but does not block P3-6; P3-SC6 objective unit features and P3-SC7 flooring follow P3-6; P3-SC8 move-in cost follows stable scoped cost inputs. `IMPLEMENTATION.md` owns the complete task table and mechanics.
P3-7a's image/Storage/hash-gating substrate landed 2026-07-13 ahead of the human reference asset; VISION ratings remain disabled until the P3-7b guide's approval and bench gates pass.
P3-8 landed 2026-07-18 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): ENRICH live with grocery proximity + Places ratings stage 1; `location_safety` ships as the override-first A+–F placeholder pending the P3-17 module.
P3-9 landed 2026-07-18 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): full §9.5 composition + utility-baselines job on the new scheduler-tick scaffold (P3-11/P3-12 extend it); its existing search-less baseline producer is unchanged by P3-5 and may adopt the shared search seam only in a separately tested follow-up.
P3-22 landed 2026-08-10 ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.75): hybrid email ownership (Supabase Auth for Auth/security, Manzil + Resend HTTPS for product/Hunt mail), a durable private outbox and signed webhooks, deterministic event recipients/defaults, account and per-Hunt Alerts surfaces, invite delivery status/resend, and the clay Tasks attention count.
P3-5 landed 2026-07-21 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): DISCOVER uses OpenRouter native web search, persists official/candidate Source links, builds the policy-capped tier/family slate, exposes the reasoned single-source badge, and supports atomic Owner/submitter policy edits. P3-SC2 landed 2026-07-21: the old Property/Criterion latest-row identity has been replaced by the scoped append-only substrate, centralized current views, Source-local Floor Plan identity, and scoped Override path. P3-SC3 landed 2026-07-22: the Property Catalog tranche, strict array/set scoring and Rubric path, persisted Floor Plan `unit_types`, Property-versus-Floor-Plan presentation, and guarded versioned development Rubric are live. P3-SC4's schema, prompt, deterministic composition, persistence, UI/filter, synthetic test, and bench-tooling implementation landed 2026-07-27. On 2026-07-28 the Owner explicitly waived its human canonical-ten/current-pin acceptance tail as a prerequisite for P3-6; the missing labels and zero-wrong-exact/Gate report remain technical debt and are not represented as passed evidence.
P3-6 landed 2026-07-28 under that waiver: the slate now fans through isolated per-Source FETCH/EXTRACT/VERIFY, scoped RECONCILE applies the family-deduped bounded ladder, dynamic escalation persists in the manifest, every resolved Extraction retains candidate edges, Source-authoritative retirement is local, `split_property` recomputes mixed graphs on both sides, and disagreement provenance/Problematic state is visible. The separately recorded scoped-label and reconciliation-model evidence remains technical debt.
P3-13's compare half plus hunt-wide filters (P3-18) landed 2026-07-19 ([§20](#20-decision-log)); mobile exploratory acceptance completed 2026-08-02. Live acceptance on 2026-08-02 also exercised a full ingest through tiers 1–3, a Detroit-metro ENRICH run, and a custom Criterion; the Owner subsequently confirmed P3-10 and all five P3-14 Tier-3 domains. P3-10's live acceptance is manual rather than programmatic.
*Formally entered 2026-07-18 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): the IMPLEMENTATION §7 table is finalized at entry per the 2026-07-11 plan — P3-2/P3-3/P3-4 and the P3-7a substrate landed ahead of entry; P3-14 scoped by the census verdict; new LLM stages ride existing pins until the P0-14 model-pin verdict.*
*Exit:* NFR1–NFR4 measured and met on the live hunt.

**Visits workstream (`VC-0`…`VC-8`) — parallel to Phase 3, gating nothing.** Ruled 2026-07-30 ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.30): the Visit Checklist ([§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist)) is a new product capability rather than agent-system work, so it is **not** renumbered into the Phase 3 table and is **not** a Phase 3 exit condition. It depends on no pipeline stage — and in v1 it is the first substantial subsystem with no LLM surface at all, a property that ends if the deferred voice-note transcription ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)) is ever built — and Phase 3's exit criteria (NFR1–NFR4 on the live hunt) are unaffected by its state. `VC-0` is design ratification (this section, [§3](#3-glossary-and-domain-model), [§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions), [§8.2](#82-tables)–[§8.3](#83-rls-strategy), [§9.7](#97-visits-and-the-visit-checklist), [§13.1](#131-routes)–[§13.3](#133-realtime), [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog), [§20](#20-decision-log)) and must land before any code, since DESIGN.md owns intent. `VC-1`…`VC-8` then run foundations → Visits tab → checklist runtime → defects → collaboration/presence → local-first sync → money/proposals → Overview and drawer surfacing. Visit photos are a separate **Visits phase 2** ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)). `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §7 owns the task table and acceptance evidence.

**Admin workstream (`AD-G`, `AD-C`, `AD-0`…`AD-5`) — parallel to Phase 3, gating nothing.** Ruled 2026-08-01 ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.37–v3.39): the operator surface — Site Admin identity, user/Hunt/Job management, the feedback inbox, and cost visibility — is a separate workstream for the same reason Visits is. It is not agent-system work, it is not renumbered into the Phase 3 table, and it is **not** a Phase 3 exit condition. Two slices deliberately ran ahead of design ratification because they stand alone and fix present-tense defects rather than building the panel: `AD-C` (tier-3 spend priced and folded into `cost_actual_usd`, per-stage cost persisted) and `AD-G` (the four places history was being destroyed — fee values, role changes, Listing curation, and deletions). `AD-0` is design ratification (this section, [§3](#3-glossary-and-domain-model), [§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions), [§8.2](#82-tables)–[§8.3](#83-rls-strategy), [§20](#20-decision-log)). `AD-1`…`AD-5` then run admin identity + audit log → panel shell and feedback inbox → People → Hunts and the ghost view → Jobs and Costs, with the hunt activity feed (`AD-F`) landing before `AD-1` because it is a pure derived read over `AD-G`'s tables. Enforcing spend caps, true impersonation, and a general-purpose all-actions audit log are all ruled **out** of v1 ([§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)). `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §7 owns the task table and acceptance evidence.

**Pre-production readiness (`PR-1`) — gates production deployment, not Phase 3 exit.** Ruled 2026-08-02 ([§20](#20-decision-log)): public self-registration is removed before production. A Site Admin provisions every new account through the existing auditable People surface; password setup/recovery is the enrollment path. Magic links and OTPs remain available only to authenticate an existing account, never to create one. `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §7 owns the implementation and deployment acceptance checks.

**Learning Track (parallel, never blocking).** Gating rule: an L-milestone starts only after the shipping milestone it depends on is green, and must never delay the next shipping milestone — **the lease deadline wins every conflict** (R11).

- **L0** (inside Phase 0): Langfuse wired; eval harness running the bench set in workflow mode.
- **L1** (after Phase 0 exit): extractor + critic A/B on the bench — measure what it catches beyond deterministic VERIFY; keep or kill on data.
- **L2** (after Phase 1 exit): agents-mode pipeline as a LangGraph graph — orchestrator–workers + triage routing; first full dual-mode eval report.
- **L3** (after Phase 2 exit): checkpoint port to LangGraph interrupt/checkpointer; comparison against the hand-rolled runner written up.
- **L4** (any time after Phase 1): the investigator crew (FR12).

*Track exit:* the written eval report in `docs/` — accuracy, tokens, latency, and a verdict on what multi-agent bought — which is the actual deliverable of the learning objective ([§2.5](#25-learning-objectives)).

(c) Match semantics: inclusive `range`; `lt`/`gt`/`range` on numbers or ISO-date strings; object values compare on `rating`; unmatched known values contribute 0; type mismatches never raise. (d) Migration granularity: enums created by the first migration needing them; catalog vocabulary columns are text + check, not enum types; `extractions.criterion_key` has no FK (custom keys aren't catalog rows) and `extractions.hunt_id` gains its FK in 0002. Also fixed the §9.3 example's arithmetic (total 8.5 → 9.5) | A gate that missing data can satisfy is no gate; conservative plan values enforce the never-look-cheaper principle; enum types would put catalog vocabulary behind migrations for zero safety gain | §3, §8.1, §9.3 |
| 2026-07-06 | v2.2: **Source Policy** added — per-submission cross-check control (`trust_link` \| `tier_1` \| `tiers_1_2` \| `tiers_1_2_3` default \| `tier_1_plus_official`), defaulted from hunt settings, persisted on `hunt_listings`, recorded in the plan manifest. Constrains discovered sibling sources only (the submitted URL is always fetched at its required tier); over-cap siblings are skipped at plan time, never escalated; `trust_link` runs single-source with a permanent badge and a documented R3/R4 posture change. The deterministic truth layer is untouched under every policy | User control over the cost/latency/assurance trade per link; a trusted official-site link shouldn't force aggregator scraping, and the choice must stay visible exactly where it weakens the trust story | §3, §4.1, §5.1, §8.2, §10.3, §10.4, §10.7, §13.2, §16 |
| 2026-07-06 | v2.3: **hunt settings contract pinned** (§8.2) — the four settings the design already implied, gathered into one Owner-edited, API-validated object: `default_source_policy` (§10.7), `cost_estimate_mode` (§9.5), `min_confidence` (§9.3, default `medium`), `proximity_mode` (§8.2 catalog). Edit effects specified per key: scoring inputs (`cost_estimate_mode`, `min_confidence`) reuse the rubric-mutation path — version bump + free rescore; `proximity_mode` triggers a field-scoped location refresh; `default_source_policy` affects future submissions only. "Edit hunt settings" added to the §4.2 matrix (Owner-only); settings panel added to the §13.1 settings route. No speculative settings added — every key is read by machinery already in the design | Four subsystems read this object, so its shape is design, not implementation (the v1.3 pinning standard); routing scoring-affecting edits through `rubric_version` keeps score provenance honest — a stored breakdown is fully explained by its version | §4.2, §5.1, §8.2, §9.2, §9.3, §9.5, §13.1, §20 |
| 2026-07-06 | v2.4: **VALIDATE split in two**, resolving the ordering conflict P0-8/10 implementation surfaced (the old diagram put VALIDATE before FETCH, but "is this a rental listing page?" needs fetched content). **VALIDATE_URL** — deterministic P2, pre-fetch, in the old diagram position: scheme + public host (private/loopback refused, an SSRF guard per §16) + not-a-binary, and the single place URL normalization (whitespace, fragments) happens. **VALIDATE** — the content judgment (heuristics first, LLM confirm), now explicitly after FETCH of the submitted source. Phase 0 spine order pinned: VALIDATE_URL → FETCH → VALIDATE → EXTRACT → VERIFY → SCORE | A URL-shaped sanity check costs nothing and belongs before any fetch; a content judgment cannot precede the content — splitting the stage lets both truths hold instead of picking one | §10.1, §10.2, §10.3, §16, §19 |
| 2026-07-07 | v2.5: **Tier 3 built ahead of the census gate, free plans only.** The 2026-06-28 deferral is partially reversed: the managed-unblocker fetcher exists now (Bright Data Web Unlocker default, ScrapingBee alternate, swappable via `MANZIL_TIER3_PROVIDER`, off the ladder until a key is set), but strictly on vendor free tiers (~5k req/mo Bright Data) — **moving to a paid plan is a new §20 decision**, not a config change. What changed since the deferral: the ladder/registry/classifier infrastructure made the fetcher a half-day adapter; free tiers cover Phase 0 volumes entirely, deleting the per-request-cost argument; the census gains a definitive tier-3 column (`hostile_unfetchable` vs `tier3_ok`), turning the P0-14 gate from "build or not" into "keep, and which provider". The Apify structured-actor path stays deferred (second data path, own decision). Stopgap added alongside: FETCH's `source unfetchable` error now carries a deterministic URL-slug search hint (zero LLM) so a human can find the same property on a fetchable source — the manual stand-in for DISCOVER (P3-5) | The infra investment changed the cost of building it, and free tiers changed the cost of running it; capping at free plans preserves the original economic judgment while removing the census blind spot (we can now *measure* whether unblockers beat the hostile five instead of guessing) | §10.7, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-07 | v2.6: **Embedded structured data mined into the cleaned text; positive content outranks the JS-shell heuristic.** Corpus reality (16 pages, 10 domains): listing sites server-render their *data* even when they don't render their DOM — JSON-LD blocks plus framework state blobs (`__NEXT_DATA__`, `window.__PRELOADED_STATE__`, bare-JSON state scripts) carry the floor plans, unit prices, sqft and fees that never reached tier-1 cleaned text. A deterministic, zero-LLM miner (`fetching/structured.py`) now parses those script tags and appends a pruned digest under `[EMBEDDED DATA]` in the cleaned text; EXTRACT's contract is unchanged (it still receives one text blob). Pruning is list-driven, drop-before-keep: `similar`/`nearby` subtrees are dropped **first** because they carry *another property's* prices (contamination, not just noise); media/URL bulk is scrubbed; digest capped at `EMBEDDED_DATA_MAX_CHARS` (40k). Classifier consequence: the positive cleaned-text check (now also matching rental-fact JSON keys like `"priceLow":`) runs **before** the JS-shell signature, so empty-DOM-but-data-shipping pages settle at tier 1 instead of escalating. JS object literals (unquoted keys, e.g. zumper's inline state) are unminable by design — no JS evaluation, ever; those sites still contribute via JSON-LD | The data was *right there* in every saved page; mining it is a cleaner concern (the stored artifact is cleaned text, §8.2), costs zero LLM calls, fixes tier-1 pages whose rendered text lacked prices, and demotes shell verdicts that were pure DOM-emptiness artifacts. Rejected alternative: per-site JSON adapters — a schema-mapping treadmill; the generic key-signal pruner is domain-blind and fails soft to the old behavior when nothing parses | §7, §10.7, §20 |
| 2026-07-07 | v2.7: **OpenRouter as sole LLM gateway; direct vendor SDKs dropped.** Supersedes the 2026-06-30 all-Anthropic decision in practice: the Gemini adapter (P0-13 bench prerequisite) had already eroded the "one bill, one SDK" argument. OpenRouter restores it — one `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, one `openai` SDK, model swaps are config string changes (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, `google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite`, …). Upstream provider pinning preserves deterministic routing and cache stickiness. What's lost: Anthropic Message Batches API (−50% on non-interactive work) — new-listing ingestion is latency-bound (NFR2), hash gating zeroes unchanged refreshes, realistic hunt savings were ~$1–3; NFR1 still holds. Prompt caching passthrough (`cache_control` on stable prefixes) remains load-bearing. ~5.5% OpenRouter fee is cents at this scale | Operational simplicity wins again, without giving up the bench's ability to sweep Gemini candidates; batch was a future Phase 3 lever that never justified maintaining two direct SDKs | §11, §12, §15, §20 |
| 2026-07-07 | v2.8: **The bench/eval kit is a local asset, never repo content.** `fixtures/corpus/` (scraped listing pages) and `fixtures/bench/labels/` (hand labels about those exact snapshots) are gitignored; only `.gitkeep` placeholders are tracked so fresh clones keep the directories and the corpus sweep test skips cleanly (`corpus_pages()` hardened to return `[]` when the dir is absent). `bench/manifest.md` (methodology, no page content) and the smoke replay fixture stay tracked; other record-mode artifacts are ignored (keyed to local corpus content, carry page evidence quotes). CI provably reads only the committed synthetic `fixtures/pages/`. Consequences accepted and mitigated: bench reproducibility is single-machine, and snapshots of since-delisted or hostile pages are unrecoverable — so the kit gets an explicit backup runbook (IMPLEMENTATION §8) and the P0-11 exit metric is reworded from "present locally + backed up"; P1-1's dev-seed retargets the committed synthetic pages.
