Also **reviewed and reaffirmed unchanged**: the 2026-06-28 cleaned-text retention line (facts in `extractions`, gzipped cleaned text in Storage, raw HTML transient) — facts answer *what we believe*, cleaned text answers *what the page said*, and dropping the text layer would collapse every re-extraction (model migrations, mid-hunt custom criteria, evidence audits) into a refetch against sites that block and listings that vanish. The repo decision is orthogonal to that production layer: test corpus in git vs cleaned text in the user's RLS-scoped Supabase are different content, different stores, different exposure | Saved pages are copyrighted third-party content that embeds vendor API keys and tracking IDs (observed in the saved corpus) — unpublishable in an open repo regardless of size; and the labels are meaningless without the exact snapshots they grade, so they travel together. Keeping the kit out of git costs only single-machine reproducibility, which the backup runbook bounds | §6, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **Dark mode shipped (Phase 1 UI polish).** Mantine color scheme with `auto` default (system preference); header toggle on hunt routes and public routes (login, hunt switcher); explicit choice persisted in `localStorage` (`manzil-color-scheme`); pre-mount inline script prevents flash. Styling stays Mantine-token-based in both schemes (`theme.ts`: `autoContrast`, `primaryShade`). | Fulfills the §7 rationale for choosing Mantine; personal hunt tool used at night; no bespoke CSS layer | §13, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **P1-14 owns `confirm_value` escalation.** Closes the 2.0.6 deferral ("activates in Phase 1 when jobs/waiting_user exist") with an explicit task between P1-13 (Tasks UI) and P1-15 (exit review). Phase 1 scope: gate-relevant VERIFY demotions below `min_confidence` only; one checkpoint per ingest job; manual answer via existing API/Tasks path. `resolve_dedupe`/`resolve_dispute` and the 24h auto-resume scheduler remain deferred. Phase 1 exit review renumbered P1-15. | The deferral left no owner once jobs existed; pinning ownership before exit review ensures suspect gate values get a human path back up instead of silently firing gates | §10.5, §10.10, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **`hunts.created_at` added** (migration 0003). The `hunts` row gains a UTC `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`; it was omitted from migration 0002 even though `HuntResponse.created_at` already exposed it and the hunt switcher orders the list newest-first by it. The missing column made the frontend `useHunts` read error (`42703 column hunts.created_at does not exist`), surfacing as an error right after hunt creation (the create POST succeeded; the post-create list refetch failed). Also aligned the frontend read models to the DB: `Score` and `FeeEntry` had spurious `id` fields — both tables use composite PKs (`scores`: hunt_listing_id+floor_plan_id; `fee_checklist`: hunt_listing_id+fee_slot) with no `id` column — now removed. | A creation timestamp is a natural, low-cost field the API already promised; per-hunt siblings (`hunt_listings`, `overrides`, …) all carry one. Aligning the hand-typed frontend models to the authoritative §8.2 schema removes latent `undefined`-as-string lies | §8.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **Schema-alignment batch** (migration 0004), from the schema review. (A) **`jobs.hunt_id`** — direct hunt scoping so the Tasks query is one indexed predicate (was: list all listing ids `IN` + a separate jsonb `payload` scan for rescore jobs) and P2-1 RLS need not reach through jsonb; nullable in Phase 1 (app always sets it), NOT-NULL rides with P2-1. (B) **`hunt_listings.unavailable_at`** — "no available floor plans (even after cross-validation)" becomes a first-class **dimmed, null-score** listing state, distinct from an error; the worker set `raise StageFatal` there, now it persists the source/extractions, marks the listing, and completes `done`. Reversible on refresh; orthogonal to `status`. Frontend Overview renders one dimmed row (was: a plan-less listing produced zero rows and vanished), null score sorts last. (C) **`floor_plans` natural unique key** `(source_id, plan_name, beds, baths)` — the worker now upserts plans so a refresh keeps a stable `floor_plan_id`, instead of inserting duplicates that orphan `scores` and dangle `pins`. | Every job belongs to a hunt, so scoping through a jsonb payload was accidental complexity; an empty availability result is a legitimate answer that must not read as a failure or vanish from the table; and a refresh that re-mints plan ids silently detaches scores and pins — the natural key makes re-ingest idempotent | §8.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **jobs.hunt_id tightened to NOT NULL** (migration 0005), reversing 0004's deferral to P2-1. Both enqueue paths already set it and 0004 backfills legacy rows, but the constraint immediately surfaced a third job creator the enqueue-path check missed: the P1-1 dev seed (`scripts/dev_seed.py`), plus several worker test fixtures inserting `jobs` rows directly via raw SQL — all brought in line in the same change. `_assert_job_owner`'s null branch removed as dead code. | The constraint IS the enforcement — deferring NOT NULL until RLS enforces the same invariant only leaves a window for a future job creator to silently omit it; a hypothetical hunt-less job type is YAGNI | §8.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **Ops batch — dead-letter cap, job metrics, fee/plan write fixes, unwired-columns audit** (schema-review leftovers; migration 0006). (1) **Dead-letter cap** `MANZIL_JOB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5): orphan reclaim caps re-queues — a `running` job reclaimed at/over the cap is dead-lettered to `failed` (`error='dead-lettered: orphaned after N attempts'`, `finished_at` set) in the same atomic sweep, below it it re-queues unchanged; a **manual retry resets `attempts=0`** (a human retry grants a fresh cycle budget). The cap lives on the reclaim side, not the dispatch side, because an orphan is the crash-loop signal — a job that keeps dying mid-run without ever failing cleanly. (2) **`jobs.started_at`** (migration 0006) — nullable ops-metrics column, stamped on first claim only (`coalesce(started_at, now())`), splitting queue-wait from run-time; kept off the API/frontend. (3) **`fee_checklist.updated_at` bumped on re-entry** — the upsert set it explicitly (the INSERT default froze it at first write). (4) **`floor_plans.raw` wired** — the ingest projection persists the per-plan extracted object (`FloorPlanIn`) as jsonb provenance. (5) **Unwired-columns audit**: `extractions.resolution_rule` is legitimately null until the RECONCILE multi-source stage exists — §10.6's ladder is the only writer, RECONCILE is single-source-trivial in Phase 1 and its stage is P3-6 (the §10.7 `single_source` value is what RECONCILE emits *when it runs*, so writing it from the projection would forward-implement a deferred feature). Deliberately deferred with reasons: `properties.place_id/lat/lng` (needs geocoding — arrives with location-type criteria), `property_sources.image_urls`/`screenshot_path` (vision/checkpoint-screenshot features unbuilt), `property_sources.is_official` (no reliable signal until DISCOVER/source classification), `property_sources.cleaned_text_path` (Postgres mode persists `cleaned_text_hash` only; cleaned text is not written to disk in Postgres mode — the path was the file-persistence era, and the gzipped-cleaned-text-in-Storage artifact is itself a future feature, so no stable path exists to wire). | A crash loop must not churn the queue forever, yet an operator's explicit retry deserves a clean budget; per-job timing needs a claim stamp that survives resumes; a re-entered fee with a frozen timestamp lies about freshness; a floor plan's raw extract is the provenance re-extraction needs; and an honest audit distinguishes "null because the producing stage is deferred" from "forgot to wire" — each unwired column is one or the other, recorded so future work knows which | §8.2, §10.6, §10.7, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **Visual identity: "Dusk & clay" (Phase 1 UI character pass).** Primary recast from stock Mantine indigo to a muted dusk indigo (`dusk` scale); warm neutrals throughout (stone `gray`, warm-paper light body via `cssVariablesResolver`, warm-charcoal `dark` scale); new `clay` accent takes `semantic.waiting`; status hues re-tuned earthy (moss/olive/ochre/earthen red) under unchanged scale names, so `semantic` roles and component code are untouched. Typography: Literata (serif) headings + wordmark, Source Sans 3 (sans) body, self-hosted via `@fontsource-variable`; tabular numerals on tables and badges. Badges drop all-caps; overlays gain gentle transitions (`respectReducedMotion` on); microcopy pass gives empty/loading/error states a plain-spoken warm voice. All inside `theme.ts` + `main.tsx` — no bespoke CSS files, no layout or contract changes. | The UI was polished but read as generic SaaS defaults; *manzil* (home / a stage of a journey) deserves warmth. Token-level identity work is exactly what the single theme seam exists for | §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-09 | **Rubric surface: view-default + single-page editor; Overview filter bar.** Rubric route drops the 3-step `Stepper` wizard — steps 1–2 were the same `CriterionCard` with different filtering, and the review step duplicated inline validation. Default landing is read-only view (criterion cards + options table); Owner edits on one page; first-run hunts open directly in edit mode (pre-builds the Phase 2 member-read-only surface). Overview gains an extensible filter bar (active Pills + expandable panel) with rent and sqft bounds alongside `hide score < N`. | User-requested UX consolidation; view mode is the natural read surface for members anyway | §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-10 | **Rubric view option rows: quieter labels and plain points.** Read-only option rows drop badge chrome for deltas (green/red/dimmed text keeps sign semantics); dealbreaker and gate set-scores become danger-colored text. Match labels omit assumed operators — `eq` shows the value only ("2 br", not "equals 2 br"), `bool` shows "yes"/"no" only, `lt`/`gt` use `<`/`>`; `between` and `any of` stay verbal. Editor operator dropdowns unchanged. | Scan table reads faster when the default case needs no word and inequalities are one glyph | §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-10 | **Extraction-confidence batch: cleaned text persisted; evidence check fragment-aware; extract prompt v3.** (1) `property_sources.cleaned_text` (migration 20260713000000) — the run's cleaned text lands as a plain column, superseding the gzipped-Storage artifact plan at Phase-1 scale (KB per page); debugging "why is this low confidence" requires seeing exactly what the model saw. (2) VERIFY check 1 accepts ellipsis-stitched quotes: each verbatim fragment must locate independently (fuzzy, then whitespace-stripped retry for compact-JSON spacing). Production runs showed models stitching legitimate fragments ('Cats Allowed... Dogs Allowed'; JSON field triples) and being falsely demoted — the dominant source of bogus `low`. Bounded loosening, adversarially verified: absent facts still demote. (3) Extract prompt v3 codifies fragment-stitching rules and bans reformatted floor-plan summary quotes. **Bench rerun still owed on the prompt change** (§5 testing rule) — pending P0-11 labels. *Addendum, same day: a Storage-vs-column assessment was requested; measured reality (avg 8.8 KB, max 22 KB per cleaned page — ~10–25 MB per thousand sources) plus the atomicity cost of moving writes outside the ingest transaction and signed-URL friction on every debugging read settled it — the column stays. Revisit trigger: MB-scale artifacts (raw HTML, screenshots, P3-7) or actual DB-size pressure; `cleaned_text_path` remains the seam for that move.* | The evidence audit exists to catch fabrication, not formatting; every false demotion erodes trust in the real ones. Persisting cleaned text turns confidence debugging from re-fetching guesswork into a column read | §8.2, §10.5, §20 |
| 2026-07-10 | **§9.5 v1 slice: species-shaped pet costs, household settings, utilities-included extraction, composition v1.** Pages frequently price pets per species ("cat rent $35, dog rent $50") — a bare `pet_rent` concept extracts nothing there, which is exactly how pet rent went missing from live runs. `pet_costs` gains `cat_rent_monthly`/`dog_rent_monthly`/`pet_rent_monthly` (generic, used for either species when the specific figure is absent); hunt settings gain `cats`/`dogs` (scoring inputs on the rubric-mutation rescore path) and `occupants` (reserved for P3-9 utility scaling — named now so the pinned shape churns once). Composition v1: `all_in = conservative rent + cats×(cat_rent ?? generic) + dogs×(dog_rent ?? generic)`; unstated rents contribute 0 and stay visible as unfilled `pet_rent_cat`/`pet_rent_dog` checklist slots; ingest composes from the extraction, rescore from slot amounts — so manual fee entries and household edits genuinely re-score. `utilities_included` extracts as an enum list landing in append-only `extractions` (`hunt_id` NULL). Both new extraction blocks are optional-with-default like `property_identity`. Mandatory fees + utility estimation stay P3-9. | The all-in number is the tool's core promise; every component it can honestly know now, it should know now — and the species split is forced by how listings actually write their pet pricing, not by taxonomy enthusiasm | §8.2, §9.5, §20 |
| 2026-07-10 | **Ratings source ladder (§10.12): Places first, apartmentratings.com second, general sites deferred.** Property reputation gathering is staged instead of built at once. Stage 1 — Google Places rating + review synthesis (structured, near-free, geocode-keyed cache) lands with the Maps tooling as the priority slice of ENRICH (P3-8). Stage 2 — apartmentratings.com as a dedicated second source (P3-15), appending a provenance-carrying `management_reviews` extraction; RECONCILE owns disagreement, no bespoke blending. Stage 3 — general ratings sites (Yelp et al.) go to §18, revisited only on demonstrated gaps. The ladder is additive per source; downstream shapes never change when a stage arrives. | Places covers nearly every complex for pennies before any scraping is attempted; renter-specific sites add signal but cost a lookup + fetch + extraction pipeline; general sites are hostile surfaces with duplicate signal — spend follows evidence of need | §10.3, §10.12, §18, §19 |
| 2026-07-10 | **Property identity rides EXTRACT as a non-catalog block; `properties` refreshed at ingest persist.** Submit-time `properties` rows carried their URL-slug placeholder name/address forever. EXTRACT's schema gains an optional `property_identity` block (name, canonical address, official URL — copied from the page, never invented), following the `floor_plans` precedent: same single structured call, zero added LLM spend. The ingest projection updates the row with non-null-wins coalesce — Phase 1 single-source last-write-wins; DEDUPE/RECONCILE own identity once multi-source arrives (P3), and extracted name+address is exactly the input DEDUPE needs. Identity is unscored display metadata, deliberately NOT a catalog entry: no option deltas, not rubric-editable, outside the scoring engine and VERIFY's gates. The block is optional-with-default so recorded LLM fixtures and stored RunState snapshots that predate it keep validating. `place_id`/`lat`/`lng`, `image_urls`, `is_official` remain deferred per the 2026-07-09 audit. | The catalog is the pinned contract for *scoreable criteria* — identity has no delta semantics and would render as nonsense rubric cards; a sibling non-catalog block fixes the placeholder bug now and feeds the designed DEDUPE input instead of fighting it | §8.2, §10.3, §20 |
| 2026-07-10 | **Overview filter bar: beds/baths bounds.** Extends the §13.2 filter registry with `minBeds`/`maxBeds` (integer, studio = 0) and `minBaths`/`maxBaths` (0.5 steps). Unit-group scalars compared inclusively; pending / no-group rows still pass (same posture as rent/sqft). | Beds/baths are the primary Unit Group identity — filtering them is the natural next bound after rent and sqft | §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-11 | **RLS boundary activated for every table.** Per-hunt policies implement the §4.2 role matrix through `private.member_role(hunt_id)`; pre-RLS Hunts backfill their Owner membership before enforcement; a partial unique index guarantees one Owner per Hunt. Global facts remain authenticated-read/service-role-write, except hunt-scoped custom-criterion Extractions, which are visible only to that Hunt's members. Hunt SELECT additionally admits its authenticated `owner_id` so PostgREST can return the creation response before membership-backed visibility is available. | RLS, rather than frontend or API convention, must be the security boundary. The helper avoids membership-policy recursion; the backfill avoids locking out Phase 1 Hunts; custom facts are Hunt opinion and must not leak globally; the narrow Owner visibility branch is required for creation while the trigger atomically establishes canonical membership. | §4.2, §8.3, §16, §20 |
| 2026-07-11 | **Atomic constrained database capabilities.** An `AFTER INSERT` trigger installs every Hunt's canonical Owner membership in the same transaction. `public.submit_listing`, exposed because Supabase's Data API serves `public` rather than `private` but executable only by authenticated users, verifies Hunt membership and atomically creates the URL-derived placeholder Property, Listing, and ingest Job; `added_by` always comes from `auth.uid()`. `public.answer_job_checkpoint` independently verifies Owner/Curator/own-Listing authority and atomically transitions the parked Job while appending its Job Event. Direct client writes to global facts and Job Events remain forbidden. | Sequential PostgREST writes can violate required invariants or leave an action without its audit record. Narrow database-atomic capabilities enforce the complete mutation for every writer. Explicit function grants and derived caller identity keep the surface narrower than table write policies or another service-role API operation. | §5.1, §8.3, §9.1, §10.10, §16, §20 |
| 2026-07-11 | **Member colors support an ordered palette plus custom hex.** Automatic invite assignment uses `dusk, clay, moss, ochre, brick, olive, stone, plum` in order, choosing the first unused token. A member may later store any valid canonical `#RRGGBB` color instead. | Tokens provide deterministic, theme-aware defaults and visually distinct collaborators without setup; accepting hex values preserves user choice without reshaping `hunt_members.color` or coupling stored data to the current theme implementation. | §8.2, §9.1, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-11 | **Collaboration identity lives on Hunt membership; comment deletion is author-only.** `hunt_members.display_name` is the RLS-readable label for comments and rating tooltips, initialized from invite email when possible with a neutral fallback. Comments soft-delete only by their author; Owner moderation stays deferred. | Auth identities are not available to direct RLS-backed frontend reads, and privileged Auth lookups would turn ordinary rendering into a server dependency. A Hunt-scoped display name keeps collaboration reads direct. Author-only deletion matches the current permissions matrix without inventing an unrequested moderation power. | §4.2, §8.2, §9.1, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-11 | **v2.9: keep worker execution in-process by default; make P3-1 process/service isolation optional and evidence-triggered.** The `worker/` package, durable Postgres Job queue, resumable Stage contract, and `MANZIL_WORKER_INPROCESS` seam remain. Tier-2 Playwright already runs through the API lifespan loop. Split only after repeated API memory/restart/502 impact, material latency impact, a concurrency requirement, or an always-on scheduling requirement is observed. | This is a private two-person Hunt, so a second paid service and its operational surface are YAGNI without demonstrated contention. The durable queue preserves a low-risk migration path if browser load later requires isolation. | §5, §19; IMPLEMENTATION §7–8 |
| 2026-07-12 | **Manifest-driven runner; planner v1 deterministic-only (P3-2, landed ahead of formal Phase 3 entry).** The runner walks the plan manifest's stage list by name; PLAN is `stages[0]` and the cursor indexes that list. A RunState with no manifest — every pre-Phase-3 snapshot, including parked `waiting_user` jobs — resumes through the legacy fixed list, **selected at the dispatcher by resume state**, so an old integer cursor is never reinterpreted against the longer Phase 3 list (the off-by-one a review caught and a dispatcher-level regression test now pins). `jobs.plan` and `jobs.cost_actual_usd` are populated from the run (estimate vs actual visible per job). Planner v1 is fully deterministic: the §10.4 judgment call (ranking >3 candidate sources) stays unwired until DISCOVER can produce that many (P3-5); the skip rule — same-property source with fresh `cleaned_text_hash` within `PLAN_FRESH_TTL_HOURS` (24) → `skip: hash_fresh`, FETCH reads the persisted cleaned text. Cross-property reuse remains DEDUPE's job (P3-4). | Inserting Phase 3 stages into an index-cursor walk would mis-resume every parked job; a manifest that travels with the job means the stage set can evolve without reinterpreting history. Deterministic-first upholds the 2026-06-28 "planner = manifest builder, not open-ended agent" decision. | §10.2, §10.4, §14, §20 |
| 2026-07-12 | **Tool mechanics landed (P3-3): seam-enforced allow-lists, tool events, Maps forever-cache in existing columns; residual SSRF gated.** `@tool` registry + turn-budgeted loop per §10.2 (`AgentBudgetExceeded` is a real outcome); the per-stage allow-list table has exactly two loop-capable stages — DISCOVER and location-type custom criteria — and is enforced inside `call_agent`, so stage code cannot widen it and callers can only offer a subset of a stage's row; extraction stages provably resolve to zero tools (§16 control, test-pinned). Every executed tool call/result is a `tool_called` Job Event (2 kB result cap; a hallucinated unknown-tool call returns an error to the model without an event — an accepted gap to close with the first live loop). Maps: three plain-httpx tools (no Google SDK); the geocode forever-cache is `properties.place_id/lat/lng` (columns wired at last, closing the 2026-07-09 deferral) and computed distances/ratings land as ordinary Extractions — no parallel cache store. **Gated follow-up:** the URL-validation gaps shared with submission (public hostname resolving to a private IP; redirects followed without re-validation) become a read-SSRF surface once a model-controlled loop holds `fetch_page`, because poisoned page content could steer fetches; a fetcher-layer guard (post-DNS-resolution IP check + per-redirect re-validation, pipeline-wide) **must land before P3-5/P3-10 wire the first live tool loop**. Dormant today — no stage passes live fetchers to a loop. | One enforcement point in the seam beats per-stage discipline; caching in columns and rows the design already owns avoids a second cache store; naming the SSRF gate converts a silent parity assumption into a scheduled control instead of blocking substrate work on a fetch-path redesign. | §10.2, §12, §16, §17, §20 |
| 2026-07-12 | **SSRF gate closed for the HTTP fetch path; the fetcher, not URL validation, is the boundary.** One policy module (`fetching/ssrf.py`) resolves and screens every fetch before connecting: an IP-literal or any resolved address that is non-global (private, loopback, link-local — so cloud metadata `169.254.169.254` — multicast, reserved, unspecified, IPv6 ULA, v4-mapped/NAT64/6to4-embedded private, CGNAT, `0.0.0.0/8`) is refused, empty/failed resolution fails closed, and odd IPv4 encodings normalize through `getaddrinfo` to their real address. Tier 1 stops following redirects blindly — a manual capped loop (`FETCH_MAX_REDIRECTS` 5) re-screens each hop — and **pins the connection to the vetted resolved IP** while preserving `Host`/SNI and hostname certificate verification, which closes the DNS-rebinding TOCTOU: because there is no second `getaddrinfo` at connect time, the screened IP and the dialed IP are the same. `VALIDATE_URL` stays deterministic and literal-only (the resolving guard lives in the fetch layer, not the stage). **Residual, tracked not closed:** Tier 2 (Chromium owns its own DNS/connect) keeps a best-effort route-guard + post-load `page.url` backstop, so it carries a narrow rebinding window — a **manual browser smoke test is gated before any live loop wires Tier 2**, and one live-HTTPS smoke fetch should confirm the pin's cert path (unprovable under the mock transport used in CI). Tier 3 is provider-side (our internal hosts are unreachable from the vendor network) and screened pre-dispatch as defense-in-depth. | The read-SSRF trigger the P3-3 gate named becomes real the moment a model-controlled loop over untrusted page content can call `fetch_page`; re-resolving without pinning is *reliably* bypassable by the guard's own named adversary (a DNS-rebinding attacker), so pinning — not a second lookup — is what actually closes it. The boundary belongs at the fetcher because every path (`fetch_page` and user submission alike) funnels through it, and keeping `VALIDATE_URL` network-free preserves its zero-cost pre-fetch contract. | §10.3, §10.7, §16, §17, §20; IMPLEMENTATION §7 |
| 2026-07-12 | **DEDUPE + `resolve_dedupe` + `split_property` landed (P3-4, Wave B); DEDUPE runs after EXTRACT, correcting §10.1's diagram order.** DEDUPE consumes EXTRACT's `property_identity` block (the input built for it, 2026-07-10): geocode the extracted address — a plain function call, zero tools; the loop allow-list keeps exactly two rows — then match existing `properties` by place_id equality or <100 m haversine (`DEDUPE_MAX_DISTANCE_METERS`) plus rapidfuzz name similarity. In-range and ≥90 name-similar → auto-merge; gray zone (60–90 in range, or place_id-equal with dissimilar names — Google says same place, the pages disagree) → `resolve_dedupe` checkpoint, options `[merge, keep_separate]`, **default `keep_separate`**: the conservative direction, since a false split re-merges cheaply while a false merge poisons shared facts (R5). A merge switches the run to the canonical Property; the terminal projection (atomic with the DONE flip) re-points the Listing and any children off the submit-time placeholder, deletes the placeholder row, and persists the run's geocode into `properties.place_id/lat/lng` only-when-null (the forever-cache). Missing identity/address or a Maps failure never fails the run — it proceeds `kept_separate` with the reason recorded (a duplicate is recoverable; a dead run is not). A parked job that already merged resumes with the canonical Property preserved (the dispatcher seeds `property_id` only into fresh state — snapshots win). The reversal shipped the same day: `manzil split-property` (admin, service-role CLI) peels a Source onto a fresh placeholder Property with null geocode (the old fix may belong to the other building), re-points its Extractions/Floor Plans and the Listings derived from ingest-job URLs, and enqueues a rescore per affected Hunt. §10.1 had drawn DEDUPE before FETCH — unimplementable for a URL-only submission, whose name+address exist only after EXTRACT; the diagram now shows submitted-source extraction → DEDUPE → (P3-5) sibling fan-out. | Global fact sharing is only safe if two URLs for one building become one Property, and only survivable if a wrong merge reverses cleanly; identity resolution belongs on extracted identity, not the URL — exactly what the 2026-07-10 identity block was added to feed | §10.1, §10.3, §10.10, §17, §20 |
| 2026-07-12 | **v3.0: reusable Invitation Links are separate from email invites.** Invitation Links always grant Member, default to seven days with unlimited successful joins, expose editable expiration/limits and an append-only join ledger, and soft-delete irreversibly from the ordinary UI. Joining is one row-locked transaction: validity is checked before existing membership; only a newly inserted membership consumes a use; concurrent final-use requests cannot exceed the limit. Expired/exhausted links remain Owner-visible and editable, while deleted links remain stored but hidden and unusable. | Email invitations identify one recipient and may intentionally grant Curator; a shareable URL has different privilege and lifecycle risks. A separate resource keeps those contracts honest, least-privileged, auditable, and concurrency-safe. | §3, §4.1–4.2, §8.2–8.3, §9.1, §13.1, §16, §20 |
| 2026-07-12 | **v3.1: confirmed email/password auth plus OTP-capable magic links; account defaults with Hunt overrides.** Registration requires email confirmation; password recovery and the magic email's link/code paths stay inside Supabase Auth. Mandatory onboarding creates `user_profiles.default_display_name`; nullable `hunt_members.display_name` is the Hunt-specific override and effective identity coalesces override over default. | Password users need a complete recovery/confirmation lifecycle, and copying a default into memberships would prevent later default changes from propagating without overwriting deliberate Hunt identities. | §4.1, §5.1, §8.2–8.3, §9.1, §13.1, §20 |
| 2026-07-12 | **Invitation Links gain an optional Owner-facing `name`.** Nullable trimmed 1–80 character label on create/patch/response; blank normalizes to null; uniqueness is not enforced. UI may prefill `Invitation Link N` from the active-link count. | Owners need a human label when managing multiple links; join tokens and ledger semantics stay unchanged. | §8.2, §9.1, §13.1, §20 |
| 2026-07-13 | **v3.2: DISCOVER/RECONCILE reshaped — tier-diverse slate, official site demoted to stored link + escalation arbiter, syndication-family voting, bounded escalation ladder** (Yusuf-directed; rescopes P3-5/P3-6). Baseline extraction set becomes the **slate**: the submitted URL (permanent member — always extracted, votes like any source) plus one sibling per permitted census fetch tier other than the submitted domain's, each from a distinct **syndication family** (new census column); the official site is found, stored (`property_sources.is_official`), and displayed, but not fetched by default. Ladder v2: tolerance collapse takes the conservative in-band figure (was: official figure); supermajority = ≥2/3 family-deduped votes; unresolved **decision-relevant** fields (gate-bearing anywhere; any negative option/unknown delta; all-in components) escalate — official extraction as arbiter first, then one sibling round from DISCOVER's ranked candidate pool (activating §10.4's >3-candidate judgment call) — then majority (>50%, ties by verify confidence then freshness), then conservative fallback stored `disputed` (listing derives a **Problematic** badge; gate-bearing disputes still raise `resolve_dispute`). Escalation appends its stages to the manifest (persist-before-advance; recorded under `plan.escalation`), never reopens settled fields, and is capped at one official fetch + one sibling round — worst case ~7 extractions (~$0.20) against an unchanged ≤3 baseline. Single-sourced properties (DISCOVER finds nothing) reuse the `trust_link` badge machinery with reason `discover_exhausted`. Missing tiers (no census candidate, policy cap, tier-3 key absent) substitute from the next cheaper tier, recorded in the manifest. NFR2 gains a <5 min baseline-slate target | Official pages are the least standardized extraction surface — leading with them buys the flakiest extraction on every run, while aggregator pages are structurally uniform; yet a majority of aggregators can be one syndication feed echoed three times, so votes must count families, not domains — and the official site remains the highest-yield *single* fetch when the slate can't settle a field (fees often exist only there), hence arbiter-first escalation. Spend follows unresolved decision-relevant fields, not completeness; the user's submitted link is never second-guessed out of the slate | §3, §4.3, §10.1, §10.3, §10.4, §10.6, §10.7, §13.2, §14, §15, §16, §17, §19 |
| 2026-07-13 | **v3.3: P3-7 split into a fail-closed image substrate and an approval-gated VISION migration.** IMAGE_FETCH is a deterministic stage after canonical identity: discover listing images, SSRF-screen downloads, normalize to ≤1024 px WebP, de-duplicate/store by normalized-byte hash, and compare the complete current hash set. The manifest retains VISION in its ordered stages and the runner honors `plan.skipped` before dispatch; unchanged hashes therefore mean zero model calls without shifting a resume cursor. Until an approved manifest has 3–4 Yusuf-rated real references for every 1–5 level of both `kitchen_quality` and `flooring_quality` (photos may cover both), exact asset hashes, provenance, and matching prompt version, PLAN records `VISION: missing_reference_set` and ratings remain disabled. P3-7 completes only after the anchored bench (within ±1), assessment/Extraction projection, gallery, and retention cleanup pass the guide. | PLAN cannot know future image bytes before FETCH, and unanchored visual taste scores would turn model drift directly into rubric drift. Separating deterministic acquisition from model judgment permits useful, resume-safe work now while making the human consistency control a hard executable gate | §8.2, §10.1–10.4, §10.8, §14, §15, §19; `docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md` |
| 2026-07-13 | **Detail panel is a right `Drawer` on mobile and desktop** (replacing the mobile bottom sheet). Open/close use Mantine slide transitions; Overview keeps selection mounted until `onExited` so the exit animation can finish. | One consistent gesture across breakpoints; mounting with `opened` already true skipped the enter transition and clearing selection on close skipped the exit. Full-width right panel on narrow screens preserves usable detail real estate without a second layout pattern. | FR5, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-14 | **Cleaner gains a price-retention guard; extraction degrades trafilatura → readability → script-stripped visible text.** Root cause found on rentcafe: the page wraps its content in class `main-content-before-contact` — trafilatura's body-detection xpaths match `main-content` and flip it into strict paragraph extraction, which prunes every floor-plan card (facts live in `<b>`/`<span>` inside plain `<div>`s), while readability's negative-class regex matches `contact` and discards the same container; the JSON miner can't rescue it because rentcafe ships floor-plan data as HTML `data-*` attributes, not JSON. The guard: distinct price tokens (`$1,234`-style) are counted in the page's visible text (scripts/styles/noscript/svg/iframe stripped); an extraction layer keeping fewer than half of them is rejected and the cleaner falls to the next layer, ending at the visible-text dump. Corpus sweep: guard fires on 8/23 pages (rentcafe, zillow ×2, forrent, rent.com, trulia, apartmentguide, zumper — each verified to have genuinely lost price content), stays quiet on the rest. | The cleaned text is the single artifact EXTRACT, the outcome classifier, and VERIFY's evidence audit all read — an extraction that silently discarded every rendered price poisons all three, and no generic extractor flag fixes markup whose class names lie about its structure. Prices are the guard signal because they are the facts listings exist to convey, cheap to detect, and their wholesale loss is unambiguous; a fuzzier length heuristic would misfire on legitimately boilerplate-heavy pages. Trade-off accepted: fallback pages keep more boilerplate (~2–3× tokens vs a clean extraction) — correctness of the stored artifact beats token savings, per the fee-table-preserver precedent | §7, §10.7, §20 |
| 2026-07-15 | **v3.4: collaboration aligns with Unit Group rows.** Ratings move from Listing-wide to `(hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key, user_id)` so each member judges the layout represented by that Overview row; existing Listing-wide ratings are copied to every Unit Group currently present during migration, preserving their prior visible meaning while allowing future divergence. Comments remain Listing-wide by default but may carry the same nullable `"{beds}-{baths}"` Unit Group key; authors may edit comment bodies, with `edited_at` recording and exposing that history. RLS validates every non-null Unit Group key against the Listing, and ratings join the Hunt Realtime publication. | A Property can have materially different layouts and prices, so repeating one rating on every row conflates separate choices. Management/location discussion still applies to the whole Listing, making forced comment scoping equally misleading; optional scoping preserves both kinds of conversation. The derived Unit Group key is already the stable identity used by pins and avoids coupling collaboration to a refreshable individual Floor Plan. | §3, §5.1, §8.2–8.3, §9.4, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-15 | **v3.5: Unit Group curation, inclusive rubric boundaries, normalized-city filters, and quieter Task History.** Interest Status + visited attach to `(hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key)`, are Owner/Curator-written and member-readable under RLS, and sync through Realtime. The status vocabulary removes ambiguous/duplicate `rejected` in favor of application/offer-direction terms; null is undecided. Rubric matches gain backward-compatible `lte`/`gte`; pure-bonus derivation now includes unknown-delta and forbids dealbreakers. DEDUPE geocoding persists normalized city. Overview filters gain score range, city, status, visited, and availability. History sorts terminal Jobs newest-finished-first and independently collapses plan/timeline. | Decision state differs by Hunt and layout, so neither global Property nor whole-Listing storage is honest; explicit inclusive operators prevent boundary bugs and date/numeric workarounds; a Gate contradicts “can only help”; locality must come from structured geocoding rather than frontend address parsing; progressive disclosure makes permanent Job history scannable without weakening FR10. | §3, §4.2, §8.2–8.3, §9.2–9.4, §13.2–13.3, §20 |
| 2026-07-16 | **`availability_date`: immediate-availability phrases → transient sentinel `available_now`, rewritten via `ctx.today()` in EXTRACT.** Pages often say "Available Now" / "Now" / "Immediately" with no calendar date. The LLM identifies that fact as the exact sentinel `available_now` (catalog `extraction_hint` + floor-plans schema description) **only when the target Property/Floor Plan has no explicit availability date**; EXTRACT normalizes both the property-level criterion and each floor plan's `availability_date` to `ctx.today().isoformat()` before VERIFY/persist. Persisted and scored values remain ISO dates only — the sentinel never reaches scoring, Postgres, or VERIFY's past-date check. Live ingest uses wall-clock today; bench labeling and clock semantics are clarified in the same-day follow-up. Do not inject "today" into the EXTRACT prompt or invent a date in the hint — the model has no trustworthy clock. | Extraction should report page truth; calendar assignment is deterministic pipeline work. Hint-alone "use today's date" asks the model to invent a date it does not know. | §8.2, §10.3, §20 |
| 2026-07-16 | **Bench freezes `ctx.today` to corpus `meta.json.saved_at`; explicit dates always win over Available Now.** Re-extracting a frozen page with wall-clock today made phrase-only `available_now` drift across bench runs. The harness now parses each listing's timezone-aware `saved_at`, clones `StageCtx` per listing with that UTC date, and grades `available_now` against it (`ListingResult.as_of_date` records the basis). Missing/naive/malformed `saved_at` fails that listing closed — never falls back to the run day. Catalog/schema/prompt v5 state the precedence rule: an explicit Property/Floor Plan availability date in prose or `[EMBEDDED DATA]` outranks UI "Available Now" wording (is the motivating counterexample — embedded `dateAvailable: 2026-07-02` is page truth; do not relabel it to `available_now` or accept `saved_at`). | Snapshot fidelity requires a frozen clock for phrase-only immediacy; substituting `saved_at` for an explicit but different page date would hide real EXTRACT regressions. | §8.2, §10.3, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-17 | **P0-14 census verdict ruled ahead of the bench: tier 3 stays; Bright Data Web Unlocker remains the provider (free plan).** The keep/provider half of the P0-14 gate is decided now rather than waiting on P0-11..13: the tier-3 fetcher stays on the ladder, Bright Data stays the default (`MANZIL_TIER3_PROVIDER` swap seam unchanged), and the free-plan cap holds — a paid plan remains its own future §20 decision (2026-07-07 v2.5). The Apify structured-actor path stays deferred, unruled — still a second data path with its own decision. Consequence: P3-14 is retained and can be scoped now instead of at Phase 3 entry; the only remaining P0-14 deliverable is the model-pin entry | The census CSV already shows the hostile five (apartments.com, zillow, realtor, trulia, forrent) unreachable below tier 3, and the real hunt keeps surfacing those domains — no bench outcome could flip "keep", so ruling early costs nothing and unblocks Phase 3 scoping | §10.7, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-17 | **Bench scope trimmed to 10 listings; model-candidate slate widened.** P0-11 labeling proved far more tedious than §19's "an evening" estimate; the bench set is cut ~20 → 10 while keeping tier/site diversity per `bench/manifest.md`'s slot rationale. Simultaneously the §11.2 sweep pool widens to every model priced in `MODEL_PRICES` (Gemini 3.x Flash tiers — current cost lean `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` — plus GPT-5.x nano, DeepSeek, and Qwen entries alongside the Haiku/Sonnet baseline and the Gemini 2.5 candidates). Pins still change only on bench evidence at P0-14 | Ground-truth quality per listing beats count — the harness grades per listing, so 10 well-labeled pages still rank models decisively; widening the priced pool costs nothing until a pin actually changes | §11.2, §19, §20 |

| 2026-07-17 | **Tier-2 SSRF smoke checks passed; redirect-chain screen added.** The two pre-live gates (2026-07-12) are cleared with live evidence. Tier-1 pin cert path holds — a real HTTPS fetch through `pin_target` succeeds and a hostname-mismatched cert is rejected, so verification still binds to the hostname not the pinned IP. Tier-2 exposed a real gap: Playwright follows server 3xx redirects internally without re-invoking the `context.route` handler, so the route guard's main-frame DNS branch never fired on redirects and only the final-`page.url` backstop was live — a `public → private(reachable) → public` chain would transit a private host while the backstop passed clean. Fix: the post-load screen now walks the whole chain (`response.request.redirected_from` + `page.url`) via `screen_urls`; any private hop refuses before the body is read. The inherent tier-2 DNS-rebinding window (public at screen, private at the browser's own connect) remains the accepted residual — the backstop and chain screen are best-effort, as noted 2026-07-12 | The smoke test existed to prove exactly this control before wiring Chromium into a live loop; it found the route guard's redirect branch dormant and the fix closes the transited-private-hop gap while keeping the residual honest | §10.7, §16, §19 |

| 2026-07-18 | **Phase 2 exited — sign-off recorded.** Green CI was the last gate and now stands. The red `test` job root-caused to the seed EXTRACT replay recordings never being committed: the 2026-07-16 availability prompt change re-keyed the content-hash recording filenames, `.gitignore`'s per-hash exceptions still listed the stale hashes, so local runs read the files from disk while CI's checkout lacked them (the failure only surfaced now because the `test` job first gained a database in the same CI change). Fix: exceptions updated to the current hashes, the three synthetic-page recordings committed (repo-safe per v2.8 — every evidence quote traces to committed fixture pages, no corpus content), and a guard test asserts each referenced seed recording is git-tracked so the next hash churn fails at the desk, not on master. Node-20-deprecated actions bumped to Node-24 majors (setup-uv settles on v7 — no v8 major tag exists yet). With CI green and the P2-9/P2-10 acceptance checks confirmed 2026-07-17, Yusuf records the Phase 2 exit: both real users active, the §4.2 matrix database-enforced and green in CI. P2-12 stays an open follow-up, not an exit condition. Formal Phase 3 entry is unblocked; the §7 Phase 3 table finalization happens at entry per the 2026-07-11 plan | Phase exits are recorded, never assumed — the CI gate existed precisely so "works on the dev machine" could not stand in for a reproducible green | §19, §20 |

| 2026-07-18 | **`location_safety` rescoped: A+–F letter grades, override-first placeholder; the assessor becomes a deferred dedicated module (P3-8 ships without safety synthesis).** Three changes. (a) The value scale moves from low/med/high to a 13-grade letter enum `A+`…`F` (CrimeGrade-style convention) — the same `value_schema` drives the rubric editor's default options (A-band +0.5, B-band +0.25, C-band 0.0, D-band −0.5, F −1.0) and the override dropdown, so a human grade is a first-class scored value. (b) P3-8's ENRICH ships `location_safety` as a **placeholder**: no web-search synthesis fires and no value is emitted — the criterion scores unknown (`unknown_delta`, gate-firing per §9) until a human override supplies a grade. The web_search-scoped synthesis idea is dropped, not merely postponed: R8 already ruled the data too weak for a cheap synthesis to be worth trusting, and pretending otherwise with a labeled guess still puts a fabricated grade in a scoring path. (c) The real producer is a **dedicated location-safety module** (§18): ARCGIS/open-data crime layers + police/FBI UCR-NIBRS reports, graded A+–F, **SE Michigan first** with expansion later, and possibly a **standalone project** this system merely consumes — commercial safety APIs were rejected on cost (hundreds of $/month; building is cheaper than buying at personal-tool scale). Tracked as P3-17 (gated on a scoping decision, never on P3-8). Existing rubrics keep any old low/med/high options until the criterion is re-added; no live hunt had scored safety values, so no data migration is needed | An unknown that fires gates honestly beats a low-confidence guess that scores; the letter scale matches how public crime-grade sources already report, so human overrides today and module output tomorrow share one vocabulary | §8.2, §10.3, §17, §18, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **P3-9 shipped: utility baselines ride the scheduler tick (not a jobs row); §9.5 composition completed with a graduated unknown rule.** Six rulings. (a) **Jobs-vs-tick**: the metro baselines pass is the first scheduled duty that is *not* a job insert — `jobs.hunt_id` is NOT NULL and baselines are global, hunt-less maintenance, so the worker loop's new scheduler tick (P3-9 lands the scaffold P3-11/P3-12 extend, per the plan's whoever-is-first rule) spawns one advisory-lock-guarded asyncio task per due metro; a dead pass retries next tick — a 120-day cadence needs no queue durability. The tick is opt-in (production entry only), so tests and dev-seed drains never fire scheduled LLM passes. (b) **Search interim**: until P3-5 lands the provider `web_search` plumbing, the pass is a plain structured call; `sources` records `"search": false` + the model's claimed references, and writes are all-or-nothing (incomplete bucket×utility coverage writes nothing, because gaps would trip the strict-unknown branch). (c) **Graduated unknown rule**: metro with no baselines → v1 composition + `utilities_not_estimated` badge (behavior-identical to shipped v1 until the tick learns the metro — no gate carnage on rollout); metro with baselines but a missing needed row → total withheld, criterion scores `unknown_delta`, `fees_unverified` badge. Page silent on inclusions → estimate everything conservatively + badge. (d) **Fee/estimate double-count guard**: a billed water/sewer or valet-trash mandatory fee suppresses that utility's baseline estimate — an actual charge beats an estimate. (e) **Metro = `properties.city`** (city-as-metro proxy, v1) and **occupants scaling** = water/sewer × `clamp(occupants/max(1,beds), 1.0, 2.0)` — `occupants` joins the scoring keys (settings change → rescore). (f) **`hunt_listings.all_in_components`**: the display plan's tagged component list + badges persists as display metadata beside the pinned breakdown so the §13.2 cell/drawer render the estimated portion without reshaping the §9.3 contract. EXTRACT gains optional-with-default `mandatory_fees`/`heating` blocks (no prompt bump — recordings stay valid; a bench re-run is owed at next convenience since schema additions are output-affecting) | The all-in number is the tool's core promise: every component honestly knowable composes now, the unknowable declines to fabricate, and the rollout path never makes existing listings look wrong while the machinery warms up | §8.2, §9.5, §13.2, §14, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **Baselines-write → metro rescore trigger (P3-9 follow-up).** The first live listing exposed a permanent race in the graduated rule's rollout path: the listing that makes a metro "due" always scores before the metro's baselines land (ingest finished 22:00:20, pass wrote 22:00:29), and no existing rescore trigger (rubric/override/fee/settings edits) ever re-composes it — it wears `utilities_not_estimated` indefinitely. Ruling: a **successful** metro baselines write enqueues one hunt-level rescore per hunt with an active listing in that metro, inside the advisory-lock scope; a failed (all-or-nothing) pass enqueues nothing. Zero LLM spend — rescore already reads `baselines_for_metro`. First live pass evidence (Detroit, 24 rows): rescore composed rent $1,179 + $415 estimated utilities → $1,594 conservative, `heat_unknown` worse-of-variants branch exercised | The graduated rule promised "behavior-identical to v1 *until the tick learns the metro*" — learning the metro must therefore actually reach the listings that were scored before it | §9.5, §14, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **Per-plan composition detail on `scores.all_in_components`; heat-variant note rescoped (P3-9 follow-up #2).** First live listing (The Villas, 6 plans / 3 unit groups) exposed two display defects. (1) `hunt_listings.all_in_components` holds only the *display plan's* composition, and the cell/drawer rendered it for **every** unit-group row — a 1br/1ba row showed the 2br display plan's rent; separately the Overview cell read the `all_in_monthly` breakdown criterion, so a rubric with that criterion disabled showed no all-in at all. Ruling: `scores` gains `all_in_components jsonb` — each plan's own §9.5 composition, written by ingest projection and rescore beside the breakdown; the frontend prefers the displayed plan's composition (total AND detail), falling back to the criterion / listing-level blob for pre-column rows; the listing-level column stays for listing-level contexts. (2) The unknown-heating note stamped "worse case" on every estimated line; it now lands only on the lines the variant choice changed (electric/gas), reworded "heating type unknown — priced as gas heat (the costlier setup)", and only when the worse-of-variants comparison actually decided (an undecidable variant carries no note) | The all-in figure must be the figure of the unit the row is about — a composition that silently belongs to a different floor plan is wrong in the way §9.5 exists to prevent; and a caveat note is copy, so it must be unmistakable and only where it applies | §8.2, §9.5, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **One-time fee category (Yusuf-directed).** Move-in costs get their own EXTRACT block `one_time_fees` — `{name, amount, basis: per_application \| per_person \| per_pet \| flat, refundable?}` — because application fees, admin fees, and pet deposits were previously invisible (the mandatory-fees block deliberately excludes them and nothing else captured them). Rulings: (a) they **never** compose into `all_in_monthly` — all-in stays a monthly figure; display-only, no catalog entry, no scoring path; (b) persistence mirrors the P3-9 blocks exactly — append-only `one_time_fees` extraction + projection onto new one-time checklist slots (`application_fee`, existing `admin`, `pet_deposit`, `pet_fee`; keyword-mapped, `manual` never overwritten, unmapped fees still display via the extraction); (c) the checklist splits into monthly vs one-time / move-in groups and shows an estimated move-in-fees line for the hunt's household (`per_person × occupants`, `per_pet × cats+dogs`; excludes the per-plan security deposit); (d) the schema addition is optional-with-default — existing EXTRACT recordings and RunState snapshots stay valid, no prompt bump. **Owed downstream:** the P0-11 bench ground-truth labels gain the field (human pass), covered by the already-owed output-affecting bench re-run; corpus re-extraction picks the field up lazily per §14 refresh, no forced backfill | A $300 pet deposit and $50-per-person application fees are real money the tool silently ignored; but folding one-time charges into a monthly figure would corrupt the §9.5 promise, so they get their own honest category | §8.2, §9.5, §13.2, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **Revert tombstone + all-in override; Overview declutter batch (Yusuf-directed).** (a) **Revert semantics**: overrides stay append-only, so "revert to original" appends a null-valued override — the latest row per criterion wins and null falls through to the extraction (`_resolve_effective_values`); no delete route, provenance intact. (b) **`all_in_monthly` override**: a live override beats the §9.5 composition — the scored value is the human's figure, `scores.all_in_components` carries `overridden: true` with the machine's components preserved, and the cell swaps the estimated portion for the manual-entry icon; drawer gains override + revert affordances on the all-in. Manual fee entries revert to the extraction-derived amount (mirrored keyword maps) or unknown when nothing was extracted (pet slots — no extraction row in v1). (c) **UI declutter**: composition warnings collapse to one amber triangle with hover titles (details stay in the drawer); the Info column folds into the score cell as `×N`; Status and Visited share one curation cell; select tokens sentence-cased; numeric criteria carry units inline — grocery proximity is **minutes** per `proximity_mode` (a "miles" label was considered and rejected: the value has always been travel time); drawer gains the P3-7a photo carousel (scroll-snap, signed URLs, no new dependency) | Warnings are for hovering, not for permanent column real estate; and undoing a human's edit must be as auditable as making it | §9.5, §9.6, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **UI polish batch #2 (Yusuf-directed): rubric editor redesign, account default color, view options.** (a) **Rubric editor card redesign** — fixed option grid (match \| points \| dealbreaker-icon \| remove) that never wraps; numeric ops in number-line order (`<` `≤` `=` `≥` `>` between) with word glosses; points as spinner inputs suffixed "pts"; dealbreaker is an ActionIcon whose armed state swaps points for a red "→ sets score" input; boolean criteria are fixed True/False **text** rows — a Switch misreads match values as settings; editor grid drops to two columns for input room. Units from a shared frontend map (`lib/criterionUnits.ts`, keyed by catalog key — the pinned §8.2 catalog shape is untouched) render in editor inputs, view labels ("between 10 and 20 min"), and value display. (b) **`user_profiles.default_color`** — account-level color propagating exactly like the display-name default: membership inserts inherit it (color null) when set; effective color coalesces override over default; `/profile` page (avatar user-menu in every header) edits both defaults; `PUT /profile` gains optional `default_color`, name-only PUTs never touch it. (c) **View options + row actions** — Overview row density (compact/normal/comfortable, localStorage) and per-row menu (open details / listing page / copy address / copy link / delete); post-auth landing is the homepage except invite/join returns (a stale `from` stranded fresh accounts on someone else's blank hunt); running job cards gain loader + elapsed timer (`jobs.started_at` now on JobResponse); drawer carousel grows to 240px and opens a dependency-free full-screen lightbox (keyboard nav, fit ↔ zoom) | Editing a rubric is the app's densest form — alignment, spinners, and honest True/False text remove the guesswork; and a collaborator's identity (name + color) is an account fact that hunts override, not re-enter | §5.1, §8.2–8.3, §13.1, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-18 | **Phase 3 formally entered; IMPLEMENTATION §7 table finalized at entry.** Same day as the Phase 2 exit, the entry pass P2-10 owed is complete against the 2026-07-11 plan (which already carried the 2026-07-13 P3-5/P3-6 rescope). Finalization is truth-marking, not redesign: P3-2/P3-3 (planner/runner + tools/Maps), P3-4 (DEDUPE), and the P3-7a substrate are marked landed-ahead-of-entry; **P3-14 is scoped by the 2026-07-17 census verdict** — retained, no longer existence-conditional: the tier-3 fetcher already runs on the Bright Data free plan, so the task reduces to the provider-key posture on the live hunt plus per-domain registry pins for exactly the five census-named hostile domains (apartments.com, zillow, realtor, trulia, forrent). DISCOVER's search provider stays the plan's decision-with-default (Anthropic server-side `web_search` through OpenRouter, fallbacks named), verified when P3-5 lands. New Phase 3 LLM stages ride the existing Haiku/Sonnet pins until the P0-14 model-pin verdict — that gate is unchanged by entry. Next: Wave B's P3-5 | Entering a phase means revising its table first — the rule exists so the table states reality at entry rather than the draft's guesses, and so conditional tasks get scoped the moment their gates rule instead of at implementation time | §10.7, §19, §20 |
| 2026-07-19 | **Collaboration palette drops obsolete `dusk`/`clay` tokens; account defaults auto-assign globally with wrap.** Theme still uses `dusky`/`burnt_clay` brand scales, but those names are not member-color tokens — `dusk`/`clay` CSS variables were never registered after the theme rename, so swatches resolved undefined. Ordered member palette is now `moss, ochre, brick, olive, stone, plum`. Onboarding creates a required `user_profiles.default_color` via an advisory-locked insert trigger: first globally unused token, then wrap from the start and continue. Invite/Invitation-Link membership assignment uses the same six-token order and wrap within a Hunt when the joiner has no account default. Custom `#RRGGBB` remains valid. Overview filter bar shows `N visible · M filtered out` while filters are active. | Distinct collaborator colors must resolve to real theme tokens; account-level defaults need the same uniqueness-then-wrap rule invite assignment already had, applied globally so new users land distinct before any Hunt join | §8.2, §9.1, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-19 | **Parking catalog adds `covered` as a distinct value.** The parking criterion vocabulary and default rubric now recognize `covered` parking alongside garage, carport, dedicated lot, street-only, and none; its default delta matches carport at `+0.25`. | Covered parking is materially better than an uncovered dedicated lot but is not necessarily a carport; preserving the listing's stated category avoids forcing either inaccurate label | §8.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-19 | **Compare view lands (P3-13's compare half), refined to Unit-Group columns with a personal per-browser set.** §13.1's "2–4 listing side-by-side" refines to: the compared entity is the Unit Group row (what the Overview shows), the selection is personal per browser (localStorage per hunt, auto-pruned), and capacity is a `COMPARE_LIMIT` constant — 3 for now, one-line change to raise. Entry via a Send-to-Compare row action + a badged nav link. Transposed layout (attributes × columns): photo carousels, header facts, monthly-composition rows from `all_in_components`, and a criteria section iterating the score-breakdown union — so P3-10 custom criteria will appear with zero Compare changes. Mobile bottom-sheet polish stays as P3-13's remainder | Comparing listings would collapse distinct bed/bath decisions into one column; a shared set would let one member's Clear wipe another's exploration; breakdown-driven rows make the page self-extending | §13.1, §13.2, §19 |
| 2026-07-19 | **Overview/Compare minor-features batch (Yusuf-directed; feature-suggestions m1–m3, m6, m7, m11, m12).** Free-text name/address search joins the filter registry (always-visible input; whitespace-inert, unknown-safe like every predicate); sort keys grow all-in cost, earliest availability (defaults ascending), and recently added; submit gains a client-side duplicate pre-check over the hunt's loaded property URLs (normalized host+path; warn with Submit anyway — the pipeline stays the identity authority); a checkbox column drives bulk set-status / send-to-Compare / archive; **"delete" is renamed archive** — the action always soft-archived — with an Active \| Archived Overview toggle and restore, both directions Owner-only via new `PATCH /v1/listings/{id}/status` (the DELETE endpoint remains, UI no longer calls it); a column-visibility picker adds optional City / Available / Deposit / Added columns with **defaults exactly the previous table**; Compare columns drag-reorder via native HTML5 drag (no dnd dependency at 3 columns) | The real hunt crossed the row count where search, bulk curation, and honest archive semantics stop being polish; every piece rides shipped substrate (predicate registry, listings PATCH pattern, localStorage view prefs), so the batch cost stayed minor-sized. m11/m12 pulled ahead of their P3 parking because m2's new sort keys need visible columns and the compare header was already open | §5.1, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-20 | **Image cap splits into `MAX_STORED_IMAGES` (20) / `MAX_VISION_IMAGES` (8); srcset parsing and the IMAGE_FETCH partial-set guard fixed.** Three changes from one investigation (: 3 candidates discovered, 0 stored). (a) **srcset parser** split the attribute on every comma, which shatters URLs whose own path carries commas — Cloudinary/Imgix transform segments (`c_fill,f_auto,q_auto,w_640`) are the common case — and the trailing fragment resolved against the page base into a URL that never existed and 404s; it now walks whitespace tokens and treats only a *trailing* comma as a candidate boundary. (b) **The all-or-nothing partial-set guard** (`incomplete and len(prepared) < MAX_IMAGES` → `property_images = []`) discarded every good image whenever any candidate failed; since persistence already keys its authoritative replace off `image_fetch_completed`, leaving that flag unset makes the write purely additive, so a partial set is now *stored* while staying non-authoritative. VISION now gates on new hashes (`current - previous`) rather than set equality, so a shrunken set no longer reads as "changed". (c) **The single `MAX_IMAGES` constant did double duty** — storage cap and VISION input cap — so raising it for coverage would have spent §15 lever 3. Split: storage 20, VISION 8. | (a)+(b) compounded into a structural failure: the parser reliably manufactured one dead candidate per Cloudinary-backed listing, and one dead candidate then voided the whole set, so images were *impossible* for that class of site. (c) Storage is ~free (~60KB/image) while the VISION cap is the real lever — and the stronger reason to hold it at 8 is signal, not spend: past the first handful, listing photos are amenity/floor-plan/stock shots that dilute the `kitchen_quality`/`flooring_quality` criteria the rubric actually scores | §15, §20, IMPLEMENTATION §5 |
| 2026-07-19 | **Hunt-wide filters: `hunt_shared_filters` table, Owner/Curator publish, seed-on-open.** Filters gain hunt-wide persistence *outside* the pinned `hunts.settings` contract (view state, not scoring inputs — settings' bump-and-rescore semantics must not apply). Owner+Curator publish via `PUT /v1/hunts/{id}/shared-filters` (curation precedent, not the Owner-only settings precedent); the set seeds every member's Overview filter state on open, members deviate freely, a badge distinguishes matching vs modified, publishing cleared filters retires the set. Stored filter objects are sanitized on read (unknown keys drop, missing default) so the filter registry can grow without migrations. Alongside: the filter registry gains laundry, parking, pets, cooling, dishwasher, max all-in, and available-by predicates reading the display plan's breakdown, with unknown always passing | A hunt benefits from one agreed starting view (Yusuf-requested) without locking anyone's exploration — seed-on-open is the lightest semantics that delivers both; enum filters ride data the Overview already loads instead of a new extraction query pattern | §8.2, §8.3, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-20 | **Unit Group and Overview-row semantics tightened; Display Floor Plan named.** A Unit Group remains the derived `(beds, baths)` presentation bucket and is not split into one row per Floor Plan. It does not assert that sibling Floor Plans share amenities, Unit type, price, or availability. One row now has an explicit two-layer contract: plan count and rent/sqft ranges are Unit Group aggregates (the optional Available column is the group's labeled earliest-availability summary), while score, all-in composition, deposit, Criterion-backed values, and future scoped amenities come from exactly one **Display Floor Plan** — the valid per-group pin, otherwise the highest-scoring plan. The row identifies `Pinned` versus `Best score`, exposes `×N` alternatives/variation, and never unions sibling-plan amenities into group truth. Collaboration state remains Unit-Group-scoped; simultaneous per-plan applications remain the existing trigger for a separate Application/entity decision | The Unit Group keeps Overview compact and stable, but scoped amenities make an unlabeled representative dangerous: one plan's laundry or balcony must not appear to describe every 2bd/1ba option. Naming the Display Floor Plan preserves the useful grouping while making every plan-specific value traceable to the actual choice it describes | §3, §9.4, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-21 | **P0-14 model-pin verdict: EXTRACT and VERIFY pinned to `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` (first non-Anthropic default); an L0-harness grading gap fixed and six dead `MODEL_PRICES` slugs pruned.** The P0-13 sweep ran the record-mode harness over the ten committed bench labels for every priced candidate, then a zero-token replay re-run. Result on the benched pair: gemini-3-flash-preview beat the Haiku 4.5 baseline — criterion accuracy 0.904 vs 0.862, tied gate accuracy (1.0), zero failures, ~63% cheaper ($0.16 vs $0.43 per ten listings) and ~2× faster (9.3 s vs 18.9 s mean). Scope is the **benched pair only**: smoke/validate/reconcile_equivalence/custom_match/enrich_reviews/utility_baselines/plan_assist stay on the Haiku `WORKHORSE_MODEL` and the TASTE tier stays Sonnet — the bench never measured them, and gemini trades a higher evidence-flag rate (0.20 vs 0.03) for its accuracy/cost win, so widening the pin needs its own bench. Two findings landed with it: (a) the harness dropped any listing where VERIFY raised a gate `confirm_value` checkpoint (no human in the bench) as an *error* — 4–5 of 10 per model, a different subset each — biasing the comparison toward whichever model checkpoints least; it now catches `CheckpointRaised` and grades the flagged state as-is ("accept at low confidence"; the flag still counts in the evidence/verify-flag metrics), so every model is graded over the same listings. (b) Six priced slugs were unusable and are pruned from `MODEL_PRICES` — deepseek-v4-flash / claude-3-haiku / qwen3.5-flash / minimax-m3 do not route on OpenRouter (404/400), and gemini-3.1-pro-preview / gemini-3.5-flash route but return no tool_calls under forced tool_choice. The seed/e2e VERIFY replay recordings re-keyed to the new pin (EXTRACT stays frozen via `_SEED_EXTRACT_RECORDINGS`, so the P1 dev-seed EXTRACT fixtures are untouched); worker 451 + api 64 green, resolving the "pre-existing" pin-flip failures noted in IMPLEMENTATION 2.0.67. This closes the last open P0-14 deliverable (the census half ruled 2026-07-17) and with it the Phase 0 decision gate. | §11.2's rule is to adopt a non-Anthropic default only on bench evidence of material savings at an equal verification pass-rate: gemini-3-flash-preview clears savings and value accuracy decisively, so it wins the extraction pair — but "equal verification pass-rate" is exactly where its weaker evidence-quote fidelity shows, so the pin is deliberately scoped to the two stages the bench measured, not the whole workhorse tier. Grading a model only on the listings it happens not to flag would reward caution over accuracy and compare models over different data; pruning slugs that cannot serve a structured stage keeps the priced-override guard (`MANZIL_MODEL_<STAGE>` must be in `MODEL_PRICES`) from implying a dead model is callable | §11.1, §11.2, §19, §20, IMPLEMENTATION §7 |
| 2026-07-21 | **Scoped Criteria and amenities package approved; P3-SC series created.** Property amenities remain Property-scoped; unit features preserve exact/all/select/unspecified applicability, with unqualified claims never promoted to universal truth. Exact mapping and diagram association are Source-local; prefer a missing association over a wrong one and defer cross-Source matching. A clean database reset permits direct replacement of the old Property/Criterion latest-row assumption with one unified append-only candidate/resolution fact store, centralized RLS-preserving current views, scoped Overrides, and authoritative-success refresh semantics. Catalog Criteria gain independent Fact Scope, acquisition-escalation, and conflict policies; `available_sources_only` spends no fetch solely for that fact, `verified_positive_preferred` can retain an advertised positive while quietly marking disagreement, and any Gate restores normal decision-relevant escalation. The first Property tranche, saved dev Rubric, Unit/Property type arrays, Floor Plan detail + diagram persistence, later objective-unit/flooring tranches, and estimated move-in composition are approved; storage, basements, granular internet, private pools, Floor Plan deep links/OCR and cross-Source matching are deferred. Established Phase 3 task numbers remain intact; P3-SC1–P3-SC4 join P3-5 at P3-6, with later P3-SC work dependency-scheduled around it. Supplementary rationale remains in `docs/scoped-criteria-and-amenities.md` | Treating every amenity mention as true for every unit creates false confidence on apartment-hunt Gates; sparse Source-local exactness captures most decision value without the cost and false-match risk of cross-site plan identity. The reset window is the safest time to correct the substrate instead of preserving a known ambiguous contract | header, §1, §3, §8–§10, §13–§14, §17–§20 |
| 2026-07-21 | **P3-5 DISCOVER landed: native search, tier/family slate, durable link-only Sources, and editable Source Policy.** DISCOVER is a six-turn bounded loop with SSRF-guarded `fetch_page` and OpenRouter's current `openrouter:web_search` server tool (the older `web` plugin is deprecated), capped at three searches, five results per search and twelve per turn; each search is $0.01 at landing, with provider-reported total cost authoritative. OpenRouter sometimes omits native-search count/citations, so the seam records one opaque search event per offered DISCOVER turn when the provider count is absent. Search output is untrusted; exact same-Property judgment and deterministic URL normalization precede persistence. The official URL is stored/displayed link-only (`is_official`, `last_fetched_at = null`); other exact candidates form a ranked pool, then a deterministic slate selects policy-permitted, tier-diverse and syndication-family-distinct siblings with cheaper-tier substitution recorded in the manifest. Known Source links are append-only and never re-parented across Properties. `single_source_reason` distinguishes `trust_link`, `discover_exhausted`, and `discover_failed`; all render with quiet explanatory presentation. Owner or submitter can edit Source Policy through an atomic database capability: a higher sibling cap enqueues a DISCOVER-only refresh; tightening and enabling only `tier_1_plus_official`'s future arbiter-fetch carve-out are metadata-only. A live search-seam pass over the ten canonical bench listings found the correct official site for 10/10 with zero official-site fetches and at least one sibling for 10/10; this validates discovery yield, not future P3-6 extraction/reconciliation quality. Live output also exposed harmless prose before a fenced JSON object, so the stage accepts one valid fenced object but never scrapes arbitrary braces. | Native search preserves one LLM/provider seam and avoids another credential; family/tier selection makes later votes meaningfully independent. Honest failure reasons avoid turning an unavailable search into false evidence of absence, and atomic policy edits prevent widened intent from existing without the refresh that realizes it. | §3, §5.1, §8.2, §10.2–§10.7, §13.2, §15–§16, §19–§20 |
| 2026-07-21 | **P3-SC2 landed: the scoped append-only truth substrate replaces the old Property/Criterion latest-row identity.** Migration `20260801000000_scoped_extraction_foundation.sql` intentionally drops/rebuilds pre-live Extraction/Override state, adds Source-local Floor Plan identity/lifecycle fields, candidate/resolved rows with explicit provenance edges, same-Property composite constraints, security-invoker current views, and scoped append-only Overrides. Writes now persist Floor Plans before resolving claim targets; complete authoritative Source refreshes append explicit `not_found` versions for disappeared claims and retire only that Source's omitted Floor Plans, while partial/failed work retires nothing. An already-known exact Source URL converges the new Listing placeholder onto the Source's existing Property without re-parenting the Source. SCORE/rescore share one pure effective-value resolver; split/merge, API, RLS, frontend detail evidence, and all audited production readers use the scoped contract. Existing unit-like Catalog output is honestly stored as `unit_scope_unspecified` until P3-SC4 changes schemas/prompts and presence scoring; P3-SC2 does not claim that migration early. `clock_timestamp()` orders multiple appends in one projection transaction. | The clean reset window removes a known false-universality hazard without a dual-read/backfill burden. Source-local exact identity and database-enforced same-Property targets favor a missing association over a wrong one; exact-URL convergence prevents the same durable Source from being projected under two Properties when geocode-less DEDUPE lacks inputs. Explicit authoritative tombstones distinguish a real successful disappearance from silence caused by partial work. Keeping output-shape/prompt changes in P3-SC4 preserves the required bench boundary. | header, §8.1–§8.3, §9.3–§9.6, §10.2–§10.6, §13–§14, §19–§20; IMPLEMENTATION §3/§7 |
| 2026-07-22 | **P3-SC3 landed: the first Property tranche and set-valued Rubric path are live.** `property` is a first-class Catalog category; the approved 13 Criteria are Catalog-generated into seed/sync SQL and the dynamic zero-tool Extraction schema. Arrays are controlled, non-empty, duplicate-free sets and score only through strict `contains_any`/`contains_all` membership; exact array equality remains absent. Source-local Floor Plans persist their controlled `unit_types`, while a Unit Group may show only their labeled set-union and never treats that union as per-plan truth. Property facts render once per Listing, separately from Display-Floor-Plan facts. The checked-in `dev_rubric.v1.json` installs only into its deterministic development Hunt, has no default Gates, no-ops when its canonical Rubric is present, and refuses local Rubric drift unless `--force` is explicit. Canonical human-labeled scoped-bench and replay refresh remain P3-SC4 work; legacy synthetic integration recordings use a narrow explicit-unknown compatibility seam until then. | Strict set operators preserve additive type evidence without making extra advertised types a mismatch. Separate Property/Floor Plan presentation prevents the new tranche from recreating the universality bug in the UI. A guarded development Hunt makes repeatable smoke tests cheap without making test preferences a production default or silently overwriting local edits. Keeping the human-label boundary in P3-SC4 avoids claiming output-quality evidence before the scoped unit schemas exist. | header, §8.2, §9.2–§9.4, §13.2, §19–§20; IMPLEMENTATION §3/§5/§7 |
| 2026-07-23 | **v3.11: state-qualified locality and utility-baseline scopes.** DEDUPE persists `properties.city`, `state` (USPS two-letter), and `county` (Google `administrative_area_level_2`). City precedence: Google `locality` → strict original-address postal city → null; `administrative_area_level_3` is never treated as a city. State precedence: Google `administrative_area_level_1.short_name` → strict original-address parse. `utility_baselines` replaces bare `metro` with `(geo_level, state, region_name)` where `geo_level` is `city | county | state`; legacy rows are truncated and regenerated. SCORE selects exactly one baseline scope — city+state, else county+state when city is null, else state-only — and does **not** fall through to broader scopes when a city pass is missing or failed. A region is fresh only when all 24 bucket×utility rows are within the 120-day TTL. County/state estimates carry a regional display note in composition. Existing Properties backfill via `manzil backfill-locality`; `geocode_property`'s forever-cache contract is unchanged. Supersedes the 2026-07-18 city-as-metro proxy. | Same-named cities in different states must not share estimates; township names must not fragment city keys; incomplete locality must not silently downgrade to broader estimates; the regenerable baseline cache cannot be safely migrated from city-only keys. | header, §3, §8.2, §9.5, §13.2, §14, §20 |
| 2026-07-24 | **Half-star Hunt member ratings.** `ratings.rating` moves from integer `smallint` to `numeric(2,1)` with a database check for 1–5 in 0.5 steps.
