| 2026-07-25 | **v3.13: Catalog categories re-grouped around the renter's question; `category` declared presentation-only.** The eight-value vocabulary (`property, unit, policy, cost, availability, condition, location, reputation`) becomes seven — `unit, cost, tenancy, location, fittings, amenities, management` — and the 32 seeded Criteria are re-assigned: the 12-entry `property` drawer splits into **amenities** (shared facilities plus `property_types`) and **management** (on-site staff, maintenance and payment channels, and `management_reviews`, absorbing the former `reputation` singleton); **tenancy** takes lease length, move-in date, pets and smoking (absorbing the `availability` singleton and the old `policy` pair); **fittings** takes the in-unit fit-out and the two vision-scored quality Criteria (the old `condition` pair); `unit` keeps layout plus `parking`; `cost` and `location` are unchanged. No category now holds fewer than two Criteria (7/6/6/5/4/2/2 vs 12/10/2/2/2/2/1/1). This **supersedes the 2026-07-22 ruling that `property` is a first-class Catalog category** — the tranche it named is intact, only its grouping moved. Ruled at the same time: `category` is **presentation vocabulary with no functional readers** — verified by sweep that nothing in `shared/`, `worker/` or `api/` branches on it, the column carries no check constraint, and Property-vs-Floor-Plan rendering runs off `fact_scope`. Re-grouping is therefore a label change, not a data-model change, and needs no backfill: `rubric_criteria` references `catalog_key`, never `category`. Delivery is the generated seed plus one full-Catalog sync migration (`20260805000000_catalog_recategorize.sql`); earlier per-tranche sync migrations are frozen history and the generator guard now pins the newest full sync. | Categories existed to help a person scan the Rubric, and had stopped doing that: one drawer held 12 unrelated Criteria (a pool and an online rent portal) while two categories held one Criterion each and still cost a full heading. Grouping by the question being asked — what is the unit, what does it cost, what am I agreeing to, where is it — survives Catalog growth, where a residual "other" bucket only grows. Recording that nothing reads the column is the durable half of the ruling: it is what makes this cheap now and cheap to revisit. | §8.2, §13.2, §20; `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-07-26 | **v3.14: map surfaces (Yusuf-directed) — a drawer Location card and a hunt-wide Map view at `/h/:huntId/map`.** Both render through the **Google Maps JavaScript API**, the [§7](#7-technology-stack) vendor already used server-side for geocode/Places/Routes; the browser key is a **new, separate, HTTP-referrer-restricted** `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, never the worker's server key, since every `VITE_*` value ships to the visitor. With it unset each surface renders an explanatory placeholder and nothing else breaks — the maps are additive, never load-bearing. Deliberately **no cloud Map ID**: that keeps the classic `styles` array (so the dark basemap is themed from `theme.ts` tokens in-repo rather than in a Google console) and keeps `google.maps.Marker`, whose data-URI SVG icons carry the score palette. Deliberately **no new data**: both surfaces read the existing `properties.lat/lng` [§12](#12-caching-and-refresh-strategy) forever-cache that DEDUPE already writes only-when-null, so **no client-side geocoding is introduced** — a Property without coordinates is an ordinary "not mapped yet" state that the drawer answers with an address-only fallback and the Map view counts in a footnote. Two shape rulings: **(a) one pin is one Listing**, and a pin whose Unit Groups score into different bands is *sliced* into those bands rather than averaged — a map may not invent a score; **(b) clicking a multi-group pin asks which Unit Group first**, because §9.4's compared/curated entity is the Unit Group, not the Listing. To make the Map honest against the table beside it, the Overview's filter state (hunt-wide seed included) is **lifted out of `OverviewPage` into a hunt-scoped provider** mounted above the layout's `Outlet` and shared by both views; behaviour is unchanged, and switching hunts remounts and re-seeds. | The hunt is a geography problem the app could only answer as a text address; the two questions a renter actually asks a map are "where is this one" and "where are all of them relative to each other," which is exactly the drawer card and the Map tab. Everything else here is a refusal to spend: no new vendor, no new key posture beyond the browser-key split, no new columns, no geocoding path, and no averaged score — the map reports what the pipeline already computed or says plainly that it doesn't know | §7, §12, §13.1, §13.2, §20; `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §1/§7 |
| 2026-07-27 | **v3.15: P3-SC4 scoped-unit Extraction implementation landed; human baseline remains the acceptance gate.** `patio_balcony`, `private_entry`, `in_unit_laundry`, `parking`, `cooling`, `dishwasher`, and the non-Catalog `heating_type` composition input now use sparse applicability-bearing claims. Raw Source value validation is separated from the effective per-Floor-Plan scoring schema through `criteria_catalog.claim_value_schema`: boolean claims compose to `confirmed | advertised_unconfirmed | none`; typed exact/all claims retain their raw value, while select/unspecified claims compose to `advertised_unconfirmed`. A generic amenities list is therefore never all-unit truth and cannot satisfy a confirmed-feature Gate. Exact claims may name one or several response-local Floor Plans; EXTRACT first establishes unique response keys, rejects foreign/duplicate concrete targets, and expands a shared claim to one candidate per Floor Plan with one `claim_group_id`. Page silence is an empty sparse list, not a negative. Legacy Property/no-applicability values for this tranche are ignored by presence composition and demoted by VERIFY, so a reset/replay seam cannot promote an old boolean. The canonical-ten label schema and harness now grade raw value + applicability + exact target, audit the required scope/negative/missing/shared-plan/diagram cases, and report wrong exact associations. **Status remains `P3-SC4 ◐`:** synthetic/clean-database implementation checks pass, but human labels are not inferred from old model output; `manzil bench-audit-scoped` currently reports the local kit incomplete and all new coverage slots missing. P3-6 stays blocked until the current model pin is run over the finalized ten and records zero wrong exact associations plus the Gate regression report. | The high-cost failure is a false exact association or a generic amenity silently satisfying a Floor Plan Gate. Separating raw evidence from effective scoring keeps the LLM contract honest without making the shared scoring engine rental-aware; expanding multi-plan claims at persistence preserves simple current-value queries and shared provenance. A tool can validate human labels but must not author its own ground truth, so engineering delivery and empirical acceptance remain visibly separate. | header, §8.2, §9.3, §10.5, §19–§20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §3/§5/§7 |
| 2026-07-27 | **v3.16: P3-7a2 kitchen-first image classification and P3-7b rollout contract.** Replace first-image quality input with an independent batched ≤384 px classifier over all 30 stored photos, deterministic difference-hash clustering/target selection, and a kitchen-only anchored quality call over at most three selected 1024 px targets. Kitchen references are criterion-ready independently: five anchors per level build five hashed 1024 px sheets. Flooring/bathroom remain disabled behind separate decisions. | Classifying every cheap thumbnail prevents page order from turning amenity/stock/diagram photos into taste evidence, while deterministic selection confines expensive quality judgment to relevant, diverse kitchens. Exact Source-local context is materially stronger than a gallery estimate, so scoring must preserve that distinction without reshaping the pinned breakdown contract. Independent rollout gates let selector evidence accumulate without silently changing live scores. | header, §8.2, §9.2–§9.4, §10.8, §13.2, §15, §20; `docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-07-27 | **v3.17: P3-21 property contact retrieval (Yusuf-directed) — a leasing phone and contact URL, resolved by provenance precedence.** §16's PII rule is **narrowed, not dropped**: property-level *business* contact (a leasing office line, a generic contact/inquiry page) is now extracted and stored; an individual's details — a named agent, broker, or manager, their direct line or personal email, and any person's name or role — remain prohibited. The control is **structural rather than prompted**: neither the EXTRACT block nor `property_contacts` has a column for a person, so an identity has nowhere to land even when a page supplies one. Three rungs, best first: **official site → Google Places → listing page**. Deliberately **not the [§10.6](#106-conflict-resolution) ladder** — that machinery is built for numeric agreement, and several aggregators carrying identical digits usually means one call-tracking proxy line echoed through a feed, not corroboration; so precedence resolves and no vote is taken, contact facts are never scored or Gate-bearing, and a disagreement never raises `resolve_dispute`. Deliberately **no new fetch**: DISCOVER persists official links link-only, and rung 1 fires only when the official site is *already* a fetched Source, so the top rung costs nothing and never widens §10.6's decision-relevant escalation policy. Rungs 1 and 3 are therefore **one** optional EXTRACT block whose provenance is decided at persist time from the Source's `is_official`; rung 2 is two fields added to the Place Details mask ENRICH already calls once per Property — Google's Contact Data SKU, billed on top of the Basic + Atmosphere tiers that call already requests, so cheap but not free. **Email is deliberately deferred**: complexes publish contact *forms*, not addresses, and the yield would not justify the extraction surface. Terminal state is **giving up, not escalating** — no contact found renders no Contact row, and the reader falls back to the Sources card and `official_url`. | The app could tell you a Property was worth calling but not how to call it. Precedence beats voting because trust, not consensus, is what distinguishes a real leasing line from a syndicated tracking number; refusing the extra fetch keeps an unscored convenience field from buying itself a fetch budget that decision-relevant facts have to justify; and a structural PII control survives prompt drift in a way an instruction does not | §8.2, §10.6, §13.2, §16, §19, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §7 |

VISION facts below the selected threshold are unknown for scoring; admitted low-confidence VISION remains persisted/displayed and may affect points, but is always omitted from the pure engine's Gate-value map. This applies to exact Floor Plan and generalized gallery VISION. | 2026-07-28 | **v3.22: IMAGE_CLASSIFY degrades on a slightly-lossy batch instead of failing the run, and jobs gain a non-fatal `warnings` surface (Yusuf-directed).** v3.18 pinned a classifier that returns 29/30 hashes and kept exact-hash validation strict; the consequence is that a run loses every image classification, and with it kitchen quality, over one dropped record. The contract is now tolerant at its edge: at most `IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ANOMALY_TOLERANCE = 2` unanswered hashes and at most two surplus records (unknown hashes + repeats) per batch are absorbed; past either bound the stage fails closed exactly as before. Unanswered images persist as `classification.status = "missing"`, irreconcilable repeats as `"disputed"`; neither is cache-keyed, so the next run re-asks, and neither can feed a quality target. Surplus unknown hashes are dropped. Repeated records for one hash resolve by higher confidence; a tie resolves to the sharper reading of the same picture (`other` loses to any real scene; `living` loses to a specific interior room), and anything else — kitchen vs exterior, or a disagreement about `diagram`/`irrelevant` — is disputed rather than guessed. Every tolerated anomaly becomes a `jobs.warnings` entry on the task card. The **new warning channel is deliberately third**, beside `error` (terminal) and checkpoints (halting): it reports degradation without pausing, failing, or asking. The classifier bench (`manzil bench-classifier`) keeps the strict check — it is a *selector* signal about model behavior, not the pipeline's operating contract. | Losing a whole gallery's classification to one missing record is a worse outcome than classifying 29 of 30 images and saying so. The tolerance is small enough that a genuinely malformed response still fails closed, and every relaxation is recorded rather than silent: a skipped image reads as skipped, not as an image with nothing in it, and a contradicted image is never quietly resolved into taste evidence. | header, §8.2, §10.8, §13.2, §20; `docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |

Because a recording's key covers `(stage, model, prompt_version, content)`, this re-keys every committed seed replay fixture: the three synthetic dev-seed pages were re-recorded for VALIDATE/EXTRACT/VERIFY and the `llm-smoke` fixture refreshed, the superseded Haiku-keyed recordings retired, and the `.gitignore` allowlist repointed. No prompt version changed. **Not benched:** this is a pin decision, not a bench result — the Phase 0 bench set has not been re-run under the collapsed tier, and that re-run is owed at next convenience. | One workhorse model is simpler to reason about and cheaper than the Haiku half of the split, and the stages moving are the cheap structural ones (is this a listing, does this criterion match, compose a baseline) rather than the taste judgments. Keeping DISCOVER's exemption explicit prevents a future cleanup from quietly undoing an observed regression. | header, §11.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `AGENTS.md` |
| 2026-07-28 | **v3.25: Cost Breakdown and Fees consolidate again into one Cost & fees section, every line overrideable; P3-SC8 move-in cost ships (Yusuf-directed).** v3.24's split existed to separate read-only arithmetic from the correction surface. That premise no longer holds: rent, mandatory fees, and every move-in charge are now overrideable, so the split forced the same charge to appear in both sections. One section now carries two summary tiles and three ledgers (Monthly, Utilities, Move-in) in a single row grammar, with the included-utilities banner demoted to the Utilities group header (keeping its gray / `scoreHigh` / `scoreHighest` levels). Norm-case qualifiers become icons or typography — refundable, non-refundable and credited are colored icons; a not-counted charge dims and strikes through; a human's figure underlines itself; a pending edit is visibly provisional — leaving chips and color to the states that can mislead. Edits reveal on hover or focus; reverts never hide. `fee_checklist` gains tri-state `counted` / `required` / `refundable` and `credited_amount`; `base_rent` becomes a Floor-Plan-scoped Override key. P3-SC8 lands with it: a worker-local composer shared by SCORE and rescore, `move_in_components` persisted per plan and on the Listing, a `composed` `estimated_move_in_cost` Criterion, household basis multipliers, credited-portion arithmetic, separate refundable / non-refundable / unstated subtotals, and the strict rule that an unknown *required* component withholds the total rather than presenting a subtotal as a floor. | A section whose every line is editable does not need a read-only twin, and the duplication was the split's real cost. Encoding the normal case as icons and typography instead of chips is what makes "not counted" and "Incomplete" legible at a glance — the whole reason those states exist. Tri-state flags keep "nobody said" distinct from "no", which is the difference between an honest Incomplete and a quietly optimistic total. | header, §8.2, §9.5–§9.6, §13.2, §19–§20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-07-28 | **v3.24: Cost Breakdown and Fees split; independently revertible utility corrections.** The former Cost & fees card becomes two sections. Cost Breakdown owns tagged arithmetic, all-in monthly, and a read-only included-utilities banner colored gray / `scoreHigh` / `scoreHighest` for none / minor-only / any-major inclusions. Fees owns edits: electric, gas, water, sewer, cooling, heat, and trash each get an Override popover with an included checkbox and, except cooling, an optional monthly amount replacing extraction/baseline. `utility_overrides` is the append-only per-Listing ledger; both correction fields null reverts one utility without disturbing the others. Extracted combined water/sewer and valet-trash slots remain inputs but no longer render as duplicate generic fee rows. `cooling` joins extraction vocabulary but its amount remains inside electricity. | A single array Override cannot independently correct and revert utility facts, while placing Include/Exclude buttons in the summary banner mixes explanation with editing. The split gives the composed total one stable read surface and every correction one consistent fee-ledger surface; hiding duplicate water/trash rows removes presentation double-counting without changing composition. | §4.2, §5.1, §8.2, §9.5–§9.6, §13.2, §20 |
| 2026-07-29 | **v3.26: P3-SC6/SC7 Catalog and effective-value contracts fixed before implementation.** The six objective unit-presence Criteria are `fittings` with boolean Source claims, `confirmed | advertised_unconfirmed | none` effective values, `+0.25 | 0 | 0` defaults, and `available_sources_only + verified_positive_preferred`. `flooring_materials` is a mixed-scope controlled set of `carpet | hardwood | engineered_wood | laminate | vinyl | tile | concrete | other`; exact and explicit-all claims score through `contains_any`/`contains_all`, while select/unspecified material claims remain provenance-visible but score unknown for a concrete Floor Plan. Enabling both `flooring_materials` and `flooring_quality` produces a non-blocking double-weight warning. | The approved scoped plan named these Criteria but did not define the persisted flooring vocabulary or how generalized arrays interact with a scoreable set schema. Fixing both before code avoids inventing a string sentinel inside an array contract and preserves the rule that uncertain unit evidence cannot satisfy a Gate. | header, §8.2, §9.2–§9.3, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-07-29 | **v3.27: settings and account surfaces get one shell; the account cluster moves to the navbar foot; feedback and Drawer deep links added; notifications deferred as a defined task (Yusuf-directed).** Six rulings. (a) **One settings shell used twice** — a tab rail plus a bounded-card column, at `/h/:huntId/settings` (rail: **Hunt** · **Your profile**) and a new `/account` (rail: **Profile** · **Account** · Alerts when P3-22 lands), replacing today's single 520 px stack of eight sections with three independent save buttons. One save bar per panel, appearing only when the panel is dirty and naming what changed; single-field commits (rename, role change) stay inline. (b) **People stays under Hunt** — Members, email invites, and invitation links merge into one card rather than three sections or a third tab; a hunt of this size never has enough rows to justify the split. (c) **The per-hunt role card is named for its mechanism** — "Your role and permissions", stating the viewer's role in a sentence and splitting into what the role permits and what it withholds, shown to **every** role including Owner (whose withheld list reads as empty by design). It is the standing answer to "why can't I do that?" and the surface P2-12's disabled controls will hang from. (d) **The account cluster moves from the header to the bottom of the navbar** — avatar, display name, and **email address**, expanding in place (not a floating dropdown) to Profile · Account settings · Sign out; the header `UserMenu` is retained below `sm`, where the navbar is behind the Burger, and on the hunt-less routes (`/`, `/account`). The hunt name moves to the **center** of the header as a switcher. (e) **A Submit-feedback entry sits directly above that cluster**, opening a modal — category, description, and an auto-attached context strip (route incl. Drawer param, hunt, build, account) — writing to a new admin-only `feedback` table whose RLS grants authenticated `INSERT` with `user_id = auth.uid()` and **no** `SELECT` policy at all, not even for the author's own rows; reads are admin-only. (f) **Drawer state becomes URL state** — `?listing=…`, `&plan=…`, `&tab=…` as query params (not path segments, so filters, sort, and the compare set survive alongside), open pushing and in-Drawer tab changes replacing, a shared link to a since-deleted or unreadable Listing showing a plain gone-state and clearing the param, and access decided by RLS rather than by possession of the URL. **Notifications are deferred**, now as a specified unit rather than a one-line backlog bullet: §18's entry and new task **P3-22** cover delivery, the Alerts tab, per-hunt overrides, and the attention-indicator addendum (count badge when the app knows how many things need you, bare dot when it only knows that something does, in clay). The Alerts surfaces are drawn in the approved design and parked. | Settings had accreted into a scroll with no answer to "what is unsaved?", and the account surface was a card floating outside the app's own navigation. Splitting on hunt-versus-you is the only division a person actually holds in their head, and one shell used twice means the second surface costs layout, not invention. Moving the account cluster to the navbar foot buys room for the email address — the one fact that answers "which account am I in?" without a click — and expanding in place keeps it from covering what the reader was looking at. Feedback is insert-only because a feedback table read by its submitters is a support inbox nobody built; the context strip is what makes a two-line report actionable. Deep-linkable Drawers are the difference between "look at the Ellsworth one" and a link. Deferring notifications while specifying them keeps the preference UI honest — an alerts screen whose toggles deliver nothing is worse than no screen — and scoping the indicator with it prevents the same badge being invented twice. | §13.1–§13.2, §18–§20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` §7, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-07-29 | **v3.28: Floor-Plan-aware filters use an ephemeral Filter-selected Floor Plan; manual pins remain authoritative (Yusuf-directed).** Property/Unit-Group predicates still apply once, but score, rent, sqft, all-in, availability state/date, and Criterion-backed predicates now test each current Floor Plan. One plan must satisfy every active Floor-Plan predicate; sibling plans cannot combine favorable facts into a false Unit Group match. An unpinned group stays visible when any plan matches, and its highest-scoring matching plan temporarily becomes the visibly marked Display Floor Plan across Overview, Map, and their Drawer. This selection is derived view state, never written to `hunt_listings.pins`, and changing/clearing filters recomputes or clears it. A valid manual pin is never auto-switched: the pinned plan alone decides visibility; if it fails while another plan matches, the group remains hidden and contributes to a separate “N pinned Unit Groups may have another Floor Plan that meets these filters” count. Unknown values retain their pass-through behavior, and Compare remains manual-pin/best-score because filter selection is not Hunt opinion. | A Unit Group is a navigation bucket, not a promise that its sibling Floor Plans are interchangeable. Filtering only the ordinary Display Floor Plan hid valid choices; aggregating “any plan” independently per predicate would create a different, equally misleading optimistic union. A derived selection exposes the actual plan that earned the match without overwriting a person's explicit choice or adding persistence for temporary view state. | header, §3, §8.2, §9.4, §13.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-07-29 | **v3.29: typed scoped Criteria retain multiple value/applicability claims; malformed exact scope degrades safely (Yusuf-directed).** Laundry, parking, cooling, and the internal heating input now persist and reconcile one `claim_variant` per controlled value, so distinct positives may coexist at one target without overwriting each other. Their scoreable values become controlled sets with ordered singleton `contains_any` options; exact and explicit-all positives union, additional select/unspecified evidence remains visibly `advertised_unconfirmed`, and `none` is exclusive with specificity winning. Boolean Criteria keep scalar presence semantics but may still carry separate exact and generalized targets: an exact dishwasher/fireplace claim confirms only its named Floor Plan and leaves siblings Unknown unless a separate generalized claim exists. EXTRACT prompt v8 states that “in select townhomes” is `select_units`, not exact. Across every scoped Criterion, a model-emitted exact claim with no valid response-local ref is normalized downward to `select_units` only when its quote explicitly says select/some/certain/varies; otherwise it becomes `unit_scope_unspecified`. No normalization invents a ref, creates all-unit truth, or turns one plan's evidence into generalized evidence. Existing typed Rubric scalar options migrate in place to set membership, bump the Hunt Rubric version, and enqueue rescore. | The observed fireplace failure was structurally safe but operationally brittle: the validator correctly rejected `specific_floor_plans` without refs, while the model repeated the same scope error on retry. Parking exposed the complementary loss: carport for all units and garage for select units are both true, yet Criterion+target identity forced one to overwrite or conflict with the other. Variant identity preserves additive typed evidence; downward-only normalization keeps exact association fail-safe while allowing ingestion to finish; and per-plan UI labels prevent either change from manufacturing certainty. | header, §8.2, §9.2–§9.3, §10.2, §13.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/scoped-criteria-and-amenities.md` |
| 2026-07-29 | **v3.29 addendum: controlled-set scoped claims also retain independent lineage.** `flooring_materials` uses a canonical set-valued `claim_variant`; applicable exact/all variants union for scoring, while select/unspecified variants remain evidence-only. | The prompt-v8 bench produced simultaneous all-unit vinyl and unspecified hardwood/carpet claims. Collapsing them to one current row either discarded confirmed evidence or promoted uncertain evidence, so the same independent-lineage principle must cover an already-set-valued Criterion. | §8.2, §9.3, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.30: the Visit Checklist — a tour is a first-class, collaborative, local-first record, and a `VC` workstream parallel to Phase 3 (Yusuf-directed).** `docs/apartment-tour-checklist.md` becomes something you operate rather than print. **A Visit hangs off a Property** with optional Floor Plan pointers and **spans every unit walked on one occasion**, one Visit per tour, timestamped, optionally prefilled from an earlier Visit at the same Property; its state is derived from timestamps rather than stored. **Sharing is a property of each item, not of the checklist:** Facts (objective, shared), Checks (tri-state physical tests that promote into the defect log when failed), Questions (un-tickable without a typed answer), Impressions (keyed per member). Items carry a **Visit Item Scope** mirroring Fact Scope, so `property` answers are recorded once and `unit` answers once per Visit Unit, with the unit switcher present only on the latter and ambiguity defaulting to `property`; §21's scoring table splits accordingly into per-unit and whole-building halves. **Visit Units are human-typed labels, not inventory** — §18's individual-unit deferral stands unchanged. Writes are **local-first with conflicts surfaced, never auto-resolved**: each carries `prev_entry_id`, nothing is discarded, and a fork renders as both values reusing the dispute-checkpoint idiom. **Ending a tour is a state, not a seal** ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.36). A completed Visit can be **reopened** by any member, which clears `ended_at` and returns it to in-progress. The reason is the shape of the feature: a tour is recorded on a phone and read on a desktop, and the desktop is where the typo gets fixed and the half-heard answer finally gets typed — freezing the record at End would make the most reflective moment read-only. Reopening is an explicit action rather than silent editability of a completed Visit, so the derived-state model stays intact, every surface already understands the result, and Realtime already broadcasts it. Nothing is rewritten in the dark: entries are append-only and each is timestamped and attributed, so a later edit is visibly later. Cancellation still outranks — a cancelled Visit must be reinstated first — and `template_version` stays immutable, so reopening can never re-resolve a finished tour against a newer template.

A door's identity across Visits is its **human-typed label** within the Listing ([§20](#20-decision-log) v3.35) — there is no unit inventory to key on, which is precisely why [§18](#18-deferred-features-and-backlog)'s deferral survives this feature. A **cancelled** tour is excluded from the roll-up entirely: it is a tour that did not happen, not evidence with a caveat.

**Visits never feed scoring**; they appear beside it as a `Visit` column on Overview, rolled up one row per Unit Group through the Floor Plan, taking the latest Visit per unit then the best unit per group, unit-scope Impressions only, and no row at all for a described unit matching no advertised group. **Confirmed fees propose rather than write**, accepted through the ordinary `fee_checklist`/`overrides` paths and rejected without disturbing the Visit's own figure; proposal rows are the single sanctioned exception to the one-icon action slot. No solo mode; the template is fixed with per-Visit custom items visibly labelled; `template_version` freezes per Visit. Photos, Rubric-derived items, and hunt-wide custom items are deferred (§18). | Every part of this answers a question the checklist could not dodge. **Property-anchored, unit-spanning** because that is what a tour physically is — one agent, one lease conversation, several doors — and because per-unit Visits would trade a presentation problem for a cross-Visit ownership-and-merge problem, while a single-unit tour is a free degenerate case of the spanning model and a multi-unit tour is not expressible in the per-unit one. **Per-item sharing** because the number of bathrooms is a fact and how the bathroom feels is not; the same split then makes conflict behaviour fall out for free, since Impressions cannot collide and Questions append. **Scope declared, not inferred**, because the dangerous failure is not a missing answer but an answer filed against the wrong unit. **Labels not inventory** because a Floor Plan is a marketed layout and a Unit Group is derived and unstored, so there is no advertised unit list to pick from — every label was always going to be typed, which also removes any "advertised versus custom unit" distinction and collapses the pickers into one control. **Local-first** because losing twenty minutes of notes to a dead bar in a basement is the one failure that ends use of the feature, and **surfacing** rather than resolving conflicts because silently discarding one of two humans' observations is worse than asking. **No scoring feedback** because the engine is pure, deterministic, and domain-blind and tour ratings are none of those; adjacency is what makes a 7-on-paper/2.2-in-person listing legible. **Best-of within a group** matches the established Display Floor Plan rule; **latest-visit-per-unit before best-of** prevents a rosy first impression outranking a careful second look; **no minted Unit Group** because groups derive from a Property's Floor Plans and inventing one would contradict §3. **Proposals not writes** because a tour yields the best cost data in the system and still must not mutate a Listing behind the reader's back; the two-icon exception is bounded to that row because accept and reject are both primary there and neither may hide behind a hover. Finally, a **parallel `VC` workstream** rather than P3 renumbering, because this is a product capability with no pipeline dependency and no LLM surface, and folding it into Phase 3 would muddy that phase's theme and appear to gate its exit. | header, §3, §4.2, §8.2–§8.3, **§9.7 (new)**, §13.1–§13.3, §18, §19, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `.claude/plans/visit-checklist.md`, `docs/apartment-tour-checklist.md` |

Optional `fields` are refresh-class tokens; changed page bytes still trigger the full text Catalog. Pricing/listing-details refreshes check the submitted Source plus current contributing, Source-Policy-permitted, Syndication-Family-distinct Sources rather than an official-only page. Per-Listing/class success markers drive quiet stale indicators and advance only after successful projection. Owner or submitter may refresh one Listing; Owner/Curator may fan out a Hunt refresh, with RLS enforcing the boundary. | A range cannot produce a deterministic due time, official-only refresh would lower assurance below the evidence that currently contributes to truth, and literal field extraction would violate the full-Catalog contract. Durable success markers separate “last Source fetch” from “this Listing was checked at its selected assurance level,” while class-combined Jobs and hash gating retain the cost target. | header, §8.2, §14, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `.claude/plans/phase-3-agent-system.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.32: P3-11 keeps checkpoint completion and defers page screenshots.** Every checkpoint kind auto-resolves after 24 hours with its declared default (`confirm_value = yes`, `resolve_dedupe = keep_separate`, `resolve_dispute = Leave unknown`). The Job event and clay clock badge preserve provenance. Tasks and the Listing drawer show extracted value, evidence quote, and Source link instead of a page screenshot. Auto-resolution retains the pre-default RunState; a late answer creates a correction Job from that immutable Stage boundary and continues through SCORE without rewinding History. Full-page checkpoint screenshots move to §18; `screenshot_path` remains reserved. | Confirmations and bounded waiting solve a real workflow failure; full-page captures do not. They exist only for browser-tier Sources, cannot point to the disputed text without another coordinate system, and add capture, private Storage, signing, cleanup, and security lifecycle. Evidence already carries the precise quote and Source. A correction Job preserves persist-before-advance and History while still letting a human replace the default. | header, §5.1, §8.2, §10.10, §12–§13, §16, §18, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `.claude/plans/phase-3-agent-system.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.33: a Visit answer fork requires disagreement, and resolution is an ordinary answer (VC-6).** `visit_entry_conflicts` (`security_invoker`, derived) defines an open conflict as two or more rows claiming the same `prev_entry_id` **whose answers differ**, surfacing one option per distinct answer and staying open until some row descends from a branch. Resolution appends an entry whose `prev_entry_id` is the chosen branch — no endpoint, no table, no resolved flag. Queued writes persist through TanStack Query's paused-mutation storage with a visible pending count; a pending row is marked local and may never be claimed as a `prev_entry_id`. | Requiring disagreement is what the word conflict means, and it is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: two members reaching the same answer have nothing to decide, and prompting anyway would train people to dismiss the picker — the one surface that must never be dismissed reflexively. It also makes the view immune to a duplicate replay, which is reachable whenever a queued write flushes as the tab navigates and is then restored from storage and sent again; byte-identical branches are agreement whatever produced them, so that costs a redundant history row and nothing else. Letting a later ordinary answer settle a fork keeps the picker from nagging about a value its own author has already replaced. Marking optimistic rows matters because a `prev_entry_id` pointing at a row the server has never seen would either fail the foreign key or manufacture a fork against a row that never existed. | §8.2, §8.3, §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `.claude/plans/visit-checklist.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.34: accepting a Fee Proposal writes exactly what the drawer would (VC-7).** `visit_fee_proposals` carries the offer; accepting **delegates to the ordinary cost writers** rather than writing the destination table itself, lands as `manual` provenance, and reuses the Override permission (§4.2) — enforced by RLS through `private.can_write_listing_cost` and again by the delegated writer. Base-rent acceptance is **Floor-Plan scoped** to the unit that was walked, Property-scoped only when that unit matched no advertised plan; `all_in_monthly` stays Property-wide. Targets are restricted to real fee slots and cost Criteria, one live offer per destination, and rejection writes only the decision. | The whole promise is that an accepted figure is indistinguishable from one typed into the drawer — that is what keeps provenance markers, rescore enqueueing and the Member-owns-their-own-Listing rule working instead of being reimplemented and drifting. Scope is the sharp edge: §9.6 already says a quoted rent corrects the plan you were standing in, and a Visit usually knows which, so writing Property-scope would have made the proposal path quietly broader than the manual one. A restricted target vocabulary stops a typo creating a fee line no surface renders, and one-live-offer-per-destination stops two numbers competing for the same slot with no way to tell which is current. | §8.2, §8.3, §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `.claude/plans/visit-checklist.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.35: the Visit roll-up is a view, and a door is identified by its label (VC-8).** `visit_unit_group_scores` (`security_invoker`, derived) implements §9.7's four rules in one place: a door's score is the mean **across members** of each member's own unit-scope Impression ratings, the **latest** tour of a door replaces earlier ones, the **best** door represents its Unit Group, and a described unit matching no advertised group produces **no row**. A door's identity across Visits is its human-typed label within the Listing; a cancelled tour is excluded outright. The Overview gains a `Visit` column beside Fit, and the drawer a Visits section. | Averaging per member before averaging across them stops whoever filled in the most fields from quietly casting the loudest vote — the failure is invisible in the number, which is what makes it worth fixing in the definition rather than the UI. Latest-wins rather than best-of-all-time because a second showing is a **correction**, and best-of across time would let a rosy first impression outrank the careful second look. Label-keyed identity is forced by §18: there is no unit inventory to key on, and inventing one to make the roll-up tidier would undo the deferral this whole feature was designed around. A cancelled tour is excluded rather than discounted because it is a tour that did not happen. The units that produce no row stay visible in the drawer, so the Overview's silence is a display rule rather than data loss. | §8.3, §9.7, §13.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `.claude/plans/visit-checklist.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.36: a completed Visit can be reopened, by any member (Yusuf-directed).** `PATCH /v1/visits/{id}` gains a `reopen` action that clears `ended_at` and returns the Visit to in-progress. It sits with start/end in [§4.2](#42-roles-and-permissions) rather than with cancel; a cancelled Visit must be reinstated first, and `template_version` remains immutable. | Ending was a seal, and it should not have been: the feature is explicitly designed to be recorded on a phone and read on a desktop, so locking the record at End made the most reflective moment — the write-up, when you remember the thing from the car — read-only, with cancel-then-reinstate as the only escape, which is both untrue and lossy in meaning. An explicit action rather than making completed Visits quietly editable, because that would add a third editability rule for the trigger, the API and the UI to keep in step, and because a state change is visible to collaborators through Realtime while a silent unlock is not. It costs nothing in traceability: entries are append-only, timestamped and attributed, so the original answer survives and a later edit reads as later. Any member, because reopening is **continuing the work**, not overriding somebody's judgement — the creator-or-Owner narrowing exists for cancel, which is a decision about whether the tour counts at all. | §4.2, §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-07-30 | **v3.35: P3-10 custom Criteria ship page-text and Maps acquisition, with author-selected scope/schema and immediate backfill.** An Owner names/describes a Criterion, receives a cheap routing suggestion, confirms or corrects it, selects Property/Floor Plan scope and boolean/number/controlled-enum truth, then defines ordinary ordered Rubric options. Text is zero-tool and may be Property/Floor Plan scoped; Maps is the second and last workflow tool loop and is Property-only. New or semantically replaced definitions mint opaque keys and immediately run `CUSTOM_MATCH → SCORE` over active Listings using cached evidence; label/options keep the key and remain a free re-score. The classifier may identify vision/web-search needs, but those routes cannot be confirmed and move to §18. | Scope and value shape cannot be inferred safely at ingestion, while asking once at authoring keeps runtime dispatch deterministic. Replacement keys prevent old hunt-scoped Extractions from changing meaning after an edit. Cached-evidence backfill makes a newly authored Criterion useful immediately without conflating Rubric editing with a Source refetch. Arbitrary VISION cannot borrow the kitchen reference set, and a third workflow search loop would violate §10.2/§16, so naming those routes without pretending to support them is the honest v1 boundary. | header, §9.2, §10.9, §18, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `.claude/plans/phase-3-agent-system.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.37: reopening a Visit is its own timestamp, not the absence of an end (VC-9).** `visits.reopened_at` holds only the **most recent** reopen; `ended_at` is never cleared. State stays derived and gains one comparison: a Visit is in progress when `reopened_at` is later than `ended_at`, and ending again stamps a new, later `ended_at` that lands back on completed without disturbing the reopen stamp. Constraints refuse a reopen with no end and a reopen before the start; a cancelled Visit may legitimately carry one. | v3.36 gave `reopen` the right *state* by the wrong *means*: it nulled `ended_at`, so the record of when a real afternoon finished was destroyed in order to note that somebody opened their laptop on Friday. Two different events were sharing one column, and the one with more meaning lost — a tour's duration is evidence about the agent and the property, and it was silently unrecoverable. Only the latest reopen is kept because the useful question afterwards is whether the record has been revisited since it closed; a full reopen log would duplicate what `visit_entries` already carries, since every actual edit is append-only, timestamped and attributed. No constraint forbids a cancelled-and-reopened row: cancellation already outranks everything in the derivation, and forbidding the pair would turn a tidiness preference into a refused cancel. | §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.38: defect severity is four named levels, not five stars (VC-10).** `visit_defects.severity` becomes the enum `noted` / `minor` / `major` / `dealbreaker`, carrying the sage → ochre → clay → brick band of the existing status ramp plus a word and a distinct glyph each. Null still means **unrated**, which a promoted defect starts as. Existing rows migrate 1–2 → `noted`/`minor`, 3–4 → `major`, 5 → `dealbreaker`. | A 1–5 scale with no vocabulary attached is two people writing different scales into one column: nothing anywhere said what a 3 was, so the number was not comparable between the members whose disagreement the feature exists to surface. Stars made it worse than merely vague — they read as *quality* everywhere else in the product, so on a defect more stars looked like better news. Four rather than three because the ramp is then evenly spaced across the decision it supports: two levels you would accept and two you would negotiate over, instead of one bucket doing the work of "cosmetic" and "annoying" at once. A word, a hue **and** a glyph because colour reinforces and never carries (`UI_DESIGN` §4), and this gets read one-handed in a stranger's kitchen. Unrated stays a real state rather than defaulting to the mildest level: a defect promoted from a failed Check has a judgement nobody made yet, and inventing one would be the same category error the stars made. | §8.2, §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.39: Visit Checklist template v2 — the actual-unit question gains `similar` (VC-11).** `TEMPLATE_VERSION` 2, seeded by its own migration; `sweep_actual_unit` now offers `actual` / `model` / `similar`. v1 rows are untouched and keep serving every Visit created against them. | The common answer on a real tour was not on offer: the agent walks you through the same plan two floors down, which is neither the unit you would rent nor a staged model. With two options that tour was recorded as one of two wrong things — on a **critical** question, which is exactly the set the Quick tier and the "N critical" counter are built from, so the error propagated into the numbers rather than sitting quietly in one field. A new version rather than an edit to the v1 rows because `template_version` is frozen into every Visit at creation: a finished Visit must keep asking what it asked, and its completion percentage must keep meaning what it meant. | §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.40: the Visit surfaces get a desktop, a navigation model, and one status-colour map (VC-12…VC-17, Yusuf-directed).** Twenty-one sections roll up into five **phases** (Before · Walk it · Ask · Money · Wrap up) with a full section picker behind the section header and previous/next through template order; the horizontal chip strip is removed. Desktop renders three panes — section rail, active section, and an insight column carrying visit meta, the per-member Impression compare table with computed spread, and the unanswered criticals — collapsing to the phone layout by *rendering* fewer panes, not by hiding them. Every status colour resolves through one map with `light` variants only. A finished tour renders as a **record** (full colour, non-interactive) and a cancelled one as **void** (genuinely disabled). Rows take notes via the existing `visit_entries.note`. | Four causes, not twelve bugs. **The phone layout was the only layout**: one column at every width made a desktop a stretched phone and left the per-member comparison — the one thing a phone physically cannot lay side by side, and the reason anyone opens a Visit three days later — with nowhere to live. **Colour bypassed the theme**: `filled` variants let `autoContrast` pick text colour per shade, and sage and dusty brick sit close enough to the flip point that the same control inverted between schemes, while `color="ochre"` named a colour `theme.ts` does not define and rendered nothing at all. One map makes that checkable in one place instead of scattered. **A record and a void tour rendered identically**, which said the wrong thing about both — a completed tour is worth reading in full colour and a cancelled one is a form nobody will fill in. And the checklist asks 248 questions with **nowhere to put the 249th thought**: a tri-state cannot say why a check failed, which is the sentence the whole visit turns on later. Notes reuse the column VC-3 already shipped, so they inherit append-only history, attribution and the offline queue rather than adding a table. | §9.7, §13.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `.claude/plans/visit-checklist.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.37: tier-3 fetching is priced, and job cost is split by what bought it (AD-C).** The run tally gains a second, non-LLM channel: `Tier3Fetcher` bills one managed-unblocker request per response received (never per transport failure) at `TIER3_PRICES_USD` — $0.0015 for Bright Data, whose free plan is **5,000 credits a month** — and the runner folds it onto `jobs.cost_actual_usd` alongside token spend. The tally moves out of `llm/` into `worker/costs.py` so fetching can bill without importing the model seam. A new `job_stage_costs` table records spend **per (job, stage)**, split into the two channels at `numeric(12,6)`, replaced rather than accumulated when a stage re-runs; `tier3_credit_usage` (`security_invoker`) counts requests per provider per calendar month. Spend from **failed stage attempts** is now retained instead of discarded on retry. | `cost_actual_usd` claimed to be the bill and was only the model half: a Hunt fetching through hostile domains under-reported itself, and the free plan's monthly allowance drained with no counter anywhere, so exhausting it would have dropped tier 3 off the ladder mid-run as an unexplained failure. Per stage rather than per call because per-call telemetry is Langfuse's job and duplicating it buys nothing; stage granularity still answers the only question an operator asks, which is which stage got expensive. Six decimals because a single $0.0015 request truncates to zero at four. Billing on response rather than on success because the provider ran and charged for a request the target blocked — over-counting a provider-side rejection is the safe direction for a budget meter, under-counting is not. An unpriced provider records calls at $0 rather than a guess, so the gap reads as a missing price instead of free traffic. Keeping failed-attempt spend because a stage that burned three credits before succeeding cost three credits. | §8.2, §10.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.38: the four places history was being destroyed, closed in the domain rather than in an audit table (AD-G).** `fee_checklist` was upserted in place, so a fee's previous amount and author died on every edit — and fees feed all-in cost, so that is a correctness gap, not a missing feature. `hunt_members.role`, and `hunt_listings.status`/`source_policy`/`pins`, were overwrite-only. Deleting anything left no trace it had existed. Four append-only companions (`fee_checklist_history`, `hunt_member_history`, `hunt_listing_history`, `deletion_tombstones`) are filled by **triggers**, and a general-purpose all-actions audit log is ruled out. Visibility splits: **data provenance is shared with members, governance history belongs to the Owner** (and, from AD-1, a Site Admin). | A generic audit log would have been a second copy of history Manzil already keeps correctly — `overrides` is append-only with an author, `visit_entries` never overwrite, extraction facts carry lineage — and once the copy disagrees with the domain table you must decide which to believe, with the audit row always the weaker witness because it records the *shape* of a write rather than its meaning. Triggers rather than writers because the API, the worker and a direct PostgREST call all reach these rows and only a trigger sees all three; it also cannot be forgotten by a future writer. Tombstones deliberately carry **no foreign keys**: an FK cascades the tombstone away with the very row it commemorates, which is the one thing it must survive. `via_cascade` so one Hunt deletion and its 200 Listings collapse to a single line for a reader. No-op suppression because the fee writer touches `updated_at` on every save and a history of "unchanged" would bury the edits that matter. A null actor (the worker's pool carries no JWT) reads as "the system did this", which is true and better than attributing a pipeline write to a person. | §4.2, §8.2, §8.3, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.39: Site Admin is an account-level role orthogonal to Hunt membership, and the ghost view is conditional (AD-0).** Access is split: a service-role admin router owns the panel and every admin **write**, while a SELECT-only `private.is_site_admin()` predicate on Hunt-scoped read policies lets the existing Hunt screens serve an admin unmodified. `site_admins` holds the flag — never a column on `user_profiles` — and its **first row is immutable**. A Site Admin viewing a Hunt they do not belong to is absent from the member list and from Presence, writes as `Admin [Name]`, and is **read-only on Visits**; an admin who *is* a member of that Hunt is an ordinary member there. Impersonating a specific user, enforcing spend caps, and a general audit log over non-admin actions are all deferred (§18). | RLS is the security boundary, so "an admin sees everything" had to be true in Postgres rather than in React — but adding a bypass predicate to every policy in the schema would make each future migration a security decision and put a cross-tenant leak one typo away. Confining writes to one auditable router while letting only *reads* widen keeps the blast radius where it can be reasoned about. `site_admins` rather than a boolean on `user_profiles` because that table is readable by anyone sharing a Hunt, which would publish the admin roster to every collaborator. The immutable first row, not the self-revocation guard, is what actually makes lockout impossible. Visits are read-only because an Entry feeds a per-member average and a ghost answering a tour they did not attend corrupts a number nobody would think to distrust. The conditional ghost matters because the alternative — an admin silently vanishing from a Hunt they genuinely belong to — would be a lie told to their own collaborators. | §3, §4.2, §8.2, §8.3, §18, §19, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.40: the hunt activity feed is a view with its own gate, and it is the Owner's (AD-F).** `hunt_activity` unions the append-only domain tables — fee history, Overrides, membership, curation, tombstones, Visits, defects and Job outcomes — into one hunt-scoped timeline with **no backing table and no new write path**. Visible to the Hunt **Owner** and to Site Admins; curators and members see nothing. Individual Visit Entries are deliberately excluded. Access lives in the view's own `WHERE`, not in `security_invoker`. | A materialized activity table would be a second copy of history that can disagree with the domain tables AD-G just made authoritative — the identical objection that ruled out a general audit log. Reading live means the feed cannot drift, and it costs nothing to keep correct. `security_invoker` was the obvious choice and is wrong here: a Site Admin is a member of nothing, so the base tables' member-scoped policies would hand them an empty feed — the exact opposite of the requirement. Putting the gate in the view makes it one auditable predicate in one place, at the price that weakening that line is a cross-tenant leak, which is why it is commented as load-bearing. Owner-only rather than member-visible because the feed's contents are governance and money, and a shared timeline of who-did-what turns a collaboration tool into a monitoring surface between people who are house-hunting together. Visit Entries are excluded for the same reason at closer range: §9.7 averages per member precisely so nobody's volume dominates, and replaying each person's answers would re-personalize what that rule exists to flatten — the feed reports that a tour happened and what red flags came out of it. | §4.2, §8.3, §9.7, §19, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.41: the panel is its own shell, and unbuilt tabs are shown disabled rather than hidden (AD-2).** `/admin/*` lives outside the `/h/:huntId` tree with its own rail; the entry point is a `NavbarFoot` item directly above *Send feedback*, rendered only for Site Admins. `feedback` gains a `triage` column (new/seen/actioned/wont_fix) and keeps its zero-SELECT-policy posture — reads stay with the service-role router, so a submitter still cannot read their own report. Tabs owned by later slices render **disabled and labelled with the slice**. | Its own shell because nothing in the panel is Hunt-scoped and the hunt navbar's items would every one of them be dead. The nav placement is not arbitrary: the panel is the reader of the inbox that button writes to, so they belong in the same meta-not-Hunt cluster. Rendering the entry only for admins is UX, never enforcement — the API refuses everyone else regardless, and stating that plainly matters because the reverse assumption is how frontend gating turns into a security control. A column rather than a `feedback_triage` table because there is exactly one state per report; a side table would buy a history nobody asked for at the cost of a join on every list. Disabled-and-labelled tabs because the panel's shape is part of what an operator needs to know, and a link that 404s is a worse answer than one that says which slice will build it. | §4.2, §13.1, §19, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/admin-dashboard.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.42: deleting a person who owns a Hunt is refused, and suspension is the reversible action (AD-3).** `DELETE /v1/admin/people/{id}` 409s while the account owns any Hunt, naming them; the detail response carries the blocking Hunts so the panel can disable Delete and offer the transfer inline. Suspension is an auth-level ban rather than a column of ours. Promoting someone to Owner routes through `transfer_hunt_ownership`. Removing an Owner from their own Hunt is likewise refused. | `hunts.owner_id` carries no cascade, but `hunt_members`, `hunt_listings`, `jobs`, `visits` and every fact beneath them do — so deleting one account would silently destroy a Hunt and its whole history, with no undo and no tombstone worth reading. Refusing is the only safe default, and naming the Hunts in the refusal is what turns it from an obstacle into an instruction. Surfacing the same list on the detail response matters because a control that looks available and then fails teaches an operator to distrust the panel. Suspension as a ban rather than a `suspended` column because two sources of truth for whether an account can sign in is exactly the pair that drifts, and only one of them is what Supabase actually enforces. Ownership through the RPC because a Hunt has exactly one Owner and a direct role write would leave the previous one in place — a bug whose symptom is two Owners and no error. | §4.2, §8.2, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/admin-dashboard.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.43: the admin read predicate is SELECT-only, and the ghost view is derived rather than declared (AD-4).** `private.can_read_hunt()` / `can_read_hunt_governance()` are added to 31 Hunt-scoped **SELECT** policies and to nothing else; `private.member_role()` is unchanged. The migration self-checks both halves and refuses to apply if a SELECT policy lacks the predicate or **any** write policy admits a Site Admin. In the client, ghost mode is computed as *is a Site Admin AND is not a member of this Hunt* — there is no ghost route and no query parameter — and it is `undefined` until membership is known, with every consumer treating not-yet-known as not-a-member. | Widening reads lets the existing Hunt screens serve an admin unmodified, which is worth far more than it costs: the alternative was a parallel read implementation that would drift. Widening *writes* would have quietly destroyed the audit story, because a PostgREST write never passes through the router that records it — hence the self-check, which makes that mistake fail the migration rather than fail review. Leaving `member_role()` alone is what keeps the two §4.2 promises mechanical rather than aspirational: an admin cannot appear in the member list, and cannot satisfy a write policy, because nothing about them is a membership. Deriving the ghost from non-membership rather than a URL flag matters most in the direction people forget — a flag that is *absent* when it should be present puts an admin into a live tour as a phantom body, and Presence is broadcast to everyone on it before anyone notices. Defaulting to observe-until-proven-member is the same reasoning applied to the loading state. | §4.2, §8.3, §9.7, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/admin-dashboard.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.44: spend-by-model is derived from the current pin and labelled as such; System is read-only; stale locks re-queue (AD-5).** The Costs surface reads `job_stage_costs` for by-stage and by-Hunt spend and `tier3_credit_usage` for the monthly credit meter; the by-model view folds stage spend under each stage's **present** pin and carries `grouped_by_current_pin`. The System surface reports queue health, model pins and credential **presence**, and edits nothing. Releasing a stale lock returns the Job to `queued`. | AD-C deliberately records cost per stage rather than per call, so a truthful per-model history is not available and inventing one would be worse than omitting it — labelling the approximation keeps the useful question answerable (*what is Gemini costing us*) without letting a reader mistake it for what actually ran. System edits nothing because a pin change is a code change plus a §20 entry: a settings screen that can silently re-pin a stage is precisely how a bill goes wrong quietly, and the same reasoning makes credentials presence-only. A stale lock re-queues rather than fails because the evidence is about the worker, not the work — failing it would discard a Job for its process's crash and lose the spend already banked against it. | §15, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/admin-dashboard.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.45: cap-saturated IMAGE_FETCH counts as freshness-complete (Yusuf-directed).** When `photo_count` reaches `MAX_STORED_IMAGES`, failed downloads on remaining candidates no longer block the `images` refresh marker or hourly TTL retries. Syndication pages expose more URLs than storage can hold; dead extras (404s, bad srcset fragments) must not loop a listing that already has a full gallery. Underfilled partials with failures stay due and retry per 2.0.104. RunState splits **`image_fetch_completed`** (freshness + photo retirement; cap-saturated OK) from **`image_fetch_strict_complete`** (diagram unlink and vision tombstones; any failed download blocks). | Treating every failed extra URL as incomplete made Filmore Place retry hourly despite 30 stored photos. Cap saturation is the intentional stop rule — we cannot store more anyway — while P3-SC5 still forbids retiring diagram associations on a pass that failed to re-download them. | §8.2, §10.8, §14, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-01 | **v3.41: the 97 MB unsigned-int8 CLIP ONNX export is the deployable IMAGE_CLASSIFY shadow, while ONNX-only authority remains a separate gated decision.** When its artifact directory is explicitly configured, the worker verifies the pinned artifact and runs it in a short-lived, single-thread CPU subprocess over one thumbnail at a time. Scene/kitchen/diagram scores persist under the independently versioned `classification_shadow`; they cannot alter the incumbent classification, image kind, selectors, VISION, Extractions, or SCORE, and failure is log-only. Public held-out evidence cleared kitchen identity and Floor Plan rejection and the hard-limit Render simulation fit; neither establishes the missing production contract — view assessability, framing, irrelevant real-gallery assets, confidence/abstention, or target-set safety. Therefore no artifact-path setting may implicitly promote it. Promotion requires an explicit backend setting, a canonical result contract, a representative live-gallery target-set audit, target-host operational evidence, cache/reclassification and rollback mechanics, and a new §20 ruling. | The LLM is unnecessary for the part CLIP already proves, and a 97 MB local artifact removes recurrent classifier cost and generated batch-shape failures. But kitchen identity is not permission to rate a kitchen: treating every match as an assessable full-room view would silently broaden quality evidence, while honestly filling missing fields as unknown would select nothing. Shadowing earns the missing evidence without risking pipeline truth. Separating artifact presence from backend authority also prevents a deployment-path typo from becoming a product decision. | header, §10.8, §15, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/onnx-image-classification.md`, `docs/image-classification-ml-analysis.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **Live-acceptance evidence recorded:** a real Listing completed an ingest through tiers 1–3, a real Detroit-metro Listing completed ENRICH, a custom Criterion scored on the real Hunt, and the mobile compare flow passed exploratory usability testing. This clears P3-8's live ENRICH and P3-13's mobile acceptance. It does **not** assert the narrower unrecorded conditions: P3-14 still needs confirmation for each of the five census-named hostile domains, and P3-10 still needs the record to show whether both its Maps and text acquisition routes were exercised. | These observations provide the operational evidence that fixture and build checks cannot, while preserving the distinction between an exercised end-to-end path and per-provider/per-route coverage. | §19; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/outstanding-bench-runbook.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **P3-14 and P3-10 live acceptance are complete; pre-production account provisioning is required.** All five census-named Tier-3 domains are confirmed. P3-10 is accepted from its live test despite no programmatic test. Before deployment, account creation becomes Site-Admin-only; public registration is disabled; magic links/OTPs may authenticate an existing account but cannot create one. Password setup/recovery is the account-enrollment path, and every provision action is covered by the existing Admin Audit Log. | Tier-3 and custom-Criterion acceptance are operational claims, so the Owner's live confirmation is sufficient. Public account creation is contrary to Manzil's small-circle posture and creates an unnecessary production surface. Preserving existing-account magic-link login retains a low-friction sign-in option without making it an account-provisioning bypass. | §4.1–§4.2, §19–§20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.46: `year_built` and `is_renovated` join the catalog; Maps custom Criteria gain optional route modifiers.** `year_built` is Property-scoped (integer, year-range rubric defaults); `is_renovated` is Floor Plan–scoped via the P3-SC6 presence contract. Both reverse the prior deliberate exclusion of building-age fields with Owner-requested rationale and non-blocking double-weight warnings when `kitchen_quality` is also enabled. `CustomCriterionDef` adds optional `route_modifiers` (`avoid_highways`, `avoid_tolls`, `avoid_ferries`); the custom Maps agent's `commute_time` tool honors them via the Directions API when any flag is set. Dirt/gravel roads remain unsupported (§18). | Building age and explicit renovation language are scoreable page-text signals distinct from VISION kitchen quality when labeled honestly. Route modifiers make commute-style custom Criteria express real routing preferences Google supports without pretending unsupported constraints are enforced. | §8.2, §9.2, §10.9, §20; `shared/catalog.py`, `maps.py` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.47: EXTRACT is two fixed forced-schema slices on Google AI Studio.** In the 2026-08-02 probes, the combined 43-field function declaration returned `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, while its two deterministic 21- and 22-field slices were accepted. Ordinary claims run first; all applicability-bearing claims run with Floor Plans and the non-catalog blocks second; their union is then validated by the original complete Pydantic schema before facts reach `RunState`. | This was an observed result for that generated request, model, and adapter—not a general Google field limit. It retained forced tool use, the Catalog as the single schema source, zero extraction tools, and full validation while making the Owner-pinned Gemini workhorse operational. Native structured output was tested and rejected the same full schema. The second call added bounded spend, but was materially cheaper and safer than re-pinning EXTRACT without a bench. | §10.2, §11.2, §15, §20; `worker/stages/extract.py` |
| 2026-08-02 | **v3.48: `unit_types` is derived from Floor Plans, restoring one-call EXTRACT.** The redundant top-level `unit_types` claim is removed from the forced schema; `floor_plans[].unit_types` remains the sole extraction input and the Catalog Criterion continues to score from that Floor Plan value. Pre-v3.48 recordings carrying the redundant claim remain replayable. The resulting 42-field request was accepted by Google AI Studio. | A Property-wide type and a Floor Plan type remain distinct truths, but asking for the Floor Plan type twice was unnecessary and risked conflicting claims. The 42-accepted / 43-rejected boundary is empirical for this exact generated schema, `google/gemini-3-flash-preview`, and OpenRouter forced-tool route; it is not a provider-wide cap and must be revalidated after schema, model, or adapter changes. This removes no user-visible data and avoids the extra call introduced by v3.47. | §8.2, §9.4, §10.2, §20; `worker/stages/schema_gen.py`, `worker/stages/extract.py` |
| 2026-08-03 | **v3.49: the Hunt switcher remains membership-scoped for Site Admins and gains an admin-only panel card.** `/` lists only Hunts where the signed-in account has a `hunt_members` row, even though Site Admin SELECT policies expose every Hunt for Ghost View. A filled primary-colour **Admin panel** card sits above those Hunt cards only for a confirmed Site Admin and links to `/admin`; ordinary members never see it. The existing in-Hunt `NavbarFoot` entry remains. | RLS widening exists so an admin can follow an explicit Ghost View link, not so every Hunt silently becomes theirs in a surface titled “Your hunts.” The membership predicate therefore belongs in the switcher query itself. The separate panel card makes the account-level destination visible before an admin enters a Hunt, while its frontend identity check remains UX rather than enforcement. | §13.1, §20; `frontend/UI_DESIGN.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md` |
| 2026-08-03 | **v3.50: failed refreshes gain durable per-class exponential backoff; synthetic seed Sources are scheduler-ineligible.** A due class that most recently failed waits 1/2/4/8/16/24 hours according to the number of failures since its last successful marker, capped at 24 hours; manual retries remain available. Sources in the committed `*.seed.example` fixture namespace never enter TTL scheduling. | Stage-local retries handle transient failures within one Job, but a permanently unreachable Source otherwise created a fresh four-attempt Job every five-minute tick. Job history already provides durable retry state. The synthetic pages are intentionally resolvable only through `FixtureFetcher`, so asking the production fetch ladder to refresh them is categorically invalid rather than a network outage. | §14, §20; `shared/config.py`, `worker/queue.py`, `scripts/dev_seed.py` |
| 2026-08-03 | **v3.51: gated score breakdown shows matched option and informational criteria (Owner-requested).** When a gate fires, `GateFiring` gains gate-pass `value` and `matched`; `criteria` is populated with point-scoring deltas (informational — `total` remains the gate cap). The detail drawer names which option triggered the gate and still renders the full breakdown. Gate-pass fields prevent misleading alert copy when point-values and gate-values diverge (e.g. low-confidence VISION). | A gate-only alert with empty criteria hid the rubric context users need to understand a listing; generic "matched a dealbreaker" copy could not name the option; criteria lookup would lie when gate-values excluded a fact still scored for points. | §9.3, §20; `shared/scoring/engine.py`, `frontend/CriterionBreakdown.tsx` |
| 2026-08-03 | **v3.52: Owner promotes the pinned ONNX artifact to exclusive IMAGE_CLASSIFY authority and disables the LLM classifier.** The canonical `classification` is the versioned ONNX scene/kitchen/diagram score record; old shadow rows promote on refresh and replaced LLM records remain audit-only. No workflow path calls or falls back to the retained LLM classifier code. Missing/corrupt configuration and inference/hash-contract failures fail the Stage. The kitchen selector deliberately narrows its contract: exclude deterministic/ONNX diagrams and deterministic `other`, rank the remaining photos by `kitchen_score`, take the top three regardless of threshold, and let anchored quality VISION reject `not_visible`. Assessability, framing, irrelevant-asset classification, confidence/abstention, near-duplicate suppression, and plan-coverage priority are not fabricated; their absence and the uncompleted representative-gallery target-set audit remain explicit accepted debt. | ONNX already supplies the only routing signal the Owner wants to pay for, while the anchored VISION call can judge whether a high-probability target is actually rateable. Ranking a real score is deterministic and more informative than random selection. Recording the narrowed contract prevents removal of an LLM field from silently becoming an invented positive, and retaining legacy code/data preserves rollback and audit without paying for dual inference. | header, §10.8, §15, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/onnx-image-classification.md`, `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md` |
| 2026-08-08 | **v3.61: a demo ships a *slate* of Replay Captures, and a visit gets a bounded, visible allowance of them.** A Demo Account no longer replays one recording every time. The build globs `capture*.json` and plays them in sorted filename order — one per "submission" — and a demo-only **Run a demo ingest** button starts the next one without needing a URL to paste. A badge next to it always states how many runs are left; when the slate is spent, both the button and **Add listing** are disabled and the badge says a reload starts the set over. The allowance lives in module memory, not `sessionStorage`. Nothing else about a Replay Capture changes: each is still a recording of a real ingest, each is still disclosed before the visitor confirms — the disclosure now also names which recording of how many — and each captured Listing is still staged `archived` by `scripts/seed_demo_hunt.py` so it appears only when "submitted". | One recording answers "what does the pipeline do" once, and a visitor who wants to see it again gets the same Property with the same numbers, which reads as a canned animation rather than as a working system. Several genuinely different Properties, each really ingested, answer it better and cost nothing extra at demo time — the Owner already pays for the ingests either way. The allowance exists because the replay is the one demo surface with an end: it publishes a staged Listing, and once every staged Listing is on the Overview there is nothing left to publish, so an unbounded button would silently do nothing. Saying "0 of 5 left, reload to start over" is the honest form of that, and it is only honest because the counter is reload-scoped: §16 promises a reload puts everything back, and an allowance persisted in `sessionStorage` would have broken that promise in the one place a visitor would notice. It is emphatically not a security control — it budgets an animation that makes no network call; what keeps a demo from spending money is the database refusing its writes. The demo button is there because a first-time visitor has no listing URL to hand, and the pipeline is the thing worth showing them. | §3, §13, §16, §20; `IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `frontend/src/features/demo/replay/`, `scripts/seed_demo_hunt.py` |
| 2026-08-08 | **v3.64: Prefer known base rent for an ordinary Display Floor Plan.** When selecting an unpinned, unfiltered Unit Group representative, exclude Floor Plans without a known base-rent range whenever one or more scored sibling plans has one, then select the highest score among the remaining candidates. Pins and Filter-selected Floor Plans remain authoritative; when every scored plan lacks rent, use the highest score normally. The exact-score known-all-in tie-break remains inside the eligible set. | A Floor Plan with unknown base rent cannot provide a useful all-in comparison, even if unrelated Criterion points make it score higher. Preferring a real market offer makes the Overview actionable without calling a lower score or a sibling cost the truth; only a complete absence of rent data warrants the old score-only fallback. | §3, §9.4, §20; `frontend/src/features/listings/`, `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-08 | **v3.63: Overview all-in tie-break and heating-warning declutter.** An unpriced duplicate Floor Plan may not win a Display Floor Plan tie over an otherwise equal-scored sibling whose own all-in composition is complete; manual pins, filter-selected plans, strictly higher scores, and the per-plan truth boundary remain unchanged. `heat_unknown` remains in the persisted composition and drawer ledger, but is removed from the Overview's collapsed warning triangle because the displayed total already uses the costlier gas/electric variant. | A known price should not disappear merely because Source order chose a duplicate plan with no rent, while substituting a lower-scoring sibling or borrowing its total would be a false statement. Unknown heating is not a cost omission: conservative calculation handles it, so surfacing it on nearly every table row trains readers to ignore the warning channel; the drawer retains the explanation. | §9.4, §9.5, §13.2, §20; `frontend/src/features/listings/`, `IMPLEMENTATION.md` |
| 2026-08-08 | **v3.62: Kitchen VISION rationales are bounded and visible as per-image provenance.** The model is told to keep each rationale below 320 characters; the validated contract permits 360 and trims only surplus surrounding/model text so an otherwise valid assessment does not fail the VISION Stage. The Drawer gallery projects the persisted per-image kitchen assessment and exposes its rating, confidence, and rationale on demand. The strict assessment contract remains: target hashes, visibility, rating, and confidence are never repaired or inferred; EXTRACT's correction retry now receives only the implicated invalid structured fragment, so repeated scoped values can be corrected without echoing a page-sized prior tool call. | A model occasionally overruns an arbitrary prose limit, and failing a full image assessment for that harmless overflow discards useful evidence.
